可怜的厨子有一天被罚了款，起因是他的节约，随后难友们联合行动了一场。那天来了一位衣着无可挑剔的绅士——在 duly 提醒魔王他将视察魔王的家政之后——我想是奥恩省的一位公职人员。犹大（时任侍寝总管〔chef de chambre〕），在除两三个被恐惧变成兔子或鼹鼠的人之外、全体难友独一无二的一致义愤支持下，一早把那只桶（那天经一致同意我们谁也没碰过）提下楼，穿过过道，上了一层楼——正撞见所长、监守和漂亮陌生人正友好而愉快地交谈。犹大放下桶；鞠躬；以拉费尔泰马塞全体男性的发言人身份，请求对咖啡的质量进行检验。「我们谁也不喝了，恕我冒昧，先生们。」他声称他是这么说的。接下来发生的事极为有趣。小个子扫地人，事件的目击者，向我描述如下：
「所长咆哮：『什么？（***Comment?***）』他气得吓人。『是，先生（*Oui, Monsieur*）。』侍寝总管谦卑地说——『为什么？（*Pourquoi?*）』所长雷霆大怒。——『因为没法喝。』侍寝总管平静地说。——『没法喝？胡说！』所长狂怒地喊。——『请您尝一尝，所长先生。』——『我尝？我凭什么尝？咖啡完全好，对你们这些人够好的了。这荒唐透顶——』——『要不咱们都尝尝？』监守谄媚地提议。——『嗯，好啊。』来客温和地说。——『尝？当然不行。这荒唐透顶，我要惩罚——』——『如果你们不介意，我倒想尝一点。』来客说。——『哦，当然，您请便。』所长温和地同意了。『给我来一杯那个咖啡，喂！』——『乐意效劳，先生。』侍寝总管说。所长——让先生，您准会笑破肚皮——抓过杯子，举到唇边，带着可怕的表情（眼珠子几乎爆出脑袋）吞了下去，凶狠地喊：『**好喝极了！**』监守取了一满杯；抿了抿；把咖啡泼了出去，一副被击中眼睛的样子，评了一句：『啊。』

===Original===

The *maître de chambre*﻿—M’sieu’ Jean he is clever﻿—scooped the third cupful from the bottom of the pail, and very politely, with a big bow, handed it to the Visitor; who took it, touched it to his lips, turned perfectly green, and cried out ‘Impossible!’ M’sieu’ Jean, we all thought﻿—the Directeur and the Surveillant and the *maître de chambre* and myself﻿—that he was going to vomit. He leaned against the wall a moment, quite green; then recovering said faintly﻿—‘The Kitchen.’ The Directeur looked very nervous and shouted, trembling all over, ‘Yes, indeed! We’ll see the cook about this perfectly impossible coffee. I had no idea that my men were getting such coffee. It’s abominable! That’s what it is, an outrage!’﻿—And they all tottered downstairs to The Cook; and M’sieu Jean, they searched the kitchen; and what do you think? They found ten pounds of coffee and twelve pounds of sugar all neatly hidden away, that The Cook had been saving for himself out of our allowance. He’s a beast, the Cook!”
I must say that, although the morning coffee improved enormously for as much as a week, it descended afterwards to its original level of excellence.
The Cook, I may add, officiated three times a week at a little table to the left as you entered the dining-room. Here he stood, and threw at everyone (as everyone entered) a hunk of the most extraordinary meat which I have ever had the privilege of trying to masticate﻿—it could not be tasted. It was pale and leathery. B. and myself often gave ours away in our hungriest moments; which statement sounds as if we were generous to others, whereas the reason for these donations was that we couldn’t eat, let alone stand the sight of this staple of diet. We had to do our donating on the sly, since the chef always gave us choice pieces and we were anxious not to hurt the chef’s feelings. There was a good deal of spasmodic protestation *apropos la viande*, but the Cook always bullied it down﻿—nor was the meat his fault; since, from the miserable carcases which I have often seen carried into the kitchen from without, the Cook had to select something which would suit the meticulous stomach of the Lord of Hell, as also the less meticulous digestive organs of his minions; and it was only after every *planton* had got a piece of viande to his plantonic taste that the captives, female and male, came in for consideration.

===Chinese===

侍寝总管——让先生，他真机灵——从桶底舀了第三杯，非常礼貌地、深深鞠了一躬，递给来客；来客接过，碰了碰嘴唇，脸登时绿透，喊出声：『不可能！』让先生，我们当时都想——所长、监守、侍寝总管和我——他要吐了。他靠着墙站了一会儿，绿得很；缓过来之后，虚弱地说——『厨房。』所长显得极为紧张，浑身发抖地喊：『正是！这杯完全不可能的咖啡，我们要找厨子算账。我根本不知道我的人喝的是这种咖啡。可恶！就是这样，暴行！』——于是他们全体摇摇晃晃地下楼找厨子；让先生，他们搜了厨房；您猜怎么着？他们找到了十磅咖啡和十二磅糖，藏得整整齐齐，那是厨子从我们的口粮里给自己攒下的。他是个畜生，那厨子！」
我必须说，虽然晨间咖啡极大改善了一个星期之久，随后又降回了它原本的卓越水准。
厨子，我补充一句，每周三次在饭厅入口左手一张小桌前主持仪式。他站在那里，朝每个进门的人扔一块我此生有幸试嚼过的最非凡的肉——它没法尝。灰白而坚韧。B.和我常在最饿的时刻把我们的那份送人；这话听着像我们慷慨待人，实则捐献的理由是我们吃不下，更经不起看这种主食。我们的捐献只能偷偷进行，因为厨长总给我们挑好的，我们又不愿伤厨长的感情。关于肉（*apropos la viande*）常有阵发性的抗议，但厨子总能骂压下去——况且肉也怪不得他；因为我常见那此可怜巴巴的胴体从外面抬进厨房，厨子得从中挑选既适合地狱之主那挑剔的胃、也适合其爪牙们不那么挑剔的消化器官的东西；要等每个卫兵都拿到一块合乎卫兵口味的肉（viande）之后，男女俘虏们才轮得上被考虑。

===Original===

On the whole, I think I never envied the Cook his strange and difficult, not to say gruesome, job. With the men en masse he was bound to be unpopular. To the goodwill of those above he was necessarily more or less a slave. And on the whole, I liked the Cook very much, as did B.﻿—for the very good and sufficient reason that he liked us both.
About the *plantons* I have something to say, something which it gives me huge pleasure to say. I have to say, about the *plantons*, that as a bunch they struck me at the time and will always impress me as the next to the lowest species of human organism; the lowest, in my experienced estimation, being the gendarme proper. The *plantons* were, with one exception﻿—he of the black holster with whom I collided on the first day﻿—changed from time to time. Again with this one exception, they were (as I have noted) apparently disabled men who were enjoying a vacation from the trenches in the lovely environs of Orne. Nearly all of them were witless. Every one of them had something the matter with him physically as well. For instance, one *planton* had a large wooden hand. Another was possessed of a long unmanageable left leg made, as nearly as I could discover, of tin. A third had a huge glass eye.
These peculiarities of physique, however, did not inhibit the *plantons* from certain essential and normal desires. On the contrary. The *plantons* probably realised that, in competition with the male world at large, their glass legs and tin hands and wooden eyes would not stand a Chinaman’s chance of winning the affection and admiration of the fair sex. At any rate they were always on the alert for opportunities to triumph over the admiration and affection of *les femmes* at La Ferté, where their success was not endangered by competition. They had the bulge on everybody; and they used what bulge they had to such good advantage that one of them, during my stay, was pursued with a revolver by their sergeant, captured, locked up and shipped off for court-martial on the charge of disobedience and threatening the life of a superior officer.

===Chinese===

总的来说，我想我从未羡慕过厨子那份古怪而艰难、不妨说狰狞的差事。面对男人全体，他注定不得人心。对上头的恩宠，他多多少少必然是个奴隶。而总的说来，我很喜欢厨子，B.也一样——理由非常充分：他喜欢我们俩。
关于卫兵们，我有些话要说，说出来让我大感痛快的话。关于卫兵们，我必须说：作为一伙，他们当时令我震动，今后也将令我难忘，是人类有机体中倒数第二低的物种；最低的，依我的经验估计，是宪兵本种。卫兵们除一人外——就是头一天同我撞个正着的那位黑枪套——不时更换。同样除这位之外，他们（如我说过的）显然是些病残者，在奥恩省可爱的郊外享受战壕之外的休假。他们几乎全都无智。每个人身上还都带点生理毛病。比如，一个卫兵有一只大木头手。另一个长着一条冗长难驯的左腿，据我尽力探查，是锡做的。第三个有一只巨大的玻璃眼。
然而这些体质上的特异，并不妨碍卫兵们某些正常而基本的欲望。恰恰相反。卫兵们想必明白：同广大男性世界竞争，他们的玻璃腿、锡手和木头眼赢得女性青睐的机会连中国人都不如。总之他们时刻警惕着在拉费尔泰征服女人们（*les femmes*）的爱慕的机会，在那儿他们的成功不受竞争威胁。他们对所有人占着上风；而这上风他们用得如此之好，以至于我待在那儿期间，其中一个被他们的军士掃着左轮追捕、抓获、关押、押去军事法庭，罪名是不服从命令并威胁上级军官的生命。

===Original===

He had been caught with the goods﻿—that is to say, in the girl’s *cabinot*﻿—by said superior: an incapable, strutting, undersized, bepimpled person in a bright uniform who spent his time assuming the poses of a general for the benefit of the ladies; of his admiration for whom and his intentions toward whom he made no secret. By all means one of the most disagreeable petty bullies whom I ever beheld. This arrest of a *planton* was, so long as I inhabited La Ferté, the only case in which abuse of the weaker sex was punished. That attempts at abuse were frequent I know from allusions and direct statements made in the letters which passed by way of the sweeper from the girls to their captive admirers. I might say that the senders of these letters, whom I shall attempt to portray presently, have my unmitigated and unqualified admiration. By all odds they possessed the most terrible vitality and bravery of any human beings, women or men, whom it has ever been my extraordinary luck to encounter, or ever will be (I am absolutely sure) in this world.
The duties of the *plantons* were those simple and obvious duties which only very stupid persons can perfectly fulfill, namely: to take turns guarding the building and its inhabitants; not to accept bribes, whether in the form of matches, cigarettes or conversation, from their prisoners; to accompany anyone who went anywhere outside the walls (as did occasionally the *balayeurs*, to transport baggage; the men who did *corvée*; and the catchers of water for the cook, who proceeded as far as the hydrant situated on the outskirts of the town﻿—a momentous distance of perhaps five hundred feet); and finally to obey any and all orders from all and any superiors without thinking. *Plantons* were supposed﻿—but only supposed﻿—to report any schemes for escaping which they might overhear during their watch upon *les femmes et les hommes en promenade*. Of course they never overheard any, since the least intelligent of the watched was a paragon of wisdom by comparison with the watchers. B. and I had a little ditty about *plantons*, of which I can quote (unfortunately) only the first line and refrain:

> “A *planton* loved a lady once

(Cabbages and cauliflowers!)”

===Chinese===

他人赃并获——也就是说，在那姑娘的小号（*cabinot*）里——被上述上级当场拿获：那是个无能、神气活现、矮小、一脸粉刺的家伙，穿一身鲜亮制服，把时间都花在为女士们摆将军的姿势上；他对她们的倾慕和意图毫不遮掩。无论如何算我此生见过最讨厌的小恶霸之一。只要我还住在拉费尔泰，这次对卫兵的逮捕就是唯一一次迫害弱性受到惩罚的事件。迫害未遂的频繁，我是从姑娘们经扫地人之手递给她们被囚爱慕者的信中的暗示和明言里知道的。我可以说，这些信的寄件人——我下面就试图描绘她们——拥有我毫无保留的钦佩。毫无疑问，她们拥有我这辈子有幸遇见的——而且我绝对确信今后也永远有幸——人类（无论女人男人）中最可怕的生命力和勇气。
卫兵们的职责是简单而明确的职责，只有非常愚蠢的人才能完美履行，即：轮流看守建筑及其住户；不接受囚犯任何形式的贿赂，无论是火柴、香烟还是交谈；护送任何出墙的人（例如扫地人偶尔搬运行李；服劳役的人；以及替厨子打水的人，他们最远走到镇郊的水栓——一段要紧的距离，约五百英尺）；最后，不加思索地服从一切上级的一切命令。卫兵们理应——但仅仅是理应——上报他们在看守放风的男女（*les femmes et les hommes en promenade*）时可能偷听到的任何越狱计划。当然他们什么也没偷听到过，因为被看守者里最笨的，同看守者一比也是智慧的楷模。B.和我为卫兵们编了一支小曲，可惜我只能引第一句和叠句：

> 「一个卫兵爱上过一位淑女

（卷心菜和花椰菜！）」

===Original===

It was a very fine song. In concluding my remarks upon *plantons* I must, in justice to my subject, mention the three prime plantonic virtues﻿—they were (1) beauty, as regards face and person and bearing, (2) chivalry, as regards women, (3) heroism, as regards males.
The somewhat unique and amusing appearance of the *plantons* rather militated against than served to inculcate Fear﻿—it was therefore not wonderful that they and the desired emotion were supported by two strictly enforced punishments, punishments which were meted out with equal and unflinching severity to both sexes alike. The less undesirable punishment was known as *pain sec*﻿—which Fritz, shortly after my arrival, got for smashing a windowpane by accident; and which Harree and Pom Pom, the incorrigibles, were getting most of the time. This punishment consisted in denying to the culprit all nutriment save two stone-hard morsels of dry bread per diem. The culprit’s intimate friends, of course, made a point of eating only a portion of their own morsels of soft, heavy, sour bread (we got two a day, with each *soupe*) and presenting the culprit with the rest. The common method of getting *pain sec* was also a simple one﻿—it was for a man to wave, shout or make other signs audible or visual to an inhabitant of the women’s quarters; and, for a girl, to be seen at her window by the Directeur at any time during the morning and afternoon promenades of the men. The punishment for sending a letter to a girl might possibly be *pain sec*, but was more often﻿—I pronounce the word even now with a sinking of the heart, though curiously enough I escaped that for which it stands﻿—*cabinot*.
There were (as already mentioned) a number of *cabinots*, sometimes referred to as *cachots* by persons of linguistic propensities. To repeat myself a little: at least three were situated on the ground floor; and these were used whenever possible in preference to the one or ones upstairs, for the reason that they were naturally more damp and chill and dark and altogether more dismal and unhealthy. Dampness and cold were considerably increased by the substitution, for a floor, of two or three planks resting here and there in mud. I am now describing what my eyes saw, not what was shown to the inspectors on their rare visits to the Directeur’s little shop for making criminals.

===Chinese===

那是一支非常好的歌。在结束关于卫兵们的评论时，为对得起我的题目，我必须提一提卫兵的三大美德——(1) 美貌，就面容、身材与仪态而言；(2) 骑士精神，就女性而言；(3) 英雄主义，就男性而言。
卫兵们那副颇为独特而滑稽的模样，与其说有助于灌输恐惧，不如说有损于恐惧——因此，他们和那种想望的情绪得到两种严格执行的惩罚的支持，也就不足为奇了；这两种惩罚对两性一视同仁、毫不留情地施加。较不可憎的那种叫干面包（*pain sec*）——我刚到不久，弗里茨就因失手打碎窗玻璃领教过；而不可救药的阿累和彭彭则几乎常年领着。这种惩罚的内容是：每天只许罪犯吃两块石头般硬的干面包，其余营养一概禁绝。罪犯的亲密朋友们当然会讲究地只吃掉自己那份又软又重又酸的面包的一部分（我们每天两块，随每顿汤〔soupe〕发放），把其余的献给罪犯。领干面包的常见方法也很简单——男人朝女监的住户挥手、喊叫或做其他可闻可见的暗号；姑娘则是赶上男人早晚放风时在窗口被所长瞧见。给姑娘递信的惩罚可能是干面包，但更常见的是——我此刻念出这个词心仍往下沉，尽管蹊跷的是我竟逃过了它所代表的东西——小号（*cabinot*）。
小号（如前所述）有若干间，有语言癖的人士有时称之为密室（*cachot*）。稍作重复：至少三间位于底层；只要可能就用它们，而不用楼上的一间或几间，理由是它们天然更潮湿、更冷、更黑，总之更阴惨、更不卫生。潮和冷又被大幅加强：地面换成了两三块这里那里搭在烂泥上的木板。我现在描述的是我亲眼所见，而不是所长那间制造罪犯的小铺在检查官们难得来访时展示给他们看的东西。

===Original===

I know what these occasional visitors beheld, because it, too, I have seen with my own eyes: seen the two *balayeurs* staggering downstairs with a bed (consisting of a high iron frame, a huge mattress of delicious thickness, spotless sheets, warm blankets, and a sort of quilt neatly folded over all); seen this bed placed by the panting sweepers in the thoroughly cleaned and otherwise immaculate *cabinot* at the foot of the stairs and opposite the kitchen, the well-scrubbed door being left wide open. I saw this done as I was going to dinner. While the men were upstairs recovering from *la soupe*, the gentleman-inspectors were invited downstairs to look at a specimen of the Directeur’s kindness﻿—a kindness which he could not restrain even in the case of those who were guilty of some terrible wrong. (The little Belgian with the Broken Arm, alias the Machine-Fixer, missed not a word nor a gesture of all this; and described the scene to me with an indignation which threatened his sanity.) Then, while *les hommes* were in the *cour* for the afternoon, the sweepers were rushed to The Enormous Room, which they cleaned to beat the band with the fear of Hell in them; after which, the Directeur led his amiable guests leisurely upstairs and showed them the way the men kept their quarters; kept them without dictation on the part of the officials, so fond were they of what was to them one and all more than a delightful temporary residence﻿—was in fact a home. From The Enormous Room the procession wended a gentle way to the women’s quarters (scrubbed and swept in anticipation of their arrival) and so departed; conscious﻿—no doubt﻿—that in the Directeur France had found a rare specimen of wholehearted and efficient generosity.
Upon being sentenced to *cabinot*, whether for writing an intercepted letter, fighting, threatening a *planton*, or committing some minor offense for the *n*th time, a man took one blanket from his bed, carried it downstairs to the *cachot*, and disappeared therein for a night or many days and nights as the case might be. Before entering he was thoroughly searched and temporarily deprived of the contents of his pockets, whatever they might include. It was made certain that he had no cigarettes nor tobacco in any other form upon his person, and no matches.

===Chinese===

这些不速之客看见的是什么，我知道，因为那也是我亲眼见过的：见过两个扫地人扛着一张床蹒跚下楼（床由高高的铁架、一张厚得可口的巨大床垫、雪白的床单、暖和的毯子和一床叠得整齐的被子组成）；见过气喘吁吁的扫地人把这张床安放在楼梯脚下、厨房对面那间彻底打扫过、其余一尘不染的小号里，擦得锃亮的门大敞四开。这是我吃晚饭的路上看见的。男人们在楼上从汤（*la soupe*）里缓神的当儿，检查官先生们被请下楼，参观所长先生的仁慈样品——一种即便对犯下可怕过错的人他也克制不住的仁慈。（断臂的小个子比利时人，即修机匠，对此一五一十全看在眼里；他向我描述那个场面时，义愤得几乎危及神智。）随后，趁男人们在院子里下午放风，扫地人被火速调去巨室，怀着地狱的恐惧把它打扫得赛过一切；之后，所长领着他可爱的客人们从容上楼，向他们展示男人们打理自己住所的方式；无需官员们吩咐自己打理，因为男人们太爱这个对他们每个人而言远不止是愉快临时居所的地方了——它实际上是个家。队伍从巨室緩緩曲折地走到女监（为他们的到来预先刷洗扫除过），就此离去；心里毫无疑问地认定：所长身上，法兰西发现了一个全心全意而高效的慷慨典范。
被判处小号——无论是因一封被截的信、打架、威胁卫兵，还是第 n 次犯点小规——的人从床上拿一条毯子，提下楼送进密室（*cachot*），视情形在里面消失一夜或许多个日夜。入内之前要被彻底搜身，暂时没收口袋里的一切，不管装的是什么。要确保他身上没有香烟或任何形式的烟草，没有火柴。

===Original===

The door was locked behind him and double and triple locked﻿—to judge by the sound﻿—by a *planton*, usually the Black Holster, who on such occasions produced a ring of enormous keys suggestive of a burlesque jailer. Within the stone walls of his dungeon (into which a beam of light no bigger than a ten-cent piece, and in some cases no light at all, penetrated) the culprit could shout and scream his or her heart out if he or she liked, without serious annoyance to His Majesty King Satan. I wonder how many times, en route to *la soupe* or The Enormous Room or promenade, I have heard the unearthly smouldering laughter of girls or of men entombed within the drooling greenish walls of La Ferté Macé. A dozen times, I suppose, I have seen a friend of the entombed stoop adroitly and shove a cigarette or a piece of chocolate under the door, to the girls or the men or the girl or man screaming, shouting, and pommeling faintly behind that very door﻿—but, you would say by the sound, a good part of a mile away.﻿ ﻿… Ah well, more of this later, when we come to *les femmes* on their own account.
The third method employed to throw Fear into the minds of his captives lay, as I have said, in the sight of the Captor Himself. And this was by far the most efficient method.
He loved to suddenly dash upon the girls when they were carrying their slops along the hall and downstairs, as (in common with the men) they had to do at least twice every morning and twice every afternoon. The *corvée* of girls and men were of course arranged so as not to coincide; yet somehow or other they managed to coincide on the average about once a week, or if not coincide, at any rate approach coincidence. On such occasions, as often as not under the *planton’s* very stupid nose, a kiss or an embrace would be stolen﻿—provocative of much fierce laughter and some scurrying. Or else, while the moneyed captives (including B. and Cummings) were waiting their turn to enter the *bureau* de M.

===Chinese===

门在他身后锁上，而且听声音是两道三道地锁——由一名卫兵执行，通常是黑枪套，这种场合他必掏出一大串钥匙，活脱脱滑稽剧里的狱卒。在地牢的石墙之内（一道不比一角硬币大的光，有时压根没有光，能射进去），罪犯尽可以随心把心喊出来叫出来，而不至于严重打搅撒旦国王陛下的清梦。我不知道有多少次，在去喝汤或去巨室或去放风的路上，我听见拉费尔泰马塞流着涎水的绿墙内活埋着的姑娘们或男人们那非人间的阴燃的笑声。我想有十几次，我看见被活埋者的朋友敏捷地俯身，从门下塞进一支烟或一块巧克力，给那些在那扇门后尖叫、嘶喊、微弱捶打的姑娘们或男人们——但听声音，你会说他们远在一英里开外。…唉，这些稍后再谈，等我们专门谈女人们。
把恐惧投进俘虏们心里的第三种方法，如我说过的，在于亲眼见到捕获者本人。而这是迄今最有效的方法。
他最爱趁姑娘们端着污水沿过道下楼时猛扑上去，因为她们（同男人们一样）每天上午至少两次、下午两次必须倒污水。姑娘们和男人们的劳役（corvée）当然错开安排；可不知怎么，它们平均每周总有那么一回撞在一起，即使不撞上，至少也逼近重合。这种场合，十有八九就在卫兵极蠢的鼻子底下，能偷到一个吻或一个拥抱——激起一阵凶猛的大笑和小小的骚动。又或者，当有钱的俘虏们（包括B.和卡明斯）排队等着进账房先生（M.

===Original===

le Gestionnaire, or even were ascending the stairs with a *planton* behind them, en route to Mecca, along the hall would come five or six women staggering and carrying huge pails full to the brim of everyone knew what; five or six heads lowered, ill-dressed bodies tense with effort, free arms rigidly extended from the shoulder downward and outward in a plane at right angles to their difficult progress and thereby helping to balance the disconcerting load﻿—all embarrassed, some humiliated, others desperately at ease﻿—along they would come under the steady sensual gaze of the men, under a gaze which seemed to eat them alive﻿ ﻿… and then one of them would laugh with the laughter which is neither pitiful nor terrible, but horrible.﻿ ﻿…
And **bang**! would a door fly open, and **roar!** a well-dressed animal about five feet six inches in height, with prominent cuffs and a sportive tie, the altogether decently and neatly clothed thick-built figure squirming from top to toe with anger, the large head trembling and white-faced beneath a flourishing mane of coarse blackish bristly perhaps hair, the arm crooked at the elbow and shaking a huge fist of pinkish well-manicured flesh, the distinct, cruel, brightish eyes sprouting from their sockets under bushily enormous black eyebrows, the big, weak, coarse mouth extended almost from ear to ear, and spouting invective, the soggily brutal lips clinched upward and backward, showing the huge horse-like teeth to the froth-shot gums﻿—
And I saw once a little girl eleven years old scream in terror and drop her pail of slops, spilling most of it on her feet; and seize it in a clutch of frail child’s fingers, and stagger, sobbing and shaking, past the Fiend﻿—one hand held over her contorted face to shield her from the Awful Thing of Things﻿—to the head of the stairs, where she collapsed, and was half-carried, half-dragged by one of the older ones to the floor below while another older one picked up her pail and lugged this and her own hurriedly downward.

===Chinese===

le Gestionnaire）的办公室，甚至已经由卫兵押着在楼梯上拾级、朝着麦加进发时，过道里会走来五六个女人，摇摇晃晃，提着满到边沿、人人知道装着什么的大桶；五六颗低垂的头，衣着寒酸的身体因用力而绷紧，空着的那条手臂从肩膀僵直地向下向外伸出，与艰难的行进成直角，以此平衡那令人心慌的负载——个个窘迫，有的羞愤，有的拼命装作自在——她们就这样在男人们steady的肉欲注视下走来，在那道仿佛要把她们活活吃掉的注视下……随后她们中会有一人笑起来，那笑既不可怜也不可怕，而是恐怖。……
接着砰！一扇门飞开，咆哮！一头衣着考究的牲口，身高约五英尺六英寸，翻着显眼的袖口，系一条运动领带，那副穿得相当体面齐整的粗壮身影从头到脚气得乱扭，大脑袋在一蓬浓密、粗硬、发黑的鬃毛——大概算头发——下发抖、发白，一条胳膊曲在肘部，摇着一只粉色、修剪精致的大肉拳，那双分明的、残忍的、微亮的眼睛从毛茸茸的巨大黑眉毛底下爆出眼眶，又大又弱又粗的嘴几乎咧到耳根，喷着咒骂，湿漉漉的粗嘴唇朝上朝后绷紧，露出马一样的大牙和满是泡沫的牙床——
我亲眼见过一次，一个十一岁的小姑娘恐怖地尖叫，摔落手里的污水桶，大半泼在脚上；她用纤弱的孩子的手指抓住桶，蹒跚着，呜咽着、发着抖，从魔王身边挤过去——一只手捂住扭曲的脸，好躲开那万可怕之物——挪到楼梯口，瘫倒在那里，被一个大点的姑娘半抱半拖下楼去，另一个大点的捡起她的桶，提着桶和自己的桶匆匆下去了。

===Original===

And after the last head had disappeared, Monsieur le Directeur continued to rave and shake and tremble for as much as ten seconds, his shoebrush mane crinkling with black anger﻿—then, turning suddenly upon *les hommes* (who cowered up against the wall as men cower up against a material thing in the presence of the supernatural) he roared and shook his pinkish fist at us till the gold stud in his immaculate cuff walked out upon the wad of clenching flesh:
“**And you﻿—take care﻿—if I catch you with the women again I’ll stick you in cabinot for two weeks, all﻿—all of you﻿—**”
for as much as half a minute; then turning his round-shouldered big back suddenly he adjusted his cuffs, muttering **prostitutes** and **whores** and **dirty filth of women**, crammed his big fists into his trousers, pulled in his chin till his fattish jowl rippled along the square jaws, panted, grunted, very completely satisfied, very contented, rather proud of himself, took a strutting stride or two in his expensive shiny boots, and shot all at once through the open door which he **slammed** after him.
---
Apropos the particular incident described for purposes of illustration, I wish to state that I believe in miracles: the miracle being that I did not knock the spit-covered mouthful of teeth and jabbering brutish outthrust jowl (which certainly were not farther than eighteen inches from me) through the bullneck bulging in its spotless collar. For there are times when one almost decides not to merely observe﻿ ﻿… besides which, never in my life before had I wanted to kill, to thoroughly extinguish and to entirely murder. Perhaps﻿ ﻿… some day.﻿ ﻿… Unto God I hope so.
Amen.
Now I will try to give the reader a glimpse of the Women of La Ferté Macé.
The little Machine-Fixer as I said in the preceding chapter, divided them into Good and Bad. He said there were as much as three Good ones, of which three he had talked to one and knew her story. Another of the three Good Women obviously was Margherite﻿—a big, strong female who did washing, and who was a permanent resident because she had been careless enough to be born of German parents. I think I spoke with number three on the day I waited to be examined by the Commission﻿—a Belgian girl, whom I shall mention later along with that incident.

===Chinese===

最后一颗脑袋消失之后，所长先生还要咆哮、发抖、震颤长达十秒钟，他那鞋刷似的鬃毛因黑色的狂怒而竖起——随即，他猛地转向男人们（男人们贴墙缩着，像人面对超自然时贴着实在的物件缩那样），朝我们吼着、摇着他粉色的拳头，直到他一尘不染的袖口上的金纽扣爬出那团攥紧的肉：
「**还有你们——当心——再让我撞见你们同女人勾搭，我把你们全体——全体——关两星期小号——**」
足足吼了半分钟；然后他忽然背过他圆肩膀的宽背，整了整袖口，咕哝着**婊子**和**野鸡**和**女人的脏烂货**，把两只大拳头塞进裤兜，把下巴收到肥下巴沿着方下巴打褶的程度，喘着，哼着，非常彻底地满足，非常舒坦，颇为自得，穿着他昂贵的亮靴子神气地跨了一两步，忽然射进那扇开着的门，随手砰地摔上。
---
关于为举例而描述的这个具体事件，我想声明：我信奇迹；奇迹在于，我没有把那口喷唾沫的牙和叨叨着兽性外凸的下巴（离我肯定不超过十八英寸）打穿到那件在雪白领子里鼓胀的牛脖子上去。因为有些时候，人几乎决定不再仅仅旁观……何况，我此生从未这样想杀过人，想彻底扑灭、彻底谋杀。也许……有朝一日。……我向上帝希望如此。
阿门。
现在我来试着让读者看一眼拉费尔泰马塞的女人们。
如我在上一章所说，小个子修机匠把她们分成好的与坏的。他说好的多达三位，三位中他同一位交谈过，知道她的身世。三位好女人中的另一位显然是玛格丽特——一个高大强壮的洗衣妇，之所以是常驻居民，只因她粗心到生在一对德国父母手里。第三位我想是在我等候委员会审讯的那天同她说过话的——一位比利时姑娘，我将在后文连同那次事件一并提到。

===Original===

Whereat, by process of elimination, we arrive at *les putains*, whereof God may know how many there were at La Ferté, but I certainly do not. To *les putains* in general I have already made my deep and sincere bow. I should like to speak here of four individuals. They are Celina, Lena, Lily, Renée.
Celina Tek was an extraordinarily beautiful animal. Her firm girl’s body emanated a supreme vitality. It was neither tall nor short, its movements nor graceful nor awkward. It came and went with a certain sexual velocity, a velocity whose health and vigour made everyone in La Ferté seem puny and old. Her deep sensual voice had a coarse richness. Her face, dark and young, annihilated easily the ancient and greyish walls. Her wonderful hair was shockingly black. Her perfect teeth, when she smiled, reminded you of an animal. The cult of Isis never worshipped a more deep luxurious smile. This face, framed in the night of its hair, seemed (as it moved at the window overlooking the *cour des femmes*) inexorably and colossally young. The body was absolutely and fearlessly alive. In the impeccable and altogether admirable desolation of La Ferté and the Normandy Autumn Celina, easily and fiercely moving, was a kinesis.
The French Government must have already recognized this; it called her incorrigible.
Lena, also a Belgian, always and fortunately just missed being a type which in the American language (sometimes called “Slang”) has a definite nomenclature. Lena had the makings of an ordinary broad, and yet, thanks to *La Misère*, a certain indubitable personality became gradually rescued. A tall hard face about which was loosely pitched some hay-coloured hair. Strenuous and mutilated hands. A loose, raucous way of laughing, which contrasted well with Celina’s definite gurgling titter. Energy rather than vitality. A certain power and roughness about her laughter. She never smiled. She laughed loudly and obscenely and always. A woman.
Lily was a German girl, who looked unbelievably old, wore white, or once white dresses, had a sort of drawling scream in her throat besides a thick deadly cough, and floundered leanly under the eyes of men. Upon the skinny neck of Lily a face had been set for all the world to look upon and be afraid. The face itself was made of flesh green and almost putrescent. In each cheek a bloody spot.

===Chinese===

如此，经由排除法，我们来到了妓女们（*les putains*）中间；拉费尔泰到底有多少位，天知道，我确实不知道。对妓女们全体，我已经深深而真诚地致敬过了。我想在此谈四个个人。她们是：塞利娜、莱娜、莉莉、勒内。
塞利娜·泰克是个非凡美丽的动物。她结实的少女身体散发着至高的生命力。不高也不矮，动作说不上优雅也说不上笨拙。它带着一种性的速度来来去去，那速度的健康与活力使拉费尔泰的每个人显得侏儒而衰老。她深沉肉感的嗓音有一种粗枥的丰厚。她的脸，黝黑而年轻，轻易抹平了那些古老发灰的墙。她美妙的头发黑得骇人。她微笑时，那口完美的牙齿让你想起某种动物。伊西斯崇拜也未曾膜拜过比这更深艳的微笑。这张脸，框在头发的夜里，似乎（当它在那俯瞰女院的窗口移动时）无可抗拒地、巨大地年轻。这身体毫无畏惧地绝对地活着。在拉费尔泰和诺曼底之秋那无可挑剔、令人赞叹的荒凉里，轻松而凶猛地移动着的塞利娜，是一种动（kinesis）。
法国政府想必早已认识到这一点；它称她为不可救药。
莱娜，也是比利时人，总是、而且幸好总是差一点够上美国话（有时叫「俚语」）里有确切名称的一个类型。莱娜有做一个寻常婆娘的坯子，然而，多亏苦难（*La Misère*），某种不可否认的个性被渐渐救了出来。一张坚硬的长脸，松松地盖着些干草色的头发。吃力和伤残的手。一种放纵嘶哑的大笑，同塞利娜明确的咯咯窃笑恰成对照。是能量，而非生命力。她的笑里有某种力量和粗砺。她从不微笑。她大声地、下流地、永远地笑。一个女人。
莉莉是个德国姑娘，看上去老得难以置信，穿白裙子，或者说曾经白过的裙子，喉咙里除了浓重的致命咳嗽，还藏着一种拖长音的尖叫，在男人们的注视下精瘦地挣扎。莉莉那瘦骨嶙峋的脖子上，安着一张供全世界观看并害怕的脸。脸本身由发绿、几乎腐烂的肉构成。每边腮上各有一块血斑。

===Original===

Which was not rouge, but the flower which consumption plants in the cheek of its favourite. A face vulgar and vast and heavy-featured, about which a smile was always flopping uselessly. Occasionally Lily grinned, showing several monstrously decayed and perfectly yellow teeth, which teeth usually were smoking a cigarette. Her bluish hands were very interestingly dead; the fingers were nervous, they lived in cringing bags of freckled skin, they might almost be alive.
She was perhaps eighteen years old.
Renée, the fourth member of the circle, was always well-dressed and somehow chic. Her silhouette had character, from the waved coiffure to the enormously high heels. Had Renée been able to restrain a perfectly toothless smile she might possibly have passed for a *jeune gonzesse*. She was not. The smile was ample and black. You saw through it into the back of her neck. You felt as if her life was in danger when she smiled, as it probably was. Her skin was not particularly tired. But Renée was old, older than Lena by several years; perhaps twenty-five. Also about Renée there was a certain dangerous fragility, the fragility of unhealth. And yet Renée was hard, immeasurably hard. And accurate. Her exact movements were the movements of a mechanism. Including her voice, which had a purely mechanical timbre. She could do two things with this voice and two only﻿—screech and boom. At times she tried to chuckle and almost fell apart. Renée was in fact dead. In looking at her for the first time, I realised that there may be something stylish about death.
This first time was interesting in the extreme. It was Lily’s birthday. We looked out of the windows which composed one side of the otherwise windowless Enormous Room; looked down, and saw﻿—just outside the wall of the building﻿—Celina, Lena, Lily and a new girl who was Renée. They were all individually intoxicated, Celina was joyously tight. Renée was stiffly bunnied. Lena was raucously pickled. Lily, floundering and staggering and tumbling and whirling was utterly soused. She was all tricked out in an erstwhile dainty dress, white, and with ribbons. Celina (as always) wore black. Lena had on a rather heavy striped sweater and skirt. Renée was immaculate in tight-fitting satin or something of the sort; she seemed to have somehow escaped from a doll’s house overnight.

===Chinese===

那不是胭脂，而是痨病种在其宠儿腮上的花。一张粗俗、阔大、五官沉重的脸，一个永远在上头徒劳扑腾的微笑。偶尔莉莉咧嘴一笑，露出几颗烂得骇人、黄得纯粹的牙，牙上通常正掃着一支烟。她发青的手有趣地死着；手指紧张，住在起雀斑的皮口袋里瑟缩，几乎算得上活着。
她大约十八岁。
这个圈子的第四位，勒内，永远穿得体面，而且说不清地时撜。她的侧影有性格，从烫过的发式到极高的高跟。假如勒内能克制住那完全不设防的无牙微笑，她没准能冒充一个妙龄小妞（jeune gonzesse）。她克制不住。那微笑又宽又黑。你透过它一直看到她的后脖颈。她一笑，你就觉得她的性命堪虞——多半也真堪虞。她的皮肤并不特别疲态。但勒内老了，比莱娜还老好几岁；大概二十五。勒内周身还有某种危险的脆弱，不健康的脆弱。然而勒内是硬的，不可估量地硬。而且精确。她那精确的动作是机械的动作。包括她的嗓音，那是一种纯粹机械的音色。这嗓音她只会干两件事，只有两件——尖叫和轰鸣。有时她试着轻笑，差点散架。勒内实际上是死的。第一次看她时我认识到：死亡也许自有其时撜。
这第一次有趣到极点。那天是莉莉的生日。我们从巨室仅有的那一面窗望出去；往下看，看见——就在楼墙外——塞利娜、莱娜、莉莉，和一个新来的姑娘，就是勒内。她们各自都醉了，塞利娜欢天喜地地醉。勒内僵硬地兔子般地醉。莱娜粗声大气地腌透了。莉莉，扑腾着、踉跄着、跌倒着、打转着，彻底醉透。她一身往日俏丽的裙子，白的，还带缎带。塞利娜（一如往常）穿黑。莱娜套着一件挺厚的条纹毛衣和裙子。勒内一身缎子或什么类似的东西，紧得 impeccable；她似乎是连夜从什么娃娃之家逃出来的。

===Original===

About the group were a number of *plantons*, roaring with laughter, teasing, insulting, encouraging, from time to time attempting to embrace the ladies. Celina gave one of them a terrific box on the ear. The mirth of the others was redoubled. Lily spun about and fell down, moaning and coughing, and screaming about her fiancée in Belgium: what a handsome young fellow he was, how he had promised to marry her﻿ ﻿… shouts of enjoyment from the *plantons*. Lena had to sit down or else fall down, so she sat down with a good deal of dignity, her back against the wall, and in that position attempted to execute a kind of dance. *Les plantons* rocked and applauded. Celina smiled beautifully at the men who were staring from every window of The Enormous Room and, with a supreme effort, went over and dragged Renée (who had neatly and accurately folded up with machine-like rapidity in the mud) through the doorway and into the house. Eventually Lena followed her example, capturing Lily en route. The scene must have consumed all of twenty minutes. The *plantons* were so mirth-stricken that they had to sit down and rest under the washing-shed. Of all the inhabitants of The Enormous Room, Fritz and Harree and Pom Pom and Bathhouse John enjoyed it most. I should include Jan, whose chin nearly rested on the windowsill with the little body belonging to it fluttering in an ugly interested way all the time. That Bathhouse John’s interest was largely cynical is evidenced by the remarks which he threw out between spittings﻿—“*Une section mesdames!*” “*A la gare!*” “*Aux armes tout le monde!*” etc. With the exception of these enthusiastic watchers, the other captives evidenced vague amusement﻿—excepting Count Bragard who said with lofty disgust that it was “no better than a bloody knocking ’ouse, Mr. Cummings” and Monsieur Pet-airs whose annoyance amounted to agony. Of course these twain were, comparatively speaking, old men.﻿ ﻿…
The four female incorrigibles encountered less difficulty in attaining *cabinot* than any four specimens of incorrigibility among *les hommes*. Not only were they placed in dungeon vile with a frequency which amounted to continuity; their sentences were far more severe than those handed out to the men. Up to the time of my little visit to La Ferté I had innocently supposed that in referring to women as “the weaker sex” a man was strictly within his rights.

===Chinese===

一圈卫兵围着她们，哄笑，逗弄，辱骂，鼓励，不时试图搂抱各位女士。塞利娜给了其中一个一记大耳光。其余人的欢乐翻了倍。莉莉打着转摔倒，呻吟着咳嗽着，尖声喊她在比利时的未婚夫：他是个多俊的年轻小伙子啊，他怎么答应过要娶她……卫兵们阵阵喝彩。莱娜不得不坐下，否则就得倒下，于是她相当有尊严地坐下，背靠着墙，在那姿势上试图表演一种舞蹈。卫兵们摇摇晃晃地鼓掌。塞利娜朝巨室每扇窗里瞪着看的男人们美丽地笑了笑，然后，以九牛二虎之力，走过去把勒内（她已干净利落、机械精准地对折进烂泥里）拖过门槛拖进楼。终于莱娜也步其后尘，半路截获了莉莉。这一幕足足演了二十分钟。卫兵们笑瘫了，不得不坐到浣洗棚下歇息。巨室全体住户中，弗里茨、阿累、彭彭和澡堂约翰看得最开心。还该算上扬，他的下巴几乎搁在窗台上，下面那具小身子一直丑陋而好奇地飘动。澡堂约翰的兴趣多半是玩世的，证据是他一口一口吐唾沫之间撂下的话——「一支队伍，女士们！（*Une section mesdames!*）」「到车站去！（*A la gare!*）」「全体拿起武器！（*Aux armes tout le monde!*）」等等。除这些热心观众外，其余俘虏只表现出模糊的乐子——布拉加尔伯爵除外，他厌恶地高论这「比血腥的下等窑子强不到哪儿去，卡明斯先生」；佩泰尔先生的恼怒则近乎痛苦。当然，这两位相较而言是老人了。……
这四个不可救药的女人进小号，比男人们中任何四个不可救药的标本都容易。她们不但被投入肮脏地牢的频繁近乎连续；她们的刑期也远比发给男人们的重。直到拉费尔泰小住之前，我一直天真地以为，称女人为「弱性」，男人是严格行使了他的权利的。

===Original===

La Ferté, if it did nothing else for my intelligence, rid it of this overpowering error. I recall, for example, a period of sixteen days and nights spent (during my stay) by the woman Lena in the *cabinot*. It was either toward the latter part of October or the early part of November that this occurred, I will not be sure which. The dampness of the Autumn was as terrible, under normal conditions﻿—that is to say in The Enormous Room﻿—as any climatic eccentricity which I have ever experienced. We had a wood-burning stove in the middle of the room, which antiquated apparatus was kept going all day to the vast discomfort of eyes and noses not to mention throats and lungs﻿—the pungent smoke filling the room with an atmosphere next to unbreathable, but tolerated for the simple reason that it stood between ourselves and death. For even with the stove going full blast the wall never ceased to sweat and even trickle, so overpowering was the dampness. By night the chill was to myself﻿—fortunately bedded at least eighteen inches from the floor and sleeping in my clothes; bedroll, blankets, and all, under and over me and around me﻿—not merely perceptible but desolating. Once my bed broke, and I spent the night perforce on the floor with only my mattress under me; to awake finally in the whitish dawn perfectly helpless with rheumatism. Yet with the exception of my bed and B.’s bed and a wooden bunk which belonged to Bathhouse John, every paillasse lay directly on the floor; moreover the men who slept thus were three-quarters of them miserably clad, nor had they anything beyond their lightweight blankets﻿—whereas I had a complete outfit including a big fur coat, which I had taken with me (as previously described) from the *Section Sanitaire*. The morning after my night spent on the floor I pondered, having nothing to do and being unable to move, upon the subject of my physical endurance﻿—wondering just how the men about me, many of them beyond middle age, some extremely delicate, in all not more than five or six as rugged constitutionally as myself, lived through the nights in The Enormous Room.

===Chinese===

拉费尔泰对我的智力哪怕没干别的，至少替它除了这个压倒性的谬误。我举个例子：莱娜在小号里度过的（我逗留期间的）十六个日夜。那要么是十月末要么是十一月初的事，我说不准是哪个。秋天的潮气，在正常条件下——也就是说在巨室里——已经是我此生经历过的任何气候怪癖中最可怕的一种。屋子中央有一只烧柴的炉子，这具老式器械整日烧着，极大地折磨着众人的眼睛鼻子更遑论喉咙肺——辛辣的烟把屋子填成几乎不可呼吸的大气，但大家容忍它，理由很简单：它隔在我们同死亡之间。因为即使炉子全速烧着，墙也从未停止冒汗甚至淌水，潮气就这样压倒一切。夜里，那寒冷对我来说——所幸我睡在离地至少十八英寸的床上，和衣而眠；铺盖卷、毯子等等压在身下身上、围在四周——不仅可感，而且凄灭。有一次我的床塌了，我被迫在地板上过夜，身下只有床垫；最后在灰白的黎明里醒来，风湿得全然动弹不得。然而，除了我的床、B.的床和澡堂约翰的一张木铺，每张草垫都直接铺在地上；况且这样睡的人四分之三衣衫单薄，除了轻飘飘的毯子一无所有——而我有一整套行头，包括一件大毛皮大衣，是我（如前所述）从卫生分队带出来的。在地板上睡过一夜之后的那个早晨，我无所事事又动弹不得，便思考起自己的耐受力——纳闷我周围这些男人，很多已过中年，有的极其孱弱，总共不过五六个体质同我一样结实，是怎么在巨室的夜里活下来的。

===Original===

Also I recollected glancing through an open door into the women’s quarters, at the risk of being noticed by the *planton* in whose charge I was at the time (who, fortunately, was stupid even for a *planton*, else I should have been well punished for my curiosity) and beholding paillasses identical in all respects with ours reposing on the floor; and I thought, if it is marvellous that old men and sick men can stand this and not die, it is certainly miraculous that girls of eleven and fifteen, and the baby which I saw once being caressed out in the women’s *cour* with unspeakable gentleness by a little *putain* whose name I do not know, and the dozen or so oldish females whom I have often seen on promenade﻿—can stand this and not die. These things I mention not to excite the reader’s pity nor yet his indignation; I mention them because I do not know of any other way to indicate﻿—it is no more than indicating﻿—the significance of the torture perpetrated under the Directeur’s direction in the case of the girl Lena. If incidentally it throws light on the personality of the torturer I shall be gratified.
Lena’s confinement in the *cabinot*﻿—which dungeon I have already attempted to describe but to whose filth and slime no words can begin to do justice﻿—was in this case solitary. Once a day, of an afternoon and always at the time when all the men were upstairs after the second promenade (which gave the writer of this history an exquisite chance to see an atrocity at firsthand), Lena was taken out of the *cabinot* by three *plantons* and permitted a half-hour promenade just outside the door of the building, or in the same locality﻿—delimited by barbed wire on one side and the washing-shed on another﻿—made famous by the scene of inebriety above described. Punctually at the expiration of thirty minutes she was shoved back into the *cabinot* by the *plantons*.

===Chinese===

我还记起曾冒险从一扇开着的门往女监里瞥了一眼——冒着被当时押我的卫兵发现的风险（所幸那家伙即便在卫兵里也算蠢的，否则我的好奇心早受重罚了）——看见同我们一模一样的草垫铺在地上；我想：如果说老人病人能扛住这个而不死是奇迹，那么十一岁、十五岁的姑娘们，以及我见过一次在女院里被一个不知名的小妓女以不可言喻的温柔爱抚着的那个婴儿，以及我常在放风时见到的十几个上了年纪的女人——能扛住这个而不死，就肯定是奇迹中的奇迹了。我提这些，不是为了激起读者的怜悯或义愤；我提它们，是因为我不知道还有什么别的办法可以指明——也只能是指明——在所长指导下对莱娜姑娘施行的酷刑的分量。若它顺带照亮了施刑者的人格，我将感到满意。
莱娜被关的小号——这地牢我已试图描述过，但它的污秽与黏滑任何语言都无从企及——这次是单独监禁。每天一次，在下午、且永远在男人们第二次放风后上楼的时候（这给了这段历史的作者一个亲眼目睹暴行的绝佳机会），莱娜被三名卫兵从小号里提出来，允许在楼门外放风半小时，或者说在同一地点——一侧以铁丝网、另一侧以浣洗棚为界——就是上面那场醉酒戏使其出名的地方。三十分钟一到，她被卫兵们塞回小号。

===Original===

Every day for sixteen days I saw her; noted the indestructible bravado of her gait and carriage, the unchanging timbre of her terrible laughter in response to the salutation of an inhabitant of The Enormous Room (for there were at least six men who spoke to her daily, and took their *pain sec* and their *cabinot* in punishment therefor with the pride of a soldier who takes the *medaille militaire* in recompense for his valour); noted the increasing pallor of her flesh, watched the skin gradually assume a distinct greenish tint (a greenishness which I cannot describe save that it suggested putrefaction); heard the coughing to which she had always been subject grow thicker and deeper till it doubled her up every few minutes, creasing her body as you crease a piece of paper with your thumbnail, preparatory to tearing it in two﻿—and I realised fully and irrevocably and for perhaps the first time the meaning of civilization. And I realised that it was true﻿—as I had previously only suspected it to be true﻿—that in finding us unworthy of helping to carry forward the banner of progress, alias the tricolour, the inimitable and excellent French government was conferring upon B. and myself﻿—albeit with other intent﻿—the ultimate compliment.
And the Machine-Fixer, whose opinion of this blond *putain* grew and increased and soared with every day of her martyrdom till the Machine-Fixer’s former classification of *les femmes* exploded and disappeared entirely﻿—the Machine-Fixer who would have fallen on his little knees to Lena had she given him a chance, and kissed the hem of her striped skirt in an ecstasy of adoration﻿—told me that Lena on being finally released, walked upstairs herself, holding hard to the banister without a look for anyone, “having eyes as big as teacups.” He added, with tears in his own eyes:
“M’sieu’ Jean, a woman.”
I recall perfectly being in the kitchen one day, hiding from the eagle-eye of the Black Holster and enjoying a talk on the economic consequences of war, said talk being delivered by Afrique. As a matter of fact, I was not in the *cuisine* proper but in the little room which I have mentioned previously. The door into the kitchen was shut. The sweetly soft odour of newly cut wood was around me.

===Chinese===

十六天里我天天见到她；注意到她步态与身姿那不可摧毁的虚张，注意到她回应巨室住户问候时那可怕笑声的一成不变的音色（因为至少有六个男人天天同她说话，并以领干面包和小号为代价，带着士兵领取军功章（medaille militaire）作为英勇报酬的骄傲）；注意到她的肉色日益苍白，看着那皮肤渐渐罩上一层明确的青绿（这种青绿我无法形容，只能说它令人联想到腐烂）；听着她一向有的咳嗽变浓变深，直到每隔几分钟就把她拦腰折起，把她的身子像你用拇指折一张纸那样压出折痕，预备撕作两半——我彻底地、不可逆地、而且也许是平生第一次，认识到文明的意义。我认识到这是真的——此前我只是疑心它是真的——法兰西那个无与伦比、出类拔萃的政府，认定我们不配帮助推进进步的旗帜、别名三色旗，实是在——尽管另有所图——给B.和我本人授以终极的赞誉。
而修机匠——这位金发妓女的殉难每进行一天，他对她的见解就增长、攀升一分，直到修机匠先前对女人们的分类彻底爆炸、烟消云散——这个修机匠，只要莱娜给他机会，他会扑通跪倒在他那对小膝盖上，在崇拜的狂喜中亲吻她条纹裙的裙边——他告诉我，莱娜最终获释时，是自己走上楼的，死死抓住楼梯扶手，谁也不看一眼，「眼睛大得像茶杯」。他眼里噙着泪补充道：
「让先生，一个女人。」
我清清楚楚记得有一天在厨房里，躲避黑枪套的鹰眼，听阿弗里克讲授战争的经济后果。实际上我不在厨房（*cuisine*）本部，而在先前提过的那间小屋里。通往厨房的门关着。新伐木料那甜软的气味围绕着我。

===Original===

And all the time that Afrique was talking I heard clearly, through the shut door and through the kitchen wall and through the locked door of the *cabinot* situated directly across the hall from *la cuisine*, the insane gasping voice of a girl singing and yelling and screeching and laughing. Finally I interrupted my speaker to ask what on earth was the matter in the *cabinot*?﻿—“*C’est la femme allemande qui s’appelle Lily*,” Afrique briefly answered. A little later **bang** went the *cabinot* door, and **roar** went the familiar coarse voice of the Directeur. “It disturbs him, the noise,” Afrique said. The *cabinot* door slammed. There was silence. Heavily steps ascended. Then the song began again, a little more insane than before; the laughter a little wilder.﻿ ﻿… “You can’t stop her,” Afrique said admiringly. “A great voice Mademoiselle has, eh? So, as I was saying, the national debt being conditioned﻿—”
But the experience apropos *les femmes*, which meant and will always mean more to me than any other, the scene which is a little more unbelievable than perhaps any scene that it has ever been my privilege to witness, the incident which (possibly more than any other) revealed to me those unspeakable foundations upon which are builded with infinite care such at once ornate and comfortable structures as *La Gloire* and *Le Patriotisme*﻿—occurred in this wise.
The men, myself among them, were leaving *le cour* for The Enormous Room under the watchful eye (as always) of a *planton*. As we defiled through the little gate in the barbed-wire fence we heard, apparently just outside the building whither we were proceeding on our way to The Great Upstairs, a tremendous sound of mingled screams, curses and crashings. The *planton* of the day was not only stupid﻿—he was a little deaf; to his ears this hideous racket had not, as nearly as one could see, penetrated. At all events he marched us along toward the door with utmost plantonic satisfaction and composure. I managed to insert myself in the fore of the procession, being eager to witness the scene within; and reached the door almost simultaneously with Fritz, Harree and two or three others. I forget which of us opened it. I will never forget what I saw as I crossed the threshold.

===Chinese===

而阿弗里克讲话的全过程中，我隔着关着的门、隔着厨房的墙、隔着厨房正对面那间锁着的小号的门，清楚地听见一个姑娘疯了似的喘着气的嗓音，又唱又喊又尖叫又笑。终于我打断了讲演者，问小号里到底出了什么事？——「是那个叫莉莉的德国女人（*C'est la femme allemande qui s'appelle Lily*）。」阿弗里克简短地答。稍后砰的一声小号门响，随后是所长那熟悉的粗嗓门的咆哮。「吵着他了，这动静。」阿弗里克说。小号门砰地关上。一片寂静。沉重的脚步上楼。然后歌声重新开始，比先前更疯了些；笑声更狂了些。……「您拦不住她的。」阿弗里克钦佩地说，「这位小姐有副好嗓子，嗯？好，正如我方才所说，国债既然取决于——」
但那段关于女人们的经历——它对我意味深长，今后也将永远比任何别的事情意味深长——那个比也许我此生有幸目睹的任何场面都更难以置信一点的场面，那件（可能比任何别的）向我揭示了那不可言喻之基础的事件——荣耀（*La Gloire*）与爱国主义（*Le Patriotisme*）这些既华丽又舒适的建筑，正是被无限精心地营造在那基础之上——是这样发生的。
男人们，包括我，正由一名卫兵（一如往常）警惕地押着离开院子回巨室。我们鱼贯穿过铁丝网的小门时，听见——显然就在楼外，我们正朝它走去、前往伟大的楼上的路上——一阵巨大的、尖叫、咒骂与撞击的混响。当班的卫兵不但蠢——还有点聋；这阵可怕的喧器，看样子根本没进过他的耳朵。总之他以最大的卫兵式的满足与镇定，押着我们朝门口行进。我设法挤到了队伍前列，急于目睹门内的场面；同弗里茨、阿累及其他两三人几乎同时到达门口。我忘了是我们谁开的门。我永远不会忘记跨过门槛那一瞬间看见的东西。

===Original===

The hall was filled with stifling smoke; the smoke which straw makes when it is set on fire, a peculiarly nauseous choking, whitish-blue smoke. This smoke was so dense that only after some moments could I make out, with bleeding eyes and wounded lungs, anything whatever. What I saw was this: five or six *plantons* were engaged in carrying out of the nearest *cabinot* two girls, who looked perfectly dead. Their bodies were absolutely limp. Their hands dragged foolishly along the floor as they were carried. Their upward white faces dangled loosely upon their necks. Their crumpled fingers sagged in the *planton’s* arms. I recognised Lily and Renée. Lena I made out at a little distance tottering against the door of the kitchen opposite the *cabinot*, her hay-coloured head drooping and swaying slowly upon the open breast of her shirtwaist, her legs far apart and propping with difficulty her hinging body, her hands spasmodically searching for the knob of the door. The smoke proceeded from the open *cabinot* in great ponderous murdering clouds. In one of these clouds, erect and tense and beautiful as an angel﻿—her wildly shouting face framed in its huge night of dishevelled hair, her deep sexual voice, hoarsely strident above the din and smoke, shouting fiercely through the darkness﻿—stood, triumphantly and colossally young, Celina. Facing her, its clenched, pinkish fists raised high above its savagely bristling head in a big, brutal gesture of impotence and rage and anguish﻿—the Fiend Himself paused quivering. Through the smoke, the great bright voice of Celina rose at him, hoarse and rich and sudden and intensely luxurious, quick, throaty, accurate, slaying deepness:
***Chiez, si vous voulez, chiez**,*
and over and beneath and around the voice I saw frightened faces of women hanging in the smoke, some screaming with their lips apart and their eyes closed, some staring with wide eyes; and among the women’s faces I discovered the large, placid, interested expression of the Gestionnaire and the nervous clicking eyes of the Surveillant. And there was a shout﻿—it was the Black Holster shouting at us as we stood transfixed﻿—
“Who the devil brought the men in here? Get up with you where you belong, you.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

过道里塞满了窒息的烟；稻草点燃时冒的那种烟，一种特别令人作呕的、噎人的、白中带蓝的烟。烟浓到好几分钟后，我才用流血的眼睛和受伤的肺勉强分辨出任何东西。我看见的是这样一幅景象：五六个卫兵正忙着从最近的小号里往外抬两个姑娘，她们看上去完全死了。她们的身体彻底瘫软。被抬着的时候，双手傻乎乎地拖过地面。朝上的白脸在脖子上松松地晃荡。蜷曲的手指在卫兵的臂弯里下坠。我认出是莉莉和勒内。莱娜我在稍远处认出：她正对着小号对面的厨房门摇摇欲坠，干草色的脑袋在她敞开的衬衫胸前低垂、缓缓摇晃，两腿远远叉开、艰难地支着她快要散架的身体，双手痉挛地摸索门把。烟从小号敞开的门里涌出，一团团巨大的、谋杀的云。在其中一团云里，笔直、绷紧、美丽得像个天使——她狂呼乱喊的脸框在蓬乱头发的巨大黑夜里，她深沉肉感的嗓音，在喧声与浓烟之上粗厉尖锐，穿过黑暗凶猛地喊——站着塞利娜，凯旋地、巨大地年轻。面对着她，本魔停在那里发抖，攥紧的粉色拳头高举过那颗凶毛直竖的大头，一个大大的、粗野的、无能为力、狂怒与剧痛的手势。穿过浓烟，塞利娜那巨大明亮的嗓音朝他升起，沙哑、丰厚、突兀、浓艳至极，迅疾、喉音、精准、杀戮的深沉：
「***拉吧，想拉就拉吧，拉**（*Chiez, si vous voulez, chiez*）*！」
在那嗓音之上、之下、四周，我看见悬在烟里的一张张惊惶的女人脸，有的张着唇闭着眼尖叫，有的睁大眼睛呆望；在女人脸中间，我发现账房那副宽大、安详、看热闹的表情和监守那双紧张咔嗒的眼睛。还有一声怒喝——是黑枪套冲我们这些僵立者喊的——
「谁他妈把男人们弄进来的？给我上去，回到你们该待的地方去，你们。……」

===Original===

—And he made a rush at us, and we dodged in the smoke and passed slowly up the hall, looking behind us, speechless to a man with the admiration of Terror till we reached the further flight of stairs; and mounted slowly, with the din falling below us, ringing in our ears, beating upon our brains﻿—mounted slowly with quickened blood and pale faces﻿—to the peace of The Enormous Room.
I spoke with both *balayeurs* that night. They told me, independently, the same story: the four incorrigibles had been locked in the *cabinot ensemble*. They made so much noise, particularly Lily, that the *plantons* were afraid the Directeur would be disturbed. Accordingly the *plantons* got together and stuffed the contents of a paillasse in the cracks around the door, and particularly in the crack under the door wherein cigarettes were commonly inserted by friends of the entombed. This process made the *cabinot* airtight. But the *plantons* were not taking any chances on disturbing Monsieur le Directeur. They carefully lighted the paillasse at a number of points and stood back to see the results of their efforts. So soon as the smoke found its way inward the singing was supplanted by coughing; then the coughing stopped. Then nothing was heard. Then Celina began crying out within﻿—“Open the door, Lily and Renée are dead”﻿—and the *plantons* were frightened. After some debate they decided to open the door﻿—out poured the smoke, and in it Celina, whose voice in a fraction of a second roused everyone in the building. The Black Holster wrestled with her and tried to knock her down by a blow on the mouth; but she escaped, bleeding a little, to the foot of the stairs﻿—simultaneously with the advent of the Directeur who for once had found someone beyond the power of his weapon, Fear, someone in contact with whose indescribable Youth the puny threats of death withered between his lips, someone finally completely and unutterably Alive whom the Lie upon his slavering tongue could not kill.
I do not need to say that, as soon as the girls who had fainted could be brought to, they joined Lena in *pain sec* for many days to come; and that Celina was overpowered by six *plantons*﻿—at the order of Monsieur le Directeur﻿—and reincarcerated in the *cabinot* adjoining that from which she had made her velocitous exit﻿—reincarcerated without food for twenty-four hours.

===Chinese===

——他朝我们冲过来，我们在烟里躲闪着，缓缓沿过道走上去，人人回头张望，被恐怖的赞叹惊得说不出话，直到抵达远处那段楼梯；缓缓登楼，喧声在脚下降落，在我们耳中鸣响，捶击我们的脑门——血液加速、面色苍白地缓缓登楼——回到巨室的安宁。
那晚我同两位扫地人都谈过。他们各自独立地告诉了我同一个故事：四个不可救药的被一起锁进了小号。她们闹出那么大动静，尤其是莉莉，卫兵们怕所长被打扰。于是卫兵们凑到一起，把一只草垫的内瓤塞进门四面的缝，尤其塞进门底那条通常被活埋者的朋友们塞香烟用的缝。这道工序使小号密不透风。但卫兵们还不放心，不敢拿打搅所长先生冒险。他们仔细地在好几个点点燃了草垫，退后欣赏劳动成果。烟一钻进去，歌声就被咳嗽取代；然后咳嗽停了。然后什么也听不见了。然后塞利娜在里面喊起来——「开门，莉莉和勒内死了」——卫兵们慌了。争辩一番之后他们决定开门——烟涌出来，塞利娜随之而出，她的嗓音在一秒钟之内唤醒了整栋楼。黑枪套同她扭打，试图一拳打嘴把她打倒；可她挣脱了，流着一点血，退到楼梯脚下——恰逢所长驾临，他生平第一次遇到了他恐惧这件武器所奈何不得的人，遇到了一个她的不可言喻的青春一贴上去、他那渺小的死亡威胁就在唇边枯萎的人，终于遇到了一个彻底地、不可言喻地活着、他流涎的舌头上那套谎言杀不死的人。
不用说，晕倒的姑娘们一经救醒，就同莱娜一道在之后许多天里领上了干面包；塞利娜则被六名卫兵——奉所长先生之命——制服，重新投入她刚刚高速撤离的那间小号隔壁的一间——重新监禁，二十四小时不给饭食。

===Original===

“*Mais, M’sieu’ Jean*,” the Machine-Fixer said trembling, “*Vous savez elle est forte.* She gave the six of them a fight, I tell you. And three of them went to the doctor as a result of their efforts, including *le vieux* (The Black Holster). But of course they succeeded in beating her up, six men upon one woman. She was beaten badly, I tell you, before she gave in. *M’sieu’ Jean, ils sont tous﻿—les plantons et le Directeur Lui-Même et le Surveillant et le Gestionnaire et tous﻿—ils sont des﻿—*” and he said very nicely what they were, and lit his little black pipe with a crisp curving upward gesture, and shook like a blade of grass.
With which specimen of purely medieval torture I leave the subject of Women, and embark upon the quieter if no less enlightening subject of Sunday.
Sunday, it will be recalled, was Monsieur le Directeur’s third weapon. That is to say: lest the ordinarily tantalising proximity of *les femmes* should not inspire *les hommes* to deeds which placed the doers automatically in the clutches of himself, his subordinates, and *la punition*, it was arranged that once a week the tantalising proximity aforesaid should be supplanted by a positively maddening approach to coincidence. Or in other words, the men and the women for an hour or less might enjoy the same exceedingly small room; for purposes of course of devotion﻿—it being obvious to Monsieur le Directeur that the representatives of both sexes at La Ferté Macé were inherently of a strongly devotional nature. And lest the temptation to err in such moments be deprived, through a certain aspect of compulsion, of its complete force, the attendance of such strictly devotional services was made optional.
The uplifting services to which I refer took place in that very room which (the night of my arrival) had yielded me my paillasse under the Surveillant’s direction. It may have been thirty feet long and twenty wide. At one end was an altar at the top of several wooden stairs, with a large candle on each side. To the right as you entered a number of benches were placed to accommodate *les femmes*. *Les hommes* upon entering took off their caps and stood over against the left wall so as to leave between them and the women an alley perhaps five feet wide.

===Chinese===

「可是，让先生（*Mais, M'sieu' Jean*），」修机匠发着抖说，「您知道她多壮（*Vous savez elle est forte*）。她一个人对付他们六个，我告诉您。他们中有三个因为出力去了军医那儿，包括老家伙（le vieux，即黑枪套）。当然他们还是把她打服了，六个人打一个女人。她被狠狠打了，我告诉您，才服的。让先生，他们全都（*ils sont tous*）——卫兵们、所长本人、监守、账房，全都是（*ils sont des*）——」接着他把他们是什么说得清清楚楚，然后以上挑的弧线干脆地划着火柴点着他的小黑烟斗，抖得像一片草叶。
带着这件纯粹中世纪酷刑的标本，我离开女人们这个题目，登上礼拜日这个更安静、却不见得更少启发的题目。
礼拜日，读者会记得，是所长先生的第三件武器。也就是说：唯恐女人们寻常那撩人的接近还激发不起男人们去干那种干了便自动落入他自己、他的下属和惩罚（*la punition*）之手的勾当，于是安排每周一次，把上述撩人的接近升级为一种真正令人发疯的、同重合的逼近。或者换句话说：每周一次，男人们和女人们可以在同一个极小的房间里共处一小时或不到一小时；当然是为了虔诚——因为在所长先生看来显而易见，拉费尔泰马塞的两性代表天然具有强烈的虔诚气质。又唯恐这种时刻的犯错诱惑因了某种强制而失其全部力量，这种严格虔诚仪式的出席是自愿的。
我说的这种提升性服务，就在（我到达那晚）监守指引我领草垫的那间屋里举行。它也许有三十英尺长、二十英尺宽。一头在几级木阶之上是一座祭坛，两侧各立一支大蜡烛。进门右手摆着若干长凳，供女人们（*les femmes*）坐。男人们（*les hommes*）进门脱帽，靠着左墙站，以便在他们同女人之间留出一条大约五英尺宽的巷子。

===Original===

In this alley stood the Black Holster with his kepi firmly resting upon his head, his arms folded, his eyes spying to left and right in order to intercept any signals exchanged between the sheep and goats. Those who elected to enjoy spiritual things left the *cour* and their morning promenade after about an hour of promenading, while the materially minded remained to finish the promenade; or if one declined the promenade entirely (as frequently occurred owing to the fact that weather conditions on Sunday were invariably more indescribable than usual) a *planton* mounted to The Enormous Room and shouted;
“*La Messe!*”
several times; whereat the devotees lined up and were carefully conducted to the scene of spiritual operations.
The priest was changed every week. His assistant (whom I had the indescribable pleasure of seeing only upon Sundays) was always the same. It was his function to pick the priest up when he fell down after tripping upon his robe, to hand him things before he wanted them, to ring a huge bell, to interrupt the peculiarly divine portions of the service with a squeaking of his shoes, to gaze about from time to time upon the worshippers for purposes of intimidation, and finally﻿—most important of all﻿—to blow out the two big candles at the very earliest opportunity, in the interests (doubtless) of economy. As he was a short, fattish, ancient, strangely soggy creature and as his longish black suit was somewhat too big for him, he executed a series of profound efforts in extinguishing the candles. In fact he had to climb part way up the candles before he could get at the flame; at which moment he looked very much like a weakly and fat boy (for he was obviously in his second or fourth childhood) climbing a flagpole. At moments of leisure he abased his fatty whitish jowl and contemplated with watery eyes the floor in front of his highly polished boots, having first placed his ugly clubby hands together behind his most ample back.
Sunday: green murmurs in coldness. Surplice fiercely fearful, praying on his bony both knees, crossing himself.﻿ ﻿… The Fake French Soldier, alias Garibaldi, beside him, a little face filled with terror﻿ ﻿… the Bell cranks the sharp-nosed priest on his knees﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

黑枪套站在巷子里，制帽纹丝不动地扣在头上，两臂抱胸，双眼左右侦察，以便拦截绵羊与山羊之间的任何暗号。选择享受灵性事物的人，放风约一小时后便离开院子、放弃晨间放风；物质派则留下放完风；若有人干脆拒绝放风（此种情形屡见，因为礼拜日的天气状况总是比平常更难以形容），便有一名卫兵登上巨室喊几嗓子：
「弥撒了！（*La Messe!*）」
于是虔信者们列队，被小心押送至灵性操作的现场。
神父每周一换。他的助手（我只有礼拜日才有难以言喻的荣幸见到）永远是同一位。他的职能是：在神父被长袍绊倒后把他扶起来；在神父还不需要东西之前把东西递给他；摇一只巨大的铃；以鞋底的吱吱声打断仪式中特别神圣的段落；不时扫视礼拜者以资威吓；最后——最重要的一条——以尽可能早的时机吹熄那两支大蜡烛，想必是出于节约的考虑。由于他是个矮小、偏胖、上了年纪、出奇湿软的生物，他那身偏长的黑西装又稍嫌大，他熄蜡烛时得做出一系列深刻的努力。实际上他得先往蜡烛上爬一段才够得着火苗；那一刻他非常像一个虚弱而胖的男孩（他显然已处在他第二或第四次童年）在爬旗杆。闲暇时刻，他垂下肥白的下颔，先把那双丑陋、棒槌似的手背到极其宽敞的背后，再用汪汪的眼睛端详自己锃亮靴子前的地板。
礼拜日：寒气里的绿色呢喃。白袍凶厉地虔敬，用他两根瘦膝盖祈祷，画着十字。……假法国兵，别名加里波第，在他旁边，一张小脸布满恐怖……那口钟把尖鼻子神父摇跪下去……

===Original===

titter from bench of whores﻿—
And that reminds me of a Sunday afternoon on our backs spent with the wholeness of a hill in Chevancourt, discovering a great apple pie, B. and Jean Stahl and Maurice le Menusier and myself; and the sun falling roundly before us.
—And then one *Dimanche* a new high old man with a sharp violet face and green hair﻿—“You are free, my children, to achieve immortality﻿—*Songes, songez, donc﻿—L’Eternité est une existence sans durée﻿—Toujours le Paradis, toujours L’Enfer*” (to the silently roaring whores) “Heaven is made for you”﻿—and the Belgian ten-foot farmer spat three times and wiped them with his foot, his nose dripping; and the nigger shot a white oyster into a far-off scarlet handkerchief﻿—and the priest’s strings came untied and he sidled crablike down the steps﻿—the two candles wiggle a strenuous softness.﻿ ﻿…
In another chapter I will tell you about the nigger.
And another Sunday I saw three tiny old females stumble forward, three very formerly and even once bonnets perched upon three wizened skulls, and flop clumsily before the priest, and take the wafer hungrily into their leathery faces.

===Chinese===

妓女的长凳上响起一声窃笑——
这让我想起一个礼拜日下午，我们仰躺在舍瓦古尔一座完整的小山上，发现了一大块苹果派，B.、让·斯塔尔、细木匠莫里斯和我；太阳圆润地落在我们前方。
——后来有个主日（Dimanche），来了个新高个子老头，一张尖细的紫脸，一头绿发——「你们自由了，我的孩子们，可以放手追求不朽——想想吧，想想——永恒是一种没有长度的存在——永远是天堂，永远是地狱（*Songes, songez, donc—L'Eternité est une existence sans durée—Toujours le Paradis, toujours L'Enfer*）」（对着默默咆哮的妓女们）「天堂是为你们造的」——那个十英尺高的比利时农夫啐了三口，用脚蹭了蹭，鼻涕直流；那个黑鬼朝一块远远的猩红手帕里射出一口白浓痰——神父的系带散了，他横着螃蟹步蹭下台阶——两支蜡烛扭出一种费劲的柔软。……
黑鬼的事，我会在另一章里告诉你们。
还有一个礼拜日，我看见三个 tiny 的老妇人跌跌撞撞走上前去，三顶很曾经、甚至一度体面的软帽架在三颗皱缩的头颅上，笨拙地扑倒在神父面前，如饥似渴地把圣饼接进她们那皮革似的脸里。

## VII An Approach to the Delectable Mountains / 七 通往悦目山的路
===Original===

> “Sunday (says Mr. Pound with infinite penetration) is a dreadful day,

Monday is much pleasanter.

Then let us muse a little space

Upon fond Nature’s morbid grace.”

===Chinese===

> 「礼拜日（庞德先生以无限的洞察力说）是可怕的一天，

礼拜一可就愉快多了。

那不妨让我们稍稍沉吟片刻，

想想愚顽自然那病态的恩惠。」

===Original===

It is a great and distinct pleasure to have penetrated and arrived upon the outside of *La Dimanche*. We may now﻿—Nature’s morbid grace being a topic whereof the reader has already heard much and will necessarily hear more﻿—turn to the “much pleasanter,” the in fact “Monday,” aspect of La Ferté; by which I mean *les nouveaux* whose arrivals and reactions constituted the actual kinetic aspect of our otherwise merely real Nonexistence. So let us tighten our belts, (everyone used to tighten his belt at least twice a day at La Ferté, but for another reason﻿—to follow and keep track of his surely shrinking anatomy) seize our staffs into our hands, and continue the ascent begun with the first pages of the story.
One day I found myself expecting *La Soupe* Number 1 with something like avidity. My appetite faded, however, upon perceiving a vision en route to the empty place at my left. It slightly resembled a tall youth not more than sixteen or seventeen years old, having flaxen hair, a face whose whiteness I have never seen equalled, and an expression of intense starvation which might have been well enough in a human being but was somewhat unnecessarily uncanny in a ghost. The ghost, floating and slenderly, made for the place beside me, seated himself suddenly and gently like a morsel of white wind, and regarded the wall before him. *La soupe* arrived. He obtained a plate (after some protest on the part of certain members of our table to whom the advent of a newcomer meant only that everyone would get less for lunch), and after gazing at his portion for a second in apparent wonderment at its size caused it gently and suddenly to disappear. I was no sluggard as a rule, but found myself outclassed by minutes﻿—which, said I to myself, is not to be worried over since ’tis sheer vanity to compete with the supernatural. But (even as I lugged the last spoonful of lukewarm greasy water to my lips) this ghost turned to me for all the world as if I too were a ghost, and remarked softly:
“Will you lend me ten cents? I am going to buy tobacco at the canteen.”
One has no business crossing a spirit, I thought; and produced the sum cheerfully﻿—which sum disappeared, the ghost arose slenderly and soundlessly, and I was left with emptiness beside me.
Later I discovered that this ghost was called Pete.

===Chinese===

已经穿透并抵达礼拜日（*La Dimanche*）的外侧，是一种巨大而分明的愉悦。自然的病态恩惠这个题目，读者已经听得很多、往后还必然听得更多——我们现在可以转向拉费尔泰那「愉快多了」的、实际上是「礼拜一」的一面；我指的是新来者们（*les nouveaux*），他们的到来与反应构成了我们那原本仅仅真实的「不存在」的真正动态面。那么让我们束紧腰带（在拉费尔泰，人人每天至少束两次腰带，但为的是另一个理由——追踪自己那必定在缩水的身体），把拐杖掃到手里，继续自故事第一页开始的攀登。
有一天，我发现自己怀着近乎贪婪的心情期待头一顿汤（*La Soupe*）。然而，一个幻影朝我左手边的空位走来，我的胃口顿时消退了。它隐约像一个高个子青年，不过十六七岁，亚麻色头发，一张白得我从未见过能与之匹敌的脸，一脸极度的饥饿表情——这在活人身上还说得过去，在幽灵身上就有点不必要地瘆人了。那幽灵，飘忽而纤细地走向我旁边的位置，突然而轻柔地坐下，像一缕白色的风，然后端详起面前的墙。汤来了。他弄到一只盘子（我们这一桌有几位对新人光临只意味着人人午饭减量的观点提出了抗议），继而对他的那份数量之巨显然惊讶地凝视了一秒，然后让它温柔地、突然地消失了。我一向不算动作慢的，这回却输了好几分钟——我对自己说，这不值得焦虑，同超自然竞争纯属虚荣。但是（就在我把最后一勺温吞油腻的水拖到唇边时）这幽灵转向我，那样子完全当我也是个幽灵，轻声说：
「借我十个生丁好吗？我要去小卖部买烟。」
跟鬼魂作对是没道理的，我想；于是愉快地掏出了这笔钱——钱消失了，幽灵纤细而无身地站起，我身边只剩下一片空。
后来我发现这幽灵名叫皮特（Pete）。

===Original===

Pete was a Hollander, and therefore found firm and staunch friends in Harree, John o’ the Bathhouse and the other Hollanders. In three days Pete discarded the immateriality which had constituted the exquisite definiteness of his advent, and donned the garb of flesh-and-blood. This change was due equally to *La Soupe* and the canteen, and to the finding of friends. For Pete had been in solitary confinement for three months and had had nothing to eat but bread and water during that time, having been told by the jailors (as he informed us, without a trace of bitterness) that they would shorten his sentence provided he did not partake of *La Soupe* during his incarceration﻿—that is to say, *le gouvernement français* had a little joke at Pete’s expense. Also he had known nobody during that time but the five fingers which deposited said bread and water with conscientious regularity on the ground beside him. Being a Hollander neither of these things killed him﻿—on the contrary, he merely turned into a ghost, thereby fooling the excellent French Government within an inch of its foolable life. He was a very excellent friend of ours﻿—I refer as usual to B. and myself﻿—and from the day of his arrival until the day of his departure to Précigne along with B. and three others I never ceased to like and to admire him. He was naturally sensitive, extremely the antithesis of coarse (which “refined” somehow does not imply) had not in the least suffered from a “good,” as we say, education, and possessed an at once frank and unobstreperous personality. Very little that had happened to Pete’s physique had escaped Pete’s mind. This mind of his quietly and firmly had expanded in proportion as its owner’s trousers had become too big around the waist﻿—altogether not so extraordinary as was the fact that, after being physically transformed as I have never seen a human being transformed by food and friends, Pete thought and acted with exactly the same quietness and firmness as before. He was a rare spirit, and I salute him wherever he is.
Mexique was a good friend of Pete’s, as he was of ours. He had been introduced to us by a man we called One Eyed David, who was married and had a wife downstairs, with which wife he was allowed to live all day﻿—being conducted to and from her society by a *planton*.

===Chinese===

皮特是荷兰人，因此在阿累、澡堂约翰和其他荷兰人那里找到了坚实的朋友。三天之内，皮特裉去了构成他光临之精妙 определeness 的那层非物质性，换上了血肉的行头。这一变化同等地归功于汤大人、小卖部，以及朋友的发现。因为皮特在单独监禁里待了三个月，期间只有面包和水吃；狱卒们告诉他（他这样告诉我们，不带一丝苦涩）——只要他监禁期间不碰汤，就给他减刑——也就是说，法国政府在皮特身上开了个小玩笑。而且那期间除了那五根手指，他谁也不认识；那五根手指以尽职的规律性把面包和水摆在他身旁的地上。作为荷兰人，这两样都没能弄死他——相反，他只是变成了幽灵，从而把那个出色的法国政府愚弄到了它可被愚弄的生命的最后一寸。他是我们极好的朋友——照例指B.和我——从到达之日起，直到他同B.及其他三人一道被解往普雷西涅之日止，我对他的喜爱与钦佩从未间断。他天生敏感，是粗鄙的绝对反义（「refined」不知怎么总含不到这层），全然未曾受过我们所谓「良好」教育的毒害，个性坦率而不张扬。皮特身体上遭遇的一切，几乎无一逃过皮特的头脑。这颗头脑安静而坚定地扩张着，与其主人裤腰的日益宽松恰成比例——这还算不上最不寻常；最不寻常的是：在食物和朋友把我从未见过任何人被如此改造的身体改造之后，皮特的思考与行事，同从前一模一样地安静而坚定。他是罕见的灵魂，无论他在哪里，我向他致敬。
墨西哥（Mexique）是皮特的好朋友，也是我们的好朋友。他是我们从一个我们称为独眼大卫（One Eyed David）的人那里认识的；大卫结了婚，妻子就住在楼下，他被允许整天同妻子厮守——由一名卫兵押送去押送回。

===Original===

He spoke Spanish well and French passably; had black hair, bright Jewish eyes, a dead-fish expression, and a both amiable and courteous disposition. One Eyed Dah-veed (as it was pronounced of course) had been in prison at Noyon during the German occupation, which he described fully and without hyperbole﻿—stating that no one could have been more considerate or just than the commander of the invading troops. Dah-veed had seen with his own eyes a French girl extend an apple to one of the common soldiers as the German army entered the outskirts of the city: “ ‘Take it,’ she said, ‘you are tired.’﻿—‘Madame,’ answered the German soldier in French, ‘thank you’﻿—and he looked in his pocket and found ten cents. ‘No, no,’ the young girl said. ‘I don’t want any money. I give it to you with good will.’﻿—‘Pardon, madame,’ said the soldier, ‘you must know that a German soldier is forbidden to take anything without paying for it.’ ”﻿—And before that, One Eyed Dah-veed had talked at Noyon with a barber whose brother was an aviator with the French Army: “ ‘My brother,’ the barber said to me, ‘told me a beautiful story the other day. He was flying over the lines, and he was amazed, one day, to see that the French guns were not firing on the boches but on the French themselves. He landed precipitously, sprang from his machine and ran to the office of the general. He saluted, and cried in great excitement: “General, you are firing on the French!” The general regarded him without interest, without budging; then, he said, very simply: “They have begun, they must finish.” ’ Which is why perhaps,” said One Eyed Dah-veed, looking two ways at once with his uncorrelated eyes, “the Germans entered Noyon.﻿ ﻿…” But to return to Mexique.
One night we had a soirée, as Dah-veed called it, apropos a pot of hot tea which Dah-veed’s wife had given him to take upstairs, it being damnably damp and cold (as usual) in The Enormous Room. Dah-veed, cautiously and in a low voice, invited us to his mattress to enjoy this extraordinary pleasure; and we accepted, B. and I, with huge joy; and sitting on Dah-veed’s paillasse we found somebody who turned out to be Mexique﻿—to whom, by his right name, our host introduced us with all the poise and courtesy vulgarly associated with a French salon.
For Mexique I cherish and always will cherish unmitigated affection.

===Chinese===

他西班牙语讲得好，法语还过得去；黑头发，明亮的犹太眼睛，一副死鱼表情，性情既和蔼又客气。独眼大卫-维德（Dah-veed，当然这么发音）在德占期间蹲过努瓦永的监狱，他完整而不夸张地描述过——说侵略部队的指挥官再没人能比他更周到更公正了。大卫-维德亲眼见过德军开进城郊时，一个法国姑娘朝一名普通士兵递去一只苹果：「『拿着吧，』她说，『你累了。』——『夫人，』德国士兵用法语回答，『谢谢』——他往口袋里摸，摸出十个生丁。『不，不，』年轻姑娘说，『我不要钱。我是好意给你的。』——『对不起，夫人，』士兵说，『您想必知道，德国士兵不许白拿任何东西。』」——在这之前，独眼大卫-维德还在努瓦永同一位理发师聊过天，理发师的兄弟在法国陆军当飞行员：「『我兄弟，』理发师对我说，『前几天给我讲了个好听的故事。他在前线飞行，有一天惊讶地发现，法军的炮火没打德国佬，倒打在法国人自己身上。他紧急降落，跳下飞机，跑到将军的办公室。敬礼，激动地大喊：「将军，您在朝法国人开炮！」将军毫无兴趣地看着他，纹丝不动；然后他说得很简单：「既然开了头，就得打完。」』所以呀，兴许就是这个缘故，」独眼大卫-维德用他那两只不相干的眼睛朝两个方向同时看着说，「德国人才进了努瓦永。……」但让我们回到墨西哥。
一天晚上，我们搞了一场晚会（soirée，大卫-维德的叫法），起因是一壶热茶，大卫-维德的妻子给他带上楼来的，巨室里（一如往常）湿冷得要命。大卫-维德小心地低声请我们去他床垫上共享这份非常的快乐；B.和我怀着巨大的喜悦接受了；在大卫-维德的草垫上坐定，我们发现那儿还有一位，后来知道是墨西哥——我们的主人以他的本名向我们作了介绍，那风度与客气，俚俗地讲，是法国沙龙专属的。
对墨西哥，我怀着而且将永远怀着毫无折扣的感情。

===Original===

He was perhaps nineteen years old, very chubby, extremely good-natured; and possessed of an unruffled disposition which extended to the most violent and obvious discomforts a subtle and placid illumination. He spoke beautiful Spanish, had been born in Mexico, and was really called Philippe Burgos. He had been in New York. He criticised someone for saying “Yes” to us, one day, stating that no American said “Yes” but “Yuh”; which﻿—whatever the reader may think﻿—is to my mind a very profound observation. In New York he had worked nights as a fireman in some big building or other and slept days, and this method of seeing America he had enjoyed extremely. Mexique had one day taken ship (being curious to see the world) and worked as chauffeur﻿—that is to say in the stokehole. He had landed in, I think, Havre; had missed his ship; had inquired something of a gendarme in French (which he spoke not at all, with the exception of a phrase or two like “*quelle heure qu’il est?*”); had been kindly treated and told that he would be taken to a ship *de suite*﻿—had boarded a train in the company of two or three kind gendarmes, ridden a prodigious distance, got off the train finally with high hopes, walked a little distance, come in sight of the grey perspiring wall of La Ferté, and﻿—“So, I ask one of them: ‘Where is the Ship?’ He point to here and tell me, ‘There is the ship.’ I say: ‘This is a God Dam Funny Ship’ ”﻿—quoth Mexique, laughing.
Mexique played dominoes with us (B. having devised a set from cardboard), strolled The Enormous Room with us, telling of his father and brother in Mexico, of the people, of the customs; and﻿—when we were in the *cour*﻿—wrote the entire conjugation of *tengo* in the deep mud with a little stick, squatting and chuckling and explaining. He and his brother had both participated in the revolution which made Carranza president. His description of which affair was utterly delightful.
“Every-body run a‑round with guns,” Mexique said. “And by-and-by no see to shoot everybody, so everybody go home.” We asked if he had shot anybody himself. “Sure. I shoot everybody I do’no” Mexique answered laughing. “I t’ink every-body no hit me,” he added, regarding his stocky person with great and quiet amusement.

===Chinese===

他约莫十九岁，胖乎乎的，极其和善；拥有一种不为所动的性情，对最剧烈最明显的困苦也报以一种细致而安详的光照。他讲一口漂亮的西班牙语，生在墨西哥，本名菲利佩·布尔戈斯（Philippe Burgos）。他去过纽约。有一天他批评某人对我们说「Yes」，说美国人不说「Yes」而说「Yuh」；这话——不管读者怎么想——在我看来是一非常深刻的观察。在纽约，他在某座大楼里当夜班司炉，白天睡觉，这种看美国的方式他喜欢极了。墨西哥有一天心血来潮想看看世界，上了船——当司机（chauffeur），确切说在锅炉舱。他在（我想是）哈佛尔下的船；误了船；用法语（他一句不会，只有一两句像「几点了？（*quelle heure qu'il est?*）」）向一个宪兵打听什么事；受到了亲切接待，被告知马上（*de suite*）就送他去船边——于是由两三个好心的宪兵陪同上了火车，坐了段惊人的距离，满怀希望地下了车，走了一小段路，望见了拉费尔泰那灰色冒汗的墙，于是——「我就问他们中一个：『船在哪儿？』他朝这儿一指，跟我说：『这就是船。』我说：『这可真是一艘他妈的好笑的船』」——墨西哥笑着说。
墨西哥同我们玩多米诺（B.用纸板做了一副），同我们在巨室里遛弯，讲他墨西哥的父亲和兄弟，讲人民，讲风俗；还有——在院子里的时候——蹲在深泥里用一根小棍写完整个的「我有」（*tengo*）变位，一边蹲一边轻笑一边讲解。他和他兄弟都参加过让卡兰萨当上总统的那场革命。他对那场大事的描述可爱极了。
「每个人都拿——着枪跑——来跑去，」墨西哥说，「后来呢，谁都看不见谁，没法乱枪打死所有人，大家就都回家了。」我们问他本人打没打着谁。「当然。我见谁打谁，认不认——得都打」墨西哥笑着答，「我想啊，大家都没打中我。」他补充道，带着巨大而安详的乐子打量自己矮壮的身板。

===Original===

When we asked him once what he thought about the war, he replied, “I t’ink lotta bull﻿—,” which, upon copious reflection, I decided absolutely expressed my own point of view.
Mexique was generous, incapable of either stupidity or despondency, and mannered as a gentleman is supposed to be. Upon his arrival he wrote almost immediately to the Mexican (or is it Spanish?) consul﻿—“He know my fader in Mexico”﻿—stating in perfect and unambiguous Spanish the facts leading to his arrest; and when I said goodbye to *La Misère* Mexique was expecting a favorable reply at any moment, as indeed he had been cheerfully expecting for some time. If he reads this history I hope he will not be too angry with me for whatever injustice it does to one of the altogether pleasantest companions I have ever had. My notebooks, one in particular, are covered with conjugations which bear witness to Mexique’s ineffable good-nature. I also have a somewhat superficial portrait of his back sitting on a bench by the stove. I wish I had another of Mexique out in *le jardin* with a man who worked there who was a Spaniard, and whom the Surveillant had considerately allowed Mexique to assist; with the perfectly correct idea that it would be pleasant for Mexique to talk to someone who could speak Spanish﻿—if not as well as he, Mexique, could, at least passably well. As it is, I must be content to see my very good friend sitting with his hands in his pockets by the stove with Bill the Hollander beside him. And I hope it was not many days after my departure that Mexique went free. Somehow I feel that he went free﻿ ﻿… and if I am right, I will only say about Mexique’s freedom what I have heard him slowly and placidly say many times concerning not only the troubles which were common property to us all but his own peculiar troubles as well.
“That’s fine.”
Here let me introduce the Guard Champêtre, whose name I have already taken more or less in vain. A little, sharp, hungry-looking person who, subsequent to being a member of a rural police force (of which membership he seemed rather proud), had served his *patrie*﻿—otherwise known as *La Belgique*﻿—in the capacity of motorcyclist. As he carried dispatches from one end of the line to the other his disagreeably big eyes had absorbed certain peculiarly inspiring details of civilised warfare.

===Chinese===

有一次我们问他怎么看这场战争，他答：「我寻思全是扯——淡——」，经反复深思，我认定这绝对表达了我的观点。
墨西哥慷慨，既不会愚蠢也不会沮丧，举止如绅士应有之雅。到达之后他几乎立即给墨西哥（还是西班牙？）领事写了信——「他认得我父亲，在墨西哥」——以完美而无歧义的西班牙语陈述了他被捕的经过；到我向苦难（*La Misère*）道别之时，墨西哥还在时刻期待着回音，事实上他一直乐呵呵地期待了好一阵。如果他读到这段历史，我希望他不要过分生我的气，无论它怎样亏待了我此生最愉快的伙伴之一。我的笔记本，特别是一本，涂满了变位，见证着墨西哥那不可言喻的好脾气。我还有一张相当表面的肖像：他坐在炉边长凳上的背影。我真想再要一张墨西哥在外头菜园（*le jardin*）里的，同在那儿干活的一个西班牙人在一起——监守体谅地准许墨西哥去给他帮忙；监守的想法完全正确：让墨西哥同一个讲西班牙语的人聊聊总是愉快的——纵然比不上他墨西哥本人讲得好，至少也过得去。现在这样，我只得满足于看见我的好友双手插兜坐在炉边，旁边是荷兰人比尔。我希望在我走后没几天墨西哥就自由了。不知怎么我觉得他自由了……如果我没猜错，关于墨西哥的自由我只想说一句，一句我听他缓慢而安详地说过许多遍的、不只针对大家共同的苦难、也针对他自己那些独特苦难的话：
「那挺好。」
这里请让我介绍田野看守（Guard Champêtre），他的名字我已经或多或少白用过几回了。一个小个子、尖刻、饿相的人，先是在某乡村警察队伍里干过（他对此会员资格似乎颇为自豪），后为国效劳（*patrie*，又名比利时〔La Belgique〕），职务是摩托车传令兵。当他把命令从战线一端送到另一端时，他那双大得讨厌的眼睛吸收了文明战争的某些格外鼓舞人心的细节。

===Original===

He had, at one time, seen a bridge hastily constructed by *les alliés* over the Yser River, the cadavers of the faithful and the enemy alike being thrown in helter-skelter to make a much needed foundation for the timbers. This little procedure had considerably outraged the Guard Champêtre’s sense of decency. The Yser, said he, flowed perfectly red for a long time. “We were all together: Belgians, French, English﻿ ﻿… we Belgians did not see any good reason for continuing the battle. But we continued. O indeed we continued. Do you know why?”
I said that I was afraid I didn’t.
“Because in front of us we had the German shells, behind, the French machine guns, always the French machine guns, *mon vieux*.”
“*Je ne comprends pas bien*” I said in confusion, recalling all the highfalutin rigmarole which Americans believed﻿—(little martyred Belgium protected by the allies from the inroads of the aggressor, etc.)﻿—“why should the French put machine guns behind you?”
The Guard Champêtre lifted his big empty eyes nervously. The vast hollows in which they lived darkened. His little rather hard face trembled within itself. I thought for a second he was going to throw a fit at my feet﻿—instead of doing which he replied pettishly, in a sunken bright whisper:
“To keep us going forward. At times a company would drop its guns and turn to run. *Pupupupupupupupup*﻿ ﻿…” his short unlovely arms described gently the swinging of a *mitrailleuse*﻿ ﻿… “finish. The Belgian soldiers to left and right of them took the hint. If they did not﻿—*pupupupupupup*.﻿ ﻿… O we went forward. Yes. *Vive le patriotisme.*”
And he rose with a gesture which seemed to brush away these painful trifles from his memory, crossed the end of the room with short rapid steps, and began talking to his best friend Judas, who was at that moment engaged in training his wobbly mustachios.﻿ ﻿… Toward the close of my visit to La Ferté the Guard Champêtre was really happy for a period of two days﻿—during which time he moved in the society of a rich, intelligent, mistakenly arrested and completely disagreeable youth in bone spectacles, copious hair and spiral putees, whom B. and I partially contented ourselves by naming Jo Jo The Lion Faced Boy.

===Chinese===

他曾亲眼见过协约国（*les alliés*）在伊瑟河上仓促架起的一座桥，忠诚者与敌人的尸体一齐乱扔进去，给桥木做急需的地基。这道小工序大大激怒了田野看守的体面感。他说，伊瑟河红了很久很久。「我们大家都在一块儿：比利时人、法国人、英国人……我们比利时人看不出继续打下去有什么好处。可我们还是打了。哦，我们真打了。您知道为什么吗？」
说我怕是不知道。
「因为我们前面是德国人的炮弹，背后是法国人的机枪，永远是法国人的机枪，老伙计（*mon vieux*）。」
「我不太明白（*Je ne comprends pas bien*）。」我慌乱地说，想起美国人信的那套高调废话（小受难者比利时受盟友保护、免遭侵略者蹂躏，等等）——「法国人为什么要把机枪架在你们背后？」
田野看守紧张地抬起他那双空茫的大眼。它们居住的巨大眼窝暗了下去。他小小而偏硬的脸在自身内部发抖。有一秒钟我以为他要在我脚边发作羊癫疯——结果他没有，只是气冲冲地、以一种陷落而明亮的耳语答道：
「为的是让我们往前冲。有时一个连会扔了枪掉头跑。噗噗噗噗噗噗噗噗……」他那两条短而不讨喜的胳膊温柔地比划着机枪的扫射……「完蛋。左右的比利时士兵就懂了。再不懂——噗噗噗噗噗。……哦，我们冲了。是啊。爱国主义万岁（*Vive le patriotisme*）。」
他站起身，做了个仿佛把这些痛苦的琐屑从记忆里掸掉的手势，迈着短促的快步穿过屋子那头，同他最好的朋友犹大聊了起来，犹大那时正忙着训练他那两撇摇摇欲坠的髭。……在我拉费尔泰之行的尾声，田野看守真的幸福了两天——期间他活动于一位富有、聪明、误捕、且彻头彻尾讨厌的青年组成的社会里；此人戴骨质眼镜，头发浓密，打着螺旋绑腿，B.和我在给他起名「狮面少年乔乔」（Jo Jo The Lion Faced Boy）之中获得了部分满足。

===Original===

Had the charges against Jo Jo been stronger my tale would have been longer﻿—fortunately for tout le monde they had no basis; and back went Jo Jo to his native Paris, leaving the Guard Champêtre with Judas and attacks of only occasionally interesting despair.
The reader may suppose that it is about time another Delectable Mountain appeared upon his horizon. Let him keep his eyes wide open, for here one comes.﻿ ﻿…
Whenever our circle was about to be increased, a bell from somewhere afar (as a matter of fact the gate which had admitted my weary self to La Ferté upon a memorable night, as already has been faithfully recounted) tanged audibly﻿—whereat up jumped the more strenuous inhabitants of The Enormous Room and made pell-mell for the common peephole, situated at the door end or nearer end of our habitat and commanding a somewhat fragmentary view of the gate together with the arrivals, male and female, whom the bell announced. In one particular case the watchers appeared almost unduly excited, shouting “four!”﻿—“big box”﻿—“five gendarmes!” and other incoherences with a loudness which predicted great things. As nearly always, I had declined to participate in the melee; and was still lying comfortably horizontal on my bed (thanking God that it had been well and thoroughly mended by a fellow prisoner whom we called The Frog and Le Coiffeur﻿—a tremendously keen-eyed man with a large drooping moustache, whose boon companion, chiefly on account of his shape and gait, we knew as The Lobster) when the usual noises attendant upon the unlocking of the door began with exceptional violence. I sat up. The door shot open, there was a moment’s pause, a series of grunting remarks uttered by two rather terrible voices; then in came four nouveaux of a decidedly interesting appearance. They entered in two ranks of two each. The front rank was made up of an immensely broad shouldered hipless and consequently triangular man in blue trousers belted with a piece of ordinary rope, plus a thickset ruffianly personage the most prominent part of whose accoutrements were a pair of hideous whiskers. I leaped to my feet and made for the door, thrilled in spite of myself. By the, in this case, shifty blue eyes, the pallid hair, the well-knit form of the rope’s owner I knew instantly a Hollander.

===Chinese===

倘若对乔乔的指控更硬一点，我的故事就会更长——所幸对全人类（tout le monde）而言指控毫无根据；于是乔乔回了他巴黎的老家，留下田野看守同犹大做伴，以及偶尔才有趣的绝望发作。
读者也许觉得，该有另一座悦目山出现在他地平线上了。让他睁大眼睛，这儿就来了一座。……
每当我们的圈子要扩员，远处什么地方（实际上就是某个难忘之夜放我疲惫之身进入拉费尔泰的那道大门，已忠实记述过）就有一口铃铛可闻地铛一响——于是巨室里那些更血性的住户一跃而起，哄抢共用的窥孔；窥孔位于我们居所的门端或近端，能俯瞰大门以及铃铛所宣告的男女来客的一片相当破碎的景观。有一回，望孔者们显得兴奋得几乎过分，喊着「四个！」——「大箱子」——「五个宪兵！」以及种种语无伦次，音量预示着大事。一如往常，我拒绝参与混战；还舒舒服服横躺在床上（感谢上帝，我的床已被一位我们称为青蛙（The Frog）兼理发师（Le Coiffeur）的难友修得又好又透——一个眼力极毒、留着大八字胡的男人，他的酒友，主要因体型和步态，我们称之为龙虾〔The Lobster〕），这时伴随开门的那些惯常响动以异常的暴力开始了。我坐起来。门弹开，片刻停顿，两道相当可怕的嗓音咕哝了几句；随后进来了四个外貌绝对有趣的新人。他们分两列进入，每列两人。前列由一个肩膀极宽、无胯、因而呈三角形的男人组成，蓝裤子，腰里系一截普通麻绳；外加一个矮壮的恶棍相人物，其装备最显眼的部分是一部可怕的络腮胡。我一跃而起奔向门口，身不由己地激动。凭这回那对游移的蓝眼睛、苍白的头发、绳子主人那副结实的身板，我即刻认出是荷兰人。

===Original===

By the coarse brutal features half-hidden in the piratical whiskers, as well as by the heavy mean wandering eyes, I recognised with equal speed a Belgian. Upon his shoulders the front rank bore a large box, blackish, well-made, obviously very weighty, which box it set down with a grunt of relief hard by the cabinet.
