The Wooden Hand even went so far as to escort our seditious minds to the nearness of their examination by the simple yet efficient method of placing one of his human fingers opposite the name of him who was (even at that moment) within, submitting to the inexorable justice of *le gouvernement français*. I cannot honestly say that the discovery of this proximity of ourselves to our respective fates wholly pleased us; yet we were so weary of waiting that it certainly did not wholly terrify us. All in all, I think I have never been so utterly un-at-ease as while waiting for the axe to fall, metaphorically speaking, upon our squawking heads.
We were still conversing with the Belgian girl when a man came out of the door unsteadily, looking as if he had submitted to several strenuous fittings of a wooden leg upon a stump not quite healed. The Wooden Hand, nodding at B., remarked hurriedly in a low voice:
“*Allez!*”
And B. (smiling at La Belge and at me) entered. He was followed by The Wooden Hand, as I suppose for greater security.

===Chinese===

这个比利时姑娘（La Belge）告诉我们，她是拉费尔泰的常驻居民，她和另一个正经女人（femme honnête）单独住一间屋，她的兄弟们正在比利时的前线打仗，她能流利地讲几种语言（包括英语和德语），这使她成了委员会诸位先生（Messieurs la commission）跟前少不了的人，她没犯过罪，她是作为嫌疑犯（suspecte）被关押的，她并非完全不幸福。她立刻给我一个印象：不但聪明，而且鲜活。她用地道的英语问我们犯了什么案，发现我们——就一切表象而言——清白无辜，似乎非常高兴。
我们压低嗓门的交谈不时被打断，打断者是和气的木手。有两次那扇门砰地甩开，所长先生弹跳出来，嘴上冒着白沫，用无穷无尽的禁闭室（cabinot）威胁所有人，理由是大家的举止（或曰无举止）正在危及委员们的沉着。每回，黑枪套（The Black Holster）都会在背景里现身，接替主子的威吓，直到人人彻底吓破了胆——过后大家又被交还给自己和木手。
木手——那一天他至少算得上个人物，不单是个 planton——允许 B. 和我从他肩膀后面偷看男人们的名单。木手甚至更进一步，用简单而高效的方法，把我们这些忤逆的心思一路护送到各自受审的临近处：把一根肉指头按在某个名字对面，此人（就在那一刻）正在里面，领受法国政府（le gouvernement français）不容分说的公正。我不能老实说，发现自己如此接近各自的命运让我们满心欢喜；可我们实在等得疲惫不堪，所以它也确实没有让我们全然恐惧。总而言之，我想我从来没有像那样浑身不自在过——比喻地说，正等着斧头落在我们咯咯乱叫的脑袋上。
我们还在同比利时姑娘说着话，这时一个男人踉踉跄跄地从门里出来了，那模样像是刚在一条未愈的残腿上试装了几回木腿。木手朝 B. 点点头，匆忙地低声说：
「去（Allez!）」
于是 B.（朝比利时姑娘和我微笑着）进去了。木手跟在他后面，我想是为了更加保险。

===Original===

The next twenty minutes, or whatever it was, were by far the most nerve-racking which I had as yet experienced. La Belge said to me:
“*Il est gentil, votre ami*,”
and I agreed. And my blood was bombarding the roots of my toes and the summits of my hair.
After (I need not say) two or three million aeons, B. emerged. I had not time to exchange a look with him﻿—let alone a word﻿—for the Wooden Hand said from the doorway:
“*Allez, l’autre américain*,”
and I entered in more confusion than can easily be imagined; entered the torture chamber, entered the inquisition, entered the tentacles of that sly and beaming polyp, *le gouvernement français*.﻿ ﻿…
As I entered I said, half aloud: The thing is this, to look ’em in the eyes and keep cool whatever happens, not for the fraction of a moment forgetting that they are made of *merde*, that they are all of them composed entirely of *merde*﻿—I don’t know how many inquisitors I expected to see; but I guess I was ready for at least fifteen, among them President Poincaré Lui-même. I hummed noiselessly:

> “si vous passez par ma vil‑le

n’oubliez pas ma maison;

on y mang‑e de bonne sou‑pe Ton Ton Tay‑ne;

faite de merde et les onions, Ton Ton Tayne Ton Ton Ton,”

===Chinese===

接下来的二十分钟，或者不管多久，是我迄今经历过最折磨神经的时刻。比利时姑娘对我说：
「他人很好，您的朋友（Il est gentil, votre ami）」
我表示同意。而我的血液正轰击着我脚趾的根和头发的梢。
在（不必说的）两三百万个纪元之后，B. 出来了。我来不及同他交换一个眼色——更别提一句话——因为木手在门口说了：
「去，另一个美国人（Allez, l'autre américain）」
于是我进去了，其慌乱之甚非笔墨所能轻易形容；走进了刑讯室，走进了宗教裁判所，走进了那阴险而满脸堆笑的珊瑚虫——法国政府——的触手。……
进门时我半出声地对自己说：要诀是这个，盯住他们的眼睛，无论发生什么都保持冷静，一秒钟也不许忘记他们是 merde 做的，他们全体是由纯而又纯的 merde 构成的——我不知道自己预想会见到多少个审判官；但估计至少预备了十五个，其中还有庞加莱总统（President Poincaré）他老人家（Lui-même）。我无声地哼着：

> 「si vous passez par ma vil-le（如果你路过我的城，）

> n'oubliez pas ma maison;（可别忘了我的家；）

> on y mang-e de bonne sou-pe Ton Ton Tay-ne;（那儿有上好的汤喝，通通泰纳；）

> faite de merde et les onions, Ton Ton Tayne Ton Ton Ton,」（拿屎和洋葱做的，通通泰纳通通通）

===Original===

remembering the fine *forgeron* of Chevancourt who used to sing this, or something very like it, upon a table﻿—entirely for the benefit of *les deux américains*, who would subsequently render “Eats uh lonje wae to Tee-pear-raer-ee,” wholly for the gratification of a roomful of what Mr. Anderson liked to call “them bastards,” alias “dirty” Frenchmen, alias *les poilus, les poilus divins*.﻿ ﻿…
A little room. The Directeur’s office? Or The Surveillant’s? Comfort. O yes, very, very comfortable. On my right a table. At the table three persons. Reminds me of Noyon a bit, not unpleasantly of course. Three persons: reading from left to right as I face them﻿—a soggy, sleepy, slumpy lump in a gendarme’s cape and cap, quite old, captain of gendarmes, not at all interested, wrinkled coarse face, only semi-*méchant*, large hard clumsy hands, floppingly disposed on table; wily tidy man in civilian clothes, pen in hand, obviously lawyer, *avocat* type, little bald on top, sneaky civility, smells of bad perfume or, at any rate, sweetish soap; tiny redheaded person, also civilian, creased worrying excited face, amusing little body and hands, brief and jumpy, must be a Dickens character, ought to spend his time sailing kites of his own construction over other people’s houses in gusty weather. Behind the Three, all tied up with deference and inferiority, mild and spineless, Apollyon.
Would the reader like to know what I was asked?
Ah, would I could say! Only dimly do I remember those moments﻿—only dimly do I remember looking through the lawyer at Apollyon’s clean collar﻿—only dimly do I remember the gradual collapse of the captain of gendarmes, his slow but sure assumption of sleepfulness, the drooping of his soggy *tête de cochon* lower and lower till it encountered one hand whose elbow, braced firmly upon the table, sustained its insensate limpness﻿—only dimly do I remember the enthusiastic antics of the little redhead when I spoke with patriotic fervour of the wrongs which La France was doing *mon ami et moi*﻿—only dimly do I remember, to my right, the immobility of The Wooden Hand, reminding one of a clothing dummy, or a life-size doll which might be made to move only by him who knew the proper combination.﻿ ﻿… At the outset I was asked: Did I want a translator? I looked and saw the *secrétaire*, weak-eyed and lemon-pale, and I said “*Non.

===Chinese===

想起了谢旺库尔那位出色的铁匠（forgeron），他惯于站到一张桌子上唱这首歌，或非常像它的什么歌——完全是为了两位美国人（les deux américains）的益处；随后两位会回敬一曲《回提帕雷利的路可长着呢》（Eats uh lonje wae to Tee-pear-raer-ee），全然为了博满屋子安德森先生喜欢称之为「那些杂种」又名「肮脏的」法国人又名 les poilus、神圣的 poilus 们一笑。……
一个小房间。所长的办公室？还是监督的？舒服。哦是的，非常、非常舒服。我右手边一张桌子。桌旁三个人。让我有点想起努瓦永（Noyon），当然想起得并不难受。三个人：面对着他们从左往右读——一摊湿软、瞌睡、瘫垮的东西，穿宪兵斗篷、戴宪兵帽，年纪相当大，宪兵上尉，全无兴趣，脸粗糙多皱，只是半分凶恶（méchant），一双粗大僵硬笨拙的手，瘫摊在桌上；一个精明整洁的男人，穿便服，手执钢笔，显然是律师，讼师（avocat）类型，头顶微秃，谄媚里透着鬼祟，散发着劣质香水的气味，或者至少是发甜的肥皂味；一个小个子的红头发男人，也是便服，一张皱巴巴、忧心忡忡而亢奋的脸，身板和双手都逗人，短促而一惊一乍，准是狄更斯笔下跑出来的人物，本该把时光用来在刮风的天气里放他自己扎的风筝，放到别人家的屋顶上去。在这三位身后，被恭敬与卑微捆成一团，温和而没有脊梁的，是亚玻伦（Apollyon）。
读者想知道他们问了我什么吗？
唉，但愿我说得出来！那些时刻我只模糊记得——只模糊记得我透过律师望着亚玻伦的干净领子——只模糊记得宪兵上尉逐渐垮塌，缓慢却确凿地沉入睡意，他那颗湿软的猪头（tête de cochon）越垂越低，直到落在一只手上，那手的肘部牢牢撑在桌上，托住它无知觉的瘫软——只模糊记得，当我以爱国的热忱谈起法兰西对我朋友和我（mon ami et moi）所行的不义时，小个子红头发那热心的手舞足蹈——只模糊记得我右边的木手一动不动，让人想起服装店的假人，或一具真人大小、只有知道正确密码组合的人才拨得动的玩偶。…… 一开场他们问我：要不要翻译？我看了看那位秘书（secrétaire），眼神软弱，脸色柠檬，我说「不（Non.

===Original===

*” I was questioned mostly by the *avocat*, somewhat by the Dickens, never by either the captain (who was asleep) or the Directeur (who was timid in the presence of these great and good delegates of hope, faith and charity per the French Government). I recall that, for some reason, I was perfectly cool. I put over six or eight hot shots without losing in the least this composure, which surprised myself and pleased myself and altogether increased myself. As the questions came for me I met them halfway, spouting my best or worst French in a manner which positively astonished the tiny redheaded demigod. I challenged with my eyes and with my voice and with my manner Apollyon Himself, and Apollyon Himself merely cuddled together, depressing his hairy body between its limbs as a spider sometimes does in the presence of danger. I expressed immense gratitude to my captors and to *le gouvernement français* for allowing me to see and hear and taste and smell and touch the things which inhabited La Ferté Macé, Orne, France. I do not think that *la commission* enjoyed me much. It told me, through its sweetish-soap leader, that my friend was a criminal﻿—this immediately upon my entering﻿—and I told it with a great deal of well-chosen politeness that I disagreed. In telling how and why I disagreed I think I managed to shove my shovel-shaped imagination under the refuse of their intellects. At least once or twice.
Rather fatiguing﻿—to stand up and be told: Your friend is no good; have you anything to say for yourself?﻿—And to say a great deal for yourself and for your friend and for *les hommes*﻿—or try your best to﻿—and be contradicted, and be told “Never mind that, what we wish to know is,” and instructed to keep to the subject, et cetera, *ad infinitum*. At last they asked each other if each other wanted to ask the man before each other anything more, and each other not wanting to do so, they said:
“*C’est fini.*”
As at Noyon, I had made an indisputably favourable impression upon exactly one of my three examiners. I refer, in the present case, to the redheaded little gentleman who was rather decent to me. I do not exactly salute him in recognition of this decency; I bow to him, as I might bow to somebody who said he was sorry he couldn’t give me a match, but there was a cigar store just around the corner, you know.

===Chinese===

）」问话主要出自讼师之口，狄更斯偶尔插几句，宪兵上尉（睡着呢）和所长（在这些希望、信仰与慈善的伟大善良的委员代表面前畏畏缩缩）则一言不发。我记得，出于某种原因，我彻底地冷静。我放出了六到八发重磅炮弹，镇静丝毫未失，这镇静让我自己吃惊、让自己得意、总之让自己膨胀。问题向我递来，我半路迎击，用我最好或最坏的法语滔滔不绝，那种架势着实让小小的红头发半神吃了一惊。我用眼睛、嗓音和气度挑战亚玻伦他本人，而亚玻伦他本人只是蜷缩起来，把毛茸茸的身子压进肢体中间，像蜘蛛临着危险时有时做的那样。我向俘虏我的人、向法国政府表达巨大的感激，感谢他们允许我看见、听见、品尝、嗅到、触摸栖居于法国奥恩省（Orne）拉费尔泰马塞的万物。我认为 la commission 不怎么欣赏我。它通过它那位发甜肥皂味的首领告诉我，我的朋友是罪犯——这话在我一进门时就说——而我用大量措辞得体的礼貌告诉它，我不敢苟同。在讲述我如何以及为何不敢苟同时，我想我成功地把我的铲形想象力，铲进了他们智识的垃圾底下。至少有一两次。
相当累人——站在那里听人训话：你的朋友不是好东西；你有什么要为自己申辩的？——然后为自己、为朋友、为男人们（les hommes）申辩上一大通——或者尽力而为——却被驳回，被教导「别扯那个，我们想知道的是」，被勒令紧扣正题，等等，ad infinitum（没完没了）。终于他们彼此询问，彼此是否还想向彼此面前的这个人再问点什么，彼此不想，于是他们说：
「完了（C'est fini.）」
一如在努瓦永，我在三位考官中恰好给恰好一位留下了无可争议的好印象。此处我指的便是那位红头发小绅士，他待我相当不坏。我并不完全为此向他致敬；我只是向他鞠一躬，如同我可能向某个说「抱歉，没法借您火柴，不过街角就有一家雪茄店，您知道的」的人鞠躬那样。

===Original===

At “*C’est fini*” the Directeur leaped into the limelight with a savage admonition to the Wooden Hand﻿—who saluted, opened the door suddenly, and looked at me with (dare I say it?) admiration. Instead of availing myself of this means of escape I turned to the little kite-flying gentleman and said:
“If you please, sir, will you be so good as to tell me what will become of my friend?”
The little kite-flying gentleman did not have time to reply, for the perfumed presence stated dryly and distinctly:
“We cannot say anything to you upon that point.”
I gave him a pleasant smile, which said, If I could see your intestines very slowly embracing a large wooden drum rotated by means of a small iron crank turned gently and softly by myself, I should be extraordinarily happy﻿—and I bowed softly and gently to Monsieur le Directeur, and I went through the door using all the perpendicular inches which God had given me.
Once outside I began to tremble like a *peuplier* in *l’automne*.﻿ ﻿… “*L’automne humide et monotone.*”
—“*Allez en bas, pour la soupe*” the Wooden Hand said not unkindly. I looked about me. “There will be no more men before the commission until tomorrow,” the Wooden Hand said. “Go get your dinner in the kitchen.”
I descended.
Afrique was all curiosity﻿—what did they say? what did I say?﻿—as he placed before me a huge, a perfectly huge, an inexcusably huge plate of something more than lukewarm grease.﻿ ﻿… B. and I ate at a very little table in *la cuisine*, excitedly comparing notes as we swallowed the red-hot stuff.﻿ ﻿… “*Du pain; prenez, mes amis*,” Afrique said. “*Mangez comme vous voulez*” the Cook quoth benignantly, with a glance at us over his placid shoulder.﻿ ﻿… Eat we most surely did. We could have eaten the French Government.
The morning of the following day we went on promenade once more. It was neither pleasant nor unpleasant to promenade in the *cour* while somebody else was suffering in the Room of Sorrow. It was, in fact, rather thrilling.
The afternoon of this day we were all up in The Enormous Room when *la commission* suddenly entered with Apollyon strutting and lisping behind it, explaining, and poo-poohing, and graciously waving his thick wicked arms.

===Chinese===

听到「完了（C'est fini）」，所长一跃而起抢进聚光灯，对木手发出一声凶狠的训示——木手敬了个礼，猛地拉开门，用（我敢说吗？）钦佩的目光看了我一眼。我没有利用这个脱身之径，反而转向那位小小的放风筝的绅士，说：
「对不起，先生，可否劳驾告诉我，我的朋友将会怎样？」
放风筝的小绅士来不及回答，因为那位喷香水的人物干巴巴而清晰地说道：
「在这一点上，我们无可奉告。」
我给了他一个愉快的微笑，那微笑说：倘若我能看见你的肠子缓缓缠上一只大木鼓，鼓由一架小铁曲柄带动，曲柄由我本人轻轻地、柔柔地摇着，我会快乐到无以复加——随后我温软地向所长先生鞠了一躬，用上帝赋予我的每一英吋身高，走出了那道门。
一出门我就开始发抖，像秋天（l'automne）里的一株杨树（peuplier）。……「潮湿而单调的秋天（l'automne humide et monotone）。」
——「下去吧，喝汤（Allez en bas, pour la soupe）」木手说得不算不客气。我环顾四周。「明天之前不会再有人过委员会了，」木手说，「去厨房吃你的晚饭吧。」
我下了楼。
阿弗里克（Afrique）浑身是好奇——他们说了什么？我说了什么？——他把一只硕大的、硕大无朋的、大得不可原谅的盘子摆到我面前，盘里是比温吞更进一步的油脂。……B. 和我在厨房（la cuisine）里一张很小的桌上吃饭，一边往喉咙里灌那滚烫的东西，一边兴奋地交换情报。……「面包（Du pain）；拿着吧，我的朋友们（prenez, mes amis）」阿弗里克说。「想吃多少吃多少（Mangez comme vous voulez）」厨子慈祥地吟道，从他平静的肩膀上方瞥了我们一眼。……我们当然大吃特吃。我们本可以把法国政府都吃了。
第二天上午我们照常放风。在 cour 里散步，而别人正在忧伤之屋里受罪，说不上愉快，也谈不上不愉快。事实上，相当刺激。
这天下午我们都在巨大的房间里楼上，la commission 突然进来了，亚玻伦在它身后昂首阔步，咬着舌头解释着、贬抑着、宽厚地挥舞他那双邪恶的粗胳膊。

===Original===

Everyone in The Enormous Room leaped to his feet, removing as he did so his hat﻿—with the exception of *les deux américains*, who kept theirs on, and The Zulu, who couldn’t find his hat and had been trying for some time to stalk it to its lair. *La commission* reacted interestingly to the Enormous Room: the captain of gendarmes looked soggily around and saw nothing with a good deal of contempt; the scented soap squinted up his face and said, “Faugh!” or whatever a French bourgeois *avocat* says in the presence of a bad smell (*la commission* was standing by the door and consequently close to the *cabinet*); but the little redhead kite-flying gentleman looked actually horrified.
“Is there in the room anyone of Austrian nationality?”
The Silent Man stepped forward quietly.
“Why are you here?”
“I don’t know,” The Silent Man said, with tears in his eyes.
“**Nonsense!** You’re here for a very good reason and you know what it is and you could tell it if you wished, you imbecile, you incorrigible, you criminal,” Apollyon shouted; then, turning to the *avocat* and the redheaded little gentleman, “He is a dangerous alien, he admits it, he has admitted it﻿—**Don’t you admit it, eh? Eh?**” he roared at The Silent Man, who fingered his black cap without raising his eyes or changing in the least the simple and supreme dignity of his poise. “He is incorrigible,” said (in a low snarl) The Directeur. “Let us go, gentlemen, when you have seen enough.” But the redheaded man, as I recollect, was contemplating the floor by the door, where six pails of urine solemnly stood, three of them having overflowed slightly from time to time upon the reeking planks.﻿ ﻿… And The Directeur was told that *les hommes* should have a tin trough to urinate into, for the sake of sanitation; and that this trough should be immediately installed, installed without delay﻿—“O yes, indeed, sirs,” Apollyon simpered, “a very good suggestion; it shall be done immediately: yes, indeed. Do let me show you the﻿—it’s just outside﻿—” and he bowed them out with no little skill. And the door **slammed** behind Apollyon and the Three Wise Men.
This, as I say, must have occurred toward the last of November.
For a week we waited.

===Chinese===

巨大的房间里人人都一跃而起，起身的同时摘下帽子——除了两位美国人，他们照旧戴着；还有祖鲁人（The Zulu），他找不到自己的帽子，已经花了好一阵子跟踪它、直捣它的巢穴。la commission 对巨大的房间的反应颇有趣味：宪兵上尉湿塌塌地四下张望，满含轻蔑地什么也没看见；香皂那位把脸皱起来说了声「呸！」或随便哪个法国资产阶级讼师闻到恶臭时说的那个字眼（la commission 正站在门边，因而离马桶（cabinet）很近）；而小小的红头发放风筝绅士，看上去是真的吓坏了。
「屋里有奥地利国籍的人吗？」
沉默的人（The Silent Man）静静地走上前。
「你为什么在这儿？」
「我不知道，」沉默的人说，眼里含着泪。
「胡说！你在这儿有十足的理由，你自己知道是什么，你要是愿意就说得出来，你这白痴，你这无可救药的，你这罪犯」亚玻伦吼道；随即转向讼师和红头发小绅士：「他是个危险的外国人，他承认了，他已经承认了——你不是承认了吗，嗯？嗯？」他冲沉默的人咆哮，后者捻着自己的黑帽子，眼睛不抬，那副质朴而至尊的尊严姿态分毫不变。「他无可救药，」所长（低声咆哮着）说。「先生们，看够了我们就走吧。」但红头发那人，如果我没记错，正盯着门边的地板，那里肃立着六桶尿，其中三桶不时漫出一点，淌在发臭的木板上。……于是有人告诫所长，男人们应该有一只锡制小便槽，为了卫生起见；这槽子应当立即安装，不得延误——「哦是的，确实，先生们」亚玻伦傻笑道，「好极了的建议；马上就办：是的，确实。务必让我带诸位去看看那个——就在外面——」他以不小的手段鞠躬送客。门在亚玻伦和三博士（Three Wise Men）身后砰地关上。
这一切，如我所说，想必发生在十一月末。
我们等了一个星期。

===Original===

Fritz, having waited months for a letter from the Danish consul in reply to the letters which he, Fritz, wrote every so often and sent through *le bureau*﻿—meaning the *sécrétaire*﻿—had managed to get news of his whereabouts to said consul by unlawful means; and was immediately, upon reception of this news by the consul, set free and invited to join a ship at the nearest port. His departure (than which a more joyous I have never witnessed) has been already mentioned in connection with the third Delectable Mountain, as has been the departure for Précigne of Pom Pom and Harree ensemble. Bill the Hollander, Monsieur Pet-airs, Mexique, The Wanderer, the little Machine-Fixer, Pete, Jean le Nègre, The Zulu and Monsieur Auguste (second time) were some of our remaining friends who passed the commission with us. Along with ourselves and these fine people were judged gentlemen like the Trick Raincoat and the Fighting Sheeney. One would think, possibly, that Justice﻿—in the guise of the Three Wise Men﻿—would have decreed different fates, to (say) The Wanderer and The Fighting Sheeney. *Au contraire.* As I have previously remarked, the ways of God and of the good and great French Government are alike inscrutable.
Bill the Hollander, whom we had grown to like, whereas at first we were inclined to fear him, Bill the Hollander who washed some towels and handkerchiefs and what-nots for us and turned them a bright pink, Bill the Hollander who had tried so hard to teach The Young Pole the lesson which he could only learn from The Fighting Sheeney, left us about a week after *la commission*. As I understand it, they decided to send him back to Holland under guard in order that he might be jailed in his native land as a deserter. It is beautiful to consider the unselfishness of *le gouvernement français* in this case. Much as *le gouvernement français* would have liked to have punished Bill on its own account and for its own enjoyment, it gave him up﻿—with a Christian smile﻿—to the punishing clutches of a sister or brother government: without a murmur denying itself the incense of his sufferings and the music of his sorrows. Then too it is really inspiring to note the perfect collaboration of *la justice française* and *la justice hollandaise* in a critical moment of the world’s history.

===Chinese===

弗里茨（Fritz）等丹麦领事的回信等了几个月——他弗里茨隔三差五写信，经由办公处（le bureau）——也就是秘书——寄出——后来设法用非法途径，把自己下落的消息送到了那位领事手上；领事一收到消息，他随即获释，并受邀到最近的港口登上一条船。他的离去（我平生所目睹的最欢天喜地的一次）已在第三座快乐山（Delectable Mountains）那里提过，蓬蓬（Pom Pom）和哈里联袂启程去普雷西涅（Précigne）也一样。荷兰人比尔（Bill the Hollander）、佩特埃尔斯先生（Monsieur Pet-airs）、墨西哥人（Mexique）、流浪者（The Wanderer）、小小的机器修理工（the little Machine-Fixer）、皮特、让·勒内格尔、祖鲁人和奥古斯特先生（Monsieur Auguste，二进宫）是同我们一起过委员会的、我们余下朋友中的一部分。同我们自己以及这些好人一道受审的，还有花哨雨衣（The Trick Raincoat）和打拳的希尼（The Fighting Sheeney）一类的绅士。人们或许会想，正义——以三博士的形貌——总该给（比如说）流浪者和打拳的希尼判下不同的命运吧。恰恰相反（Au contraire.）。如我从前说过的，上帝的道路与善良而伟大的法国政府的道路，同样不可测度。
荷兰人比尔——我们已经渐渐喜欢上他，虽然起初我们倾向于怕他——荷兰人比尔，他帮我们洗了些毛巾、手绢和零七八碎，把它们洗成了鲜艳的粉红色——荷兰人比尔，他曾那么努力地想教会小波兰人（The Young Pole）那一课，而那一课只有从打拳的希尼那里才学得到——在 la commission 之后大约一星期离开了我们。据我理解，他们决定把他押解回荷兰，好让他在自己的祖国以逃兵的罪名坐牢。想一想法国政府在这桩案子里的无私，真是美不胜言。尽管法国政府非常乐意亲自惩罚比尔、亲自享用，它却把他放弃了——带着一个基督徒的微笑——交进姊妹或兄弟政府的惩罚魔掌：毫无怨言地舍弃了他苦难的香烟与悲愁的音乐。还有，在世界历史的关键时刻，法兰西司法（la justice française）与荷兰司法（la justice hollandaise）的完美协作，也着实令人振奋。

===Original===

Bill certainly should feel that it was a great honour to be allowed to exemplify this wonderful accord, this exquisite mutual understanding, between the punitive departments of two nations superficially somewhat unrelated﻿—that is, as regards customs and language. I fear Bill didn’t appreciate the intrinsic usefulness of his destiny. I seem to remember that he left in a rather *Gottverdummerish* condition. Such is ignorance.
Poor Monsieur Pet-airs came out of the commission looking extraordinarily *épaté*. Questioned, he averred that his penchant for inventing force-pumps had prejudiced *ces messieurs* in his disfavour; and shook his poor old head and sniffed hopelessly. Mexique exited in a placidly cheerful condition, shrugging his shoulders and remarking:
“I no do nut’ing. Dese fellers tell me wait few days, after you go free,” whereas Pete looked white and determined and said little﻿—except in Dutch to the Young Skipper and his mate; which pair took *la commission* more or less as a healthy bull calf takes nourishment: there was little doubt that they would refind *la liberté* in a short while, judging from the inability of the Three Wise Men to prove them even suspicious characters. The Zulu uttered a few inscrutable gestures made entirely of silence and said he would like us to celebrate the accomplishment of this ordeal by buying ourselves and himself a good fat cheese apiece﻿—his friend The Young Pole looked as if the ordeal had scared the life out of him temporarily; he was unable to say whether or no he and “*mon ami*” would leave us: *la commission* had adopted, in the case of these twain, an awe-inspiring taciturnity. Jean Le Nègre, who was one of the last to pass, had had a tremendously exciting time, due to the fact that *le gouvernement français’s* polished tools had failed to scratch his mystery either in French or English﻿—he came dancing and singing toward us; then, suddenly suppressing every vestige of emotion, solemnly extended for our approval a small scrap of paper on which was written:

> **Calais**,

===Chinese===

比尔当然应该感到，能让他亲身示范两国惩罚部门之间这种美妙的和谐、这种精致的相互理解，是无上的光荣——两国就表面而言（也就是就风俗与语言而言）并无多少干系。我怕比尔并没有领会他命运的内在价值。我似乎记得，他走的时候处于一种相当 Gottverdummerish（天杀的）的状态。无知即是如此。
可怜的佩特埃尔斯先生从委员会里出来，模样格外地挨了闷棍（épaté）。被问起时，他断言说，他发明压力泵的癖好让这几位先生（ces messieurs）对他先入为主地生了恶感；他摇着他那可怜的老脑袋，绝望地抽着鼻子。墨西哥人出来时心境平和而愉快，耸耸肩说：
「我没干什么（I no do nut'ing）。这帮家伙叫我等几天，以后你就自由了（Dese fellers tell me wait few days, after you go free）」而皮特脸色发白，神色决绝，话不多——除了用荷兰语对少年船长（the Young Skipper）和他的伙伴说了几句；这一对过 la commission，差不多像一头健壮的小公牛犊进食：毫无疑问他们很快就会重新找到自由（la liberté），从三博士连证明他们是可疑分子都办不到这一点来看。祖鲁人做了几个全然由沉默构成的莫测手势，说我们应当庆祝这场考验的完成，方式是给我们自己和他一人买一大块好奶酪——他的朋友小波兰人看上去像被这场考验暂时吓掉了半条命；他说不上他和「我的朋友（mon ami）」会不会离开我们：la commission 对这二位采取了令人敬畏的缄默。让·勒内格尔是最后几个过堂的之一，他过得极其刺激，因为法国政府的精良工具们无论用法语还是英语，都没能刮伤他的神秘——他连唱带跳地朝我们而来；随即突然收起每一丝感情的痕迹，郑重其事地递给我们一张小纸片请求鉴定，上面写着：

> **加来（Calais）**

===Original===

remarking: “*Qu‑est‑ce que ça veut dire?*”﻿—and when we read the word for him, “*m’en vais à Calais, moi, travailler à Calais, très bon!*”﻿—with a jump and a shout of laughter pocketing the scrap and beginning the Song of Songs:
“*après la guerre finit.﻿ ﻿…*”
A trio which had been hit and hard hit by the Three Wise Men were, or was, The Wanderer and the Machine-Fixer and Monsieur Auguste﻿—the former having been insulted in respect to Chocolat’s mother (who also occupied the witness-stand) and having retaliated, as nearly as we could discover, with a few remarks straight from the shoulder apropos Justice (O Wanderer, did you expect honour among the honourable?); the Machine-Fixer having been told to shut up in the midst of a passionate plea for mercy, or at least fair-play, if not in his own case in the case of the wife who was crazed by his absence; Monsieur Auguste having been asked (as he had been asked three months before by the honorable commissioners), Why did you not return to Russia with your wife and your child at the outbreak of the war?﻿—and having replied, with tears in his eyes and that gentle ferocity of which he was occasionally capable:
“Be-cause I didn’t have the means. I am not a mil‑lion‑aire, Sirs.”
The Baby-Snatcher, the Trick Raincoat, the Messenger Boy, the Fighting Sheeney and similar gentry passed the commission without the slightest apparent effect upon their disagreeable personalities.
It was not long after Bill the Hollander’s departure that we lost two Delectable Mountains in The Wanderer and Surplice. Remained The Zulu and Jean le Nègre.﻿ ﻿… B. and I spent most of our time when on promenade collecting rather beautifully hued leaves in *la cour*. These leaves we inserted in one of my notebooks, along with all the colours which we could find on cigarette boxes, chocolate wrappers, labels of various sorts and even postage stamps. (We got a very brilliant red from a certain piece of cloth.) Our efforts puzzled everyone (including the *plantons*) more than considerably; which was natural, considering that everyone did not know that by this exceedingly simple means we were effecting a study of colour itself, in relation to what is popularly called “abstract” and sometimes “non-representative” painting.

===Chinese===

并说：「这是什么意思（Qu-est-ce que ça veut dire?）」——当我们把这个词念给他听，「我去加来，我，去加来干活，好得很（m'en vais à Calais, moi, travailler à Calais, très bon!）」——他一蹦老高，大笑一声，把纸片揣进口袋，唱起了那支歌中之歌：
「等打完了仗（après la guerre finit.）……」
被三博士结结实实敲了一记的三人组，是流浪者、机器修理工和奥古斯特先生——头一位因为巧克力的母亲（她也列席证人席）而受了侮辱，并以——就我们所能探知的——几句关于正义的直来直去的话还了嘴（哦流浪者，你难道指望高尚者中间还有高尚吗？）；机器修理工在为宽免、或至少为公平竞赛的热切哀求中途被喝令闭嘴——纵使不为他自己，也为那位因他的离开而发疯的妻子；奥古斯特先生被问到（正如三个月前尊敬的委员们问过的那样）：战争爆发时，你为何不带着妻儿回俄国去？——他答道，眼里含着泪，带着他偶尔能够有的那种温和的凶猛：
「因——因为我没有那个财力。我不是百万富翁，先生们（Be-cause I didn't have the means. I am not a mil-lion-aire, Sirs.）。」
拐小孩的（The Baby-Snatcher）、花哨雨衣、送信小子（the Messenger Boy）、打拳的希尼以及类似的角色们过委员会时毫发无损，他们讨厌的人格看不出丝毫变化。
荷兰人比尔走后不久，我们失去了两座快乐山：流浪者和苏普利斯（Surplice）。剩下祖鲁人和让·勒内格尔。……放风时，B. 和我把大部分时间用来在 cour 里采集色泽相当美丽的叶子。这些叶子我们夹进我的一个笔记本，连同我们能找到的所有颜色——烟盒上的、巧克力纸上的、各种标签上的，甚至邮票上的。（我们还从某一块布头上弄到了一种非常鲜艳的红。）我们的举动让所有人（包括 planton 们）大惑不解，甚为不解；这很自然，因为大家都不知道，我们是在用这个极其简单的手段研究色彩本身，结合那种俗称「抽象」、有时叫「非具象」的绘画。

===Original===

Despite their natural puzzlement everyone (*plantons* excepted) was extraordinarily kind and brought us often valuable additions to our chromatic collection. Had I, at this moment and in the city of New York, the complete confidence of one-twentieth as many human beings I should not be so inclined to consider The Great American Public as the most aesthetically incapable organization ever created for the purpose of perpetuating defunct ideals and ideas. But of course The Great American Public has a handicap which my friends at La Ferté did not as a rule have﻿—education. Let no one sound his indignant yawp at this. I refer to the fact that, for an educated gent or lady, to create is first of all to destroy﻿—that there is and can be no such thing as authentic art until the *bons trucs* (whereby we are taught to see and imitate on canvas and in stone and by words this so-called world) are entirely and thoroughly and perfectly annihilated by that vast and painful process of Unthinking which may result in a minute bit of purely personal Feeling. Which minute bit is Art.
Ah well, the revolution﻿—I refer of course to the intelligent revolution﻿—is on the way; is perhaps nearer than some think, is possibly knocking at the front doors of The Great Mister Harold Bell Wright and The Great Little Miss Pollyanna. In the course of the next ten thousand years it may be possible to find Delectable Mountains without going to prison﻿—captivity, I mean, Monsieur le Surveillant﻿—it may be possible, I daresay, to encounter Delectable Mountains who are not in prison.﻿ ﻿…
The Autumn wore on.
Rain did, from time to time, not fall: from time to time a sort of unhealthy almost-light leaked from the large uncrisp corpse of the sky, returning for a moment to our view the ruined landscape. From time to time the eye, travelling carefully with a certain disagreeable suddenly fear no longer distances of air, coldish and sweet, stopped upon the incredible clearness of the desolate, without-motion, Autumn. Awkward and solemn clearness, making louder the unnecessary cries, the hoarse laughter of the invisible harlots in their muddy yard, pointing a cool actual finger at the silly and ferocious group of man-shaped beings huddled in the mud under four or five little trees, came strangely in my own mind pleasantly to suggest the ludicrous and hideous and beautiful antics of the insane.

===Chinese===

尽管天生的困惑，每个人（planton 除外）都出奇地友善，常给我们的色彩收藏添些宝贵的进项。倘若此时此地，在纽约市，我能获得只相当于这里二十分之一人数者的完全信任，我就不至于那么倾向于把伟大的美国公众（The Great American Public），看成有史以来为使死掉的理想和观念不朽而创建的最无能审美的组织了。不过当然，伟大的美国公众有一项我的拉费尔泰朋友们照例没有的障碍——教育。谁也别为此发出愤怒的嚎叫。我指的是这样一桩事实：对一个受过教育的绅士或女士来说，创造首先是破坏——除非那些 bons trucs（教我们在画布上、石头上、文字里观看并模仿这个所谓世界的好把戏）被那个浩大而痛苦的去学（Unthinking）过程彻底地、完全地、干干净净地歼灭，否则就没有、也不可能有真正的艺术这种东西；而去学或可产出一星半点纯然个人的感受。那一星半点，就是艺术。
唉，革命——我当然指那场有头脑的革命——正在路上；也许比某些人想的更近，也许正敲着大哈罗德·贝尔·赖特先生（The Great Mister Harold Bell Wright）和小波莉安娜小姐（The Great Little Miss Pollyanna）的前门。在未来的一万年里，也许不必坐牢也能遇上快乐山——我是说囚禁，监督先生——我敢说，也许能遇上不在监狱里的快乐山。……
秋深了。
雨，间或，并不落下：间或有一种不健康的、几乎算不上光的东西，从天空那巨大而不脆爽的尸体里渗漏出来，一时把倾颓的风景还给我们眼前。间或，目光沿着空气小心翼翼地旅行，带着某种不快的、突如其来的恐惧——那空气已不再有距离，凉而发甜——停驻在荒凉、无动于衷的秋之那不可思议的清澈上。笨拙而庄严的清澈，放大着看不见的娼妇们在泥泞院子里不必要的叫喊与嘶哑的笑声，用一根清凉而实在的手指，指着四五棵小树下蜷在泥里、那伙愚蠢而凶残的人形生物，奇怪地在我自己心里，令人愉快地想起疯子们那些滑稽、丑陋而美丽的古怪举动。

===Original===

Frequently I would discover so perfect a command over myself as to reduce *la promenade* easily to a recently invented mechanism; or to the demonstration of a collection of vivid and unlovely toys around and around which, guarding them with impossible heroism, funnily moved purely unreal *plantons* always absurdly marching, the maimed and stupid dolls of my imagination. Once I was sitting alone on the long beam of silent iron and suddenly had the gradual complete unique experience of death.﻿ ﻿…
It became amazingly cold.
One evening B. and myself and, I think it was the Machine-Fixer, were partaking of the warmth of a *bougie* hard by and, in fact, between our ambulance beds, when the door opened, a *planton* entered, and a list of names (none of which we recognized) was hurriedly read off with (as in the case of the last *partis*, including The Wanderer and Surplice) the admonition:
“Be ready to leave early tomorrow morning.”
—and the door shut loudly and quickly. Now one of the names which had been called sounded somewhat like “Broom,” and a strange inquietude seized us on this account. Could it possibly have been “B.”? We made inquiries of certain of our friends who had been nearer the *planton* than ourselves. We were told that Pete and The Trick Raincoat and The Fighting Sheeney and Rockyfeller were leaving﻿—about “B.” nobody was able to enlighten us. Not that opinions in this matter were lacking. There were plenty of opinions﻿—but they contradicted each other to a painful extent. *Les hommes* were in fact about equally divided; half considering that the occult sound had been intended for “B.,” half that the somewhat asthmatic *planton* had unwittingly uttered a spontaneous grunt or sigh, which sigh or grunt we had mistaken for a proper noun. Our uncertainty was augmented by the confusion emanating from a particular corner of The Enormous Room, in which corner The Fighting Sheeney was haranguing a group of spectators on the pregnant topic: What I won’t do to Précigne when I get there. In deep converse with Bathhouse John we beheld the very same youth who, some time since, had drifted to a place beside me at *la soupe*﻿—Pete The Ghost, white and determined, blond and fragile: Pete the Shadow.﻿ ﻿…
I forget who, but someone﻿—I think it was the little Machine-Fixer﻿—established the truth that an American was to leave the next morning.

===Chinese===

我常常发现我对自制力有如此完美的掌握，以致能轻松地把散步（la promenade）还原成一台新近发明的机器；或者还原成一组鲜活而不可爱的玩具的演示，一些全然不真实的 planton 围着它们转圈，以不可能的英雄主义看守着它们，可笑地永远齐步走——全是我想象中缺胳膊少腿的蠢娃娃。有一次我独自坐在那根沉默的铁长梁上，突然经历了那渐进的、完全的、独一无二的死亡体验。……
天冷得惊人。
一天晚上，B. 和我，还有——我想是机器修理工——正分享一支蜡烛（bougie）的暖意，就在我们两张救护车床之间，这时门开了，进来一个 planton，急匆匆宣读了一份名单（上面的名字我们一个也不认识），并附上一句（同上一批离去的、包括流浪者和苏普利斯那次的）训示：
「明儿一早动身，做好准备。」
——门又响又快地关上了。念过的名字里有一个听着有点像「布鲁姆」，为此一种奇怪的不安攫住了我们。会不会是「B.」？我们向几位比我们离那 planton 更近的朋友打听。得到的答复是：皮特、花哨雨衣、打拳的希尼和洛基菲勒（Rockyfeller）要走了——关于「B.」，谁也无法给我们指点。倒不是说在这件事上缺少意见。意见多得很——只是彼此矛盾到痛苦的程度。男人们事实上约略分成两半：一半认定那神秘的声音指的就是「B.」，另一半认定那个有点哮喘的 planton 无意中发出了一声自发的哼哧或叹息，而这声叹息或哼哧被我们误当作了一个专有名词。我们的不确定，又被巨大的房间某个角落里散发出的混乱加重了，在那个角落，打拳的希尼正向一群看客发表演说，题目意味深长：《等我到了普雷西涅，我要怎么收拾那地方》。我们还看见同澡堂约翰（Bathhouse John）深谈着的那位，正是不久前在喝汤（la soupe）时飘到我身旁坐下的小子——鬼魂皮特（Pete The Ghost），苍白而决绝，金发而脆弱：影子皮特。……
我忘了是谁，反正有人——我想是小小的机器修理工——确证了一个事实：明早有一个美国人要走。

===Original===

That, moreover, said American’s name was B.
Whereupon B. and I became extraordinarily busy.
The Zulu and Jean le Nègre, upon learning that B. was among the *partis*, came over to our beds and sat down without uttering a word. The former, through a certain shy orchestration of silence, conveyed effortlessly and perfectly his sorrow at the departure; the latter, by his bowed head and a certain very delicate restraint manifested in the wholly exquisite poise of his firm alert body, uttered at least a universe of grief.
The little Machine-Fixer was extremely indignant; not only that his friend was going to a den of thieves and ruffians, but that his friend was leaving in such company as that of *cette crapule* (meaning Rockyfeller) and *les deux mangeurs de blanc* (to wit, The Trick Raincoat and The Fighting Sheeney). “*c’est malheureux*,” he repeated over and over, wagging his poor little head in rage and despair﻿—“it’s no place for a young man who has done no wrong, to be shut up with pimps and cutthroats, *pour la durée de la guerre; le gouvernement français a bien fait!*” and he brushed a tear out of his eye with a desperate rapid brittle gesture.﻿ ﻿… But what angered the Machine-Fixer most was that B. and I were about to be separated﻿—“*M’sieu’ Jean*” (touching me gently on the knee) “they have no hearts, *la commission*; they are not simply unjust, they are cruel, *savez-vous*? Men are not like these; they are not men, they are Name of God I don’t know what, they are worse than the animals; and they pretend to Justice” (shivering from top to toe with an indescribable sneer) “Justice! My God, Justice!”
All of which, somehow or other, did not exactly cheer us.
And, the packing completed, we drank together for The Last Time. The Zulu and Jean Le Nègre and the Machine-Fixer and B. and I﻿—and Pete The Shadow drifted over, whiter than I think I ever saw him, and said simply to me:
“I’ll take care o’ your friend, Johnny.”
… and then at last it was *lumières éteintes*; and *les deux américains* lay in their beds in the cold rotten darkness, talking in low voices of the past, of Petroushka, of Paris, of that brilliant and extraordinary and impossible something: Life.
---
Morning. Whitish. Inevitable. Deathly cold.
There was a great deal of hurry and bustle in The Enormous Room. People were rushing hither and thither in the heavy half-darkness.

===Chinese===

而且，据说那美国人的名字就是 B.。
于是 B. 和我变得格外忙碌起来。
祖鲁人和让·勒内格尔听说 B. 名列离去者之中，走到我们床边，一语不发地坐下。前者借一种羞怯的沉默的管弦乐，毫不费力而完美地传达了他对离别的悲伤；后者以他低垂的头，以及他那警觉而结实的身体在完全精妙的姿态中显示出的一种十分细腻的克制，至少说出了整整一个宇宙的哀伤。
小小的机器修理工气愤极了；不光因为他的朋友要去一个盗贼与恶棍的巢穴，还因为他的朋友要同这个无赖（cette crapule）（指洛基菲勒）和那两个吃白食的（les deux mangeurs de blanc）（也就是花哨雨衣和打拳的希尼）之流为伍。「真不幸（c'est malheureux）」他一遍又一遍地重复，愤怒而绝望地摇着他那可怜的小脑袋——「一个没做过坏事的年轻人，跟皮条客和杀人犯关在一起，为着战争的整个期间（pour la durée de la guerre）；法国政府干的好事（le gouvernement français a bien fait!）」他用一个绝望、急促而脆弱的手势，从眼睛里抹去一滴泪。……但最让机器修理工气愤的是，B. 和我就要被拆散了——「让先生（M'sieu' Jean）」（轻轻碰了碰我的膝盖）「他们没有心肠，la commission；他们不只是不公正，他们残忍，您知道吗（savez-vous）？人不该是这副样子；他们不是人，他们是名字叫上帝的我都不知道是什么，他们比畜生还不如；他们还自称正义」（从头到脚打个说不出的寒噤，带着一阵冷笑）「正义！我的上帝，正义！」
这一切，不知怎么的，没能让我们振奋起来。
于是，行装打点完毕，我们共饮了最后一杯。祖鲁人、让·勒内格尔、机器修理工、B. 和我——影子皮特也飘了过来，比我记得任何时候见到的都白，对我简单地说：
「我会照看你朋友的，约翰尼（I'll take care o' your friend, Johnny.）。」
……终于，熄灯了（lumières éteintes）；两个美国人躺在各自的床上，在寒冷而腐烂的黑暗里，低声谈着过去，谈着彼得鲁什卡（Petroushka），谈着巴黎，谈着那件辉煌、非凡、不可能的东西：生活。
---
早晨。灰白。不可回避。冷得要命。
巨大的房间里一片忙乱。人们在浓重的半明半暗里跑来跑去。

===Original===

People were saying goodbye to people. Saying goodbye to friends. Saying goodbye to themselves. We lay and sipped the black evil dull certainly not coffee; lay on our beds, dressed, shuddering with cold, waiting. Waiting. Several of *les hommes* whom we scarcely knew came up to B. and shook hands with him and said good luck and goodbye. The darkness was going rapidly out of the dull black evil stinking air. B. suddenly realized that he had no gift for The Zulu; he asked a fine Norwegian to whom he had given his leather belt if he, the Norwegian, would mind giving it back, because there was a very dear friend who had been forgotten. The Norwegian, with a pleasant smile, took off the belt and said “Certainly”﻿ ﻿… he had been arrested at Bordeaux, where he came ashore from his ship, for stealing three cans of sardines when he was drunk﻿ ﻿… a very great and dangerous criminal﻿ ﻿… he said “Certainly,” and gave B. a pleasant smile, the pleasantest smile in the world. B. wrote his own address and name in the inside of the belt, explained in French to The Young Pole that any time The Zulu wanted to reach him all he had to do was to consult the belt; The Young Pole translated; The Zulu nodded; The Norwegian smiled appreciatively; The Zulu received the belt with a gesture to which words cannot do the faintest justice﻿—
A *planton* was standing in The Enormous Room, a *planton* roaring and cursing and crying, “Hurry, those who are going to go.”﻿—B. shook hands with Jean and Mexique and the Machine-Fixer and the Young Skipper, and Bathhouse John (to whom he had given his ambulance tunic, and who was crazy-proud in consequence) and the Norwegian and the Washing Machine Man and The Hat and many of *les hommes* whom we scarcely knew.﻿—The Black Holster was roaring:
“*Allez, nom de Dieu, l’américain!*”
I went down the room with B. and Pete, and shook hands with both at the door. The other *partis*, alias The Trick Raincoat and The Fighting Sheeney, were already on the way downstairs. The Black Holster cursed us and me in particular and slammed the door angrily in my face﻿—
Through the little peephole I caught a glimpse of them, entering the street. I went to my bed and lay down quietly in my great pelisse. The clamour and filth of the room brightened and became distant and faded.

===Chinese===

人们在互相道别。向朋友们道别。向自己道别。我们躺着，啜着那黑色、邪恶、乏味、肯定不是咖啡的东西；和衣躺在床上，冷得发抖，等着。等着。几个我们几乎不认识的男人们走到 B. 面前，同他握手，祝他好运，道声再见。黑暗正从那沉闷、黑恶、发臭的空气里迅速退出。B. 突然想起没有给祖鲁人的礼物；他问一位他曾把皮带赠与的好心的挪威人，能不能把皮带还回来，因为漏掉了一位非常亲爱的朋友。挪威人愉快地一笑，解下皮带说「当然」……他是在波尔多被捕的，当时他从船上上岸，喝醉了偷了三罐沙丁鱼……一个非常伟大而危险的罪犯……他说「当然」，并给了 B. 一个愉快的微笑，全世界最愉快的微笑。B. 把自己的地址和姓名写进皮带内侧，用法语向小波兰人解释，祖鲁人任何时候想找他，只消查看皮带就行；小波兰人翻译；祖鲁人点头；挪威人赞赏地微笑；祖鲁人接过皮带，那一个手势，言语连最轻微的公道都无法给予——
一个 planton 站在巨大的房间里，咆哮着、咒骂着、大喊：「要走的赶紧。」——B. 同让、墨西哥人、机器修理工、少年船长握了手，同澡堂约翰（他曾把救护车短外套送了他，他因此骄傲得发疯）、挪威人、洗衣机人（the Washing Machine Man）、帽子（The Hat），还有许多我们几乎不认识的男人们握了手。——黑枪套在吼：
「走，以上帝之名，那个美国人（Allez, nom de Dieu, l'américain!）」
我同 B. 和皮特一起走到房间那头，在门口同两人握了手。其余的离去者，别名花哨雨衣和打拳的希尼，已经在下楼的路上了。黑枪套咒骂我们，尤其咒骂我，当着我的面愤怒地摔上了门——
透过小小的窥视孔，我瞥见了他们，走进了街道。我回到自己床上，裹着我那件大皮衣，静静躺下。房间的喧嚷与污秽亮了一亮，变得遥远，淡去了。

===Original===

I heard the voice of the jolly Alsatian saying:
“*Courage, mon ami*, your comrade is not dead; you will see him later,” and after that, nothing. In front of and on and within my eyes lived suddenly a violent and gentle and dark silence.
---
The Three Wise Men had done their work. But wisdom cannot rest.﻿ ﻿…
Probably at that very moment they were holding their court in another La Ferté committing to incomparable anguish some few merely perfectly wretched criminals: little and tall, tremulous and brave﻿—all of them white and speechless, all of them with tight bluish lips and large whispering eyes, all of them with fingers weary and mutilated and extraordinarily old﻿ ﻿… desperate fingers; closing, to feel the final lukewarm fragment of life glide neatly and softly into forgetfulness.

===Chinese===

我听见快活的阿尔萨斯人的嗓音在说：
「勇敢些，我的朋友（Courage, mon ami），你的同志没有死；你以后会见着他的」此后，便什么也没有了。我的眼睛前头、上面、里面，骤然住进了一种暴烈而温柔而黑暗的寂静。
---
三博士干完了他们的活计。但智慧无法安歇。……
很可能就在那一刻，他们正在另一座拉费尔泰开庭，把若干仅仅无比凄惶的罪犯，交付给无可比拟的痛苦：矮小的与高大的，颤抖的与勇敢的——全都脸色惨白，一言不发，全都嘴唇青紫，眼睛大而低语，全都手指疲惫、残缺、老得不同寻常……绝望的手指；合拢起来，去感受生命的最后一片温热，干净而轻柔地滑入遗忘。

## XIII I Say Goodbye to La Misère / XIII 我向拉米泽尔道别
===Original===

To convince the reader that this history is mere fiction (and rather vulgarly violent fiction at that) nothing perhaps is needed save that ancient standby of sob-story writers and thrill-artists alike﻿—the Happy Ending. As a matter of fact, it makes not the smallest difference to me whether anyone who has thus far participated in my travels does or does not believe that they and I are (as that mysterious animal, “the public” would say) “real.” I do, however, very strenuously object to the assumption, on the part of anyone, that the heading of this, my final, chapter stands for anything in the nature of happiness. In the course of recalling (in God knows a rather clumsy and perfectly inadequate way) what happened to me between the latter part of August, 1917, and the first of January, 1918, I have proved to my own satisfaction (if not to anyone else’s) that I was happier in La Ferté Macé, with The Delectable Mountains about me, than the very keenest words can pretend to express. I daresay it all comes down to a definition of happiness. And a definition of happiness I most certainly do not intend to attempt; but I can and will say this: to leave La Misère with the knowledge, and worse than that the feeling, that some of the finest people in the world are doomed to remain prisoners thereof for no one knows how long﻿—are doomed to continue, possibly for years and tens of years and all the years which terribly are between them and their deaths, the grey and indivisible Nonexistence which without apology you are quitting for Reality﻿—cannot by any stretch of the imagination be conceived as constituting a Happy Ending to a great and personal adventure. That I write this chapter at all is due, purely and simply, to the, I daresay, unjustified hope on my part that﻿—by recording certain events﻿—it may hurl a little additional light into a very tremendous darkness.﻿ ﻿…
At the outset let me state that what occurred subsequent to the departure for Précigne of B. and Pete and The Sheeneys and Rockyfeller is shrouded in a rather ridiculous indistinctness; due, I have to admit, to the depression which this departure inflicted upon my altogether too human nature. The judgment of the Three Wise Men had﻿—to use a peculiarly vigorous (not to say vital) expression of my own day and time﻿—knocked me for a loop.

===Chinese===

要说服读者相信这部历史纯属虚构（而且还是相当俗气的暴力虚构），恐怕什么也不需要，只需要悲情小说家和惊险艺术家们共同的看家法宝——大团圆结局。事实上，那些一路跟随我游历的人，信不信他们和我（正如那种神秘动物「公众」会说的）是「真实」的，对我不产生丝毫影响。然而，我极其强烈地反对任何人作如下假定：我这最后一章的标题代表任何形式的幸福。在回忆（以天知道多么笨拙而全然不够的方式）1917年8月下旬至1918年1月1日之间发生在我身上的事情的过程中，我已经令自己满意地证明了（如果说没有令别人满意的话）：我在拉费尔泰马塞、被快乐山们环绕着，比最锋利的言辞所能装作表达的还要幸福。我想这归根结底取决于幸福的定义。而幸福的定义我绝对不打算尝试；但我能说也将要说的是：离开拉米泽尔（La Misère）时知道——更糟的是感觉到——世上一些最好的人注定还要留在那里当囚徒，不知要留多久——注定要继续，可能几年、几十年、以及横在他们与死亡之间的所有那些可怕的年头，过那种灰色的、不可分割的不存在，而你毫无歉意地丢下它奔向实在——这无论如何想象，都不能被设想为一场伟大的个人冒险的快乐结局。我之所以还要写这一章，纯粹而简单地说，是由于我那恐怕并无根据的希望——通过记录某些事件——或许能往一片巨大的黑暗里多掷进一点光亮。……
一开场就让我说明：B.、皮特、希尼们和洛基菲勒动身去普雷西涅之后发生的事，笼罩在一层相当可笑的模糊里；我得承认，原因是这次离别给我这副太有人性的天性造成的消沉。三博士的判决——用我自己那个年代一个格外有力（不妨说致命）的说法——把我整个打懵了。

===Original===

I spent the days intervening between the separation from “*votre camarade*” and my somewhat supernatural departure for freedom in attempting to partially straighten myself. When finally I made my exit, the part of me popularly referred to as “mind” was still in a slightly bent if not twisted condition. Not until some weeks of American diet had revolutionized my exterior did my interior completely resume the contours of normality. I am particularly neither ashamed nor proud of this (one might nearly say) mental catastrophe. No more ashamed or proud, in fact, than of the infection of three fingers which I carried to America as a little token of La Ferté’s goodwill. In the latter case I certainly have no right to boast, even should I find myself so inclined; for B. took with him to Précigne a case of what his father, upon B.’s arrival in The Home of The Brave, diagnosed as scurvy﻿—which scurvy made my mutilations look like thirty cents or even less. One of my vividest memories of La Ferté consists in a succession of crackling noises associated with the disrobing of my friend. I recall that we appealed to Monsieur Ree-chard together, B. in behalf of his scurvy and I in behalf of my hand plus a queer little row of sores, the latter having proceeded to adorn that part of my face which was trying hard to be graced with a moustache. I recall that Monsieur Ree-chard decreed a *bain* for B., which *bain* meant immersion in a large tin tub partially filled with not quite lukewarm water. I, on the contrary, obtained a speck of zinc ointment on a minute piece of cotton, and considered myself peculiarly fortunate. Which details cannot possibly offend the reader’s aesthetic sense to a greater degree than have already certain minutiae connected with the sanitary arrangements of The Directeur’s little home for homeless boys and girls﻿—therefore I will not trouble to beg the reader’s pardon; but will proceed with my story proper or improper.
“*Mais qu’est-ce que vous avez*,” Monsieur le Surveillant demanded, in a tone of profound if kindly astonishment, as I wended my lonely way to *la soupe* some days after the disappearance of *les partis*.
I stood and stared at him very stupidly without answering, having indeed nothing at all to say.
“But why are you so sad?” he asked.
“I suppose I miss my friend,” I ventured.

===Chinese===

同「您的同志（votre camarade）」分别之后、到我那场有点超自然的奔赴自由之前，中间的那些日子，我都在设法把自己部分地捋直。等我终于出场时，我身上那块俗称「头脑」的部件仍处于轻微弯曲若非扭曲的状态。直到几个星期的美国伙食彻底革新了我的外部，我的内部才完全恢复正常的轮廓。对这场（几乎可以说是）精神崩溃，我既不特别羞愧也不特别骄傲。事实上，既不比三根手指的感染更羞愧，也不更骄傲——那是我带去美国的一点拉费尔泰善意的纪念品。后一种情况我当然没有权利吹嘘，即使我发现自己想吹嘘；因为 B. 把一例病带去了普雷西涅，他父亲在 B. 抵达勇士之家时诊断为坏血病——那坏血病让我的残缺看起来只值三毛钱甚至更少。我对拉费尔泰最鲜活的记忆之一，是我朋友脱衣时一连串噼啪的声响。我记得我们曾一同向里夏尔先生（Monsieur Ree-chard）求告，B. 为他的坏血病，我为我的手外加一小排古怪的小疮——后者已经开始装点我脸上那处正努力配得上两撇小胡子的地方。我记得里夏尔先生为 B. 颁赐了一个澡（bain），此澡意味着浸入一只大锡桶，桶里部分盛着说不上温热的水。我呢，正相反，得到了一点锌软膏，涂在一小团棉花上，我自觉格外幸运。这些细节对读者审美感的冒犯，不可能超过所长那所收容无家可归少男少女的小宅子里与卫生安排有关的某些细节已经冒犯的程度——因此我不打算费神请求读者原谅；而将继续讲我的故事，得体也罢不得体也罢。
「可是您怎么了（Mais qu'est-ce que vous avez）」离去者们（les partis）消失几天后，我独自一人走向汤（la soupe）时，监督先生以一种深沉而不失好意的惊讶语气问我。
我站着，非常愚蠢地瞪着他，没有回答，实在一言不发。
「可您为什么这么伤心？」他问。
「大概是想我朋友了，」我斗胆说。

===Original===

“*Mais﻿—mais﻿—*” he puffed and panted like a very old and fat person trying to persuade a bicycle to climb a hill﻿—“*mais﻿—vous avez de la chance!*”
“I suppose I have,” I said without enthusiasm.
“*Mais﻿—mais﻿—parfaitement﻿—vous avez de la chance﻿—uh-ah﻿—uh-ah﻿—parce que﻿—comprenez-vous﻿—votre camarade﻿—ah-ah﻿—a attrapé prison!*”
“Uh-ah!” I said wearily.
“Whereas,” continued Monsieur, “you haven’t. You ought to be extraordinarily thankful and particularly happy!”
“I should rather have gone to prison with my friend,” I stated briefly; and went into the dining-room, leaving the Surveillant uh-ahing in nothing short of complete amazement.
I really believe that my condition worried him, incredible as this may seem. At the time I gave neither an extraordinary nor a particular damn about Monsieur le Surveillant, nor indeed about “*l’autre américain*” alias myself. Dimly, through a fog of disinterested inapprehension, I realized that﻿—with the exception of the *plantons* and, of course, Apollyon﻿—everyone was trying very hard to help me; that The Zulu, Jean, The Machine-Fixer, Mexique, The Young Skipper, even The Washing Machine Man (with whom I promenaded frequently when no one else felt like taking the completely unagreeable air) were kind, very kind, kinder than I can possibly say. As for Afrique and The Cook﻿—there was nothing too good for me at this time. I asked the latter’s permission to cut wood, and was not only accepted as a sawyer, but encouraged with assurances of the best coffee there was, with real sugar *dedans*. In the little space outside the *cuisine*, between the building and *la cour*, I sawed away of a morning to my great satisfaction; from time to time clumping my saboted way into the chef’s domain in answer to a subdued signal from Afrique. Of an afternoon I sat with Jean or Mexique or The Zulu on the long beam of silent iron, pondering very carefully nothing at all, replying to their questions or responding to their observations in a highly mechanical manner. I felt myself to be, at last, a doll﻿—taken out occasionally and played with and put back into its house and told to go to sleep.﻿ ﻿…
One afternoon I was lying on my couch, thinking of the usual Nothing, when a sharp cry sung through The Enormous Room:
“*Il tombe de la neige﻿—Noël! Noël!*”
I sat up.

===Chinese===

「可——可——」他喘着粗气，像一位很老很胖的人竭力劝一辆自行车爬坡——「可——您运气好着呢（vous avez de la chance!）」
「大概吧，」我毫无热情地说。
「可——可——完全地——您运气好——嗯啊——嗯啊——因为——您明白吗——您的同志——啊啊——抓进监狱啦（a attrapé prison!）」
「嗯啊！」我疲惫地说。
「而您没有，」先生接着说，「您应该格外感恩、特别幸福才是！」
「我倒宁愿跟我朋友一起进监狱，」我简短地声明；随即走进饭厅，留下监督在丝毫不逊色的惊愕中嗯啊个不停。
我真的相信我的状况让他担心了，尽管这看来难以置信。当时我对监督先生既不格外、也不特别在乎，对「另一个美国人（l'autre américain）」——别名我本人——也一样。隔着一片漠然的迷雾，我隐约意识到——除了 planton 们，当然还有亚玻伦（Apollyon）——人人都在非常努力地帮我；祖鲁人、让、机器修理工、墨西哥人、少年船长，甚至洗衣机人（没有别人想呼吸那完全不宜人的空气时，我常同他一起放风）都很善良，非常善良，善良得我说不出。至于阿弗里克和厨子（The Cook）——这时候给我什么都不嫌好。我请求后者允许我劈柴，结果不但被接纳为锯木匠，还受到最好的咖啡加真糖（dedans）的许诺的鼓舞。在厨房外头、大楼与 cour 之间的那一小片空地上，我每天早晨锯木锯得心满意足；不时蹬着木鞋咚咚地走进大厨的领地，响应阿弗里克一个压低的暗号。下午我同让或墨西哥人或祖鲁人坐在那根沉默的铁长梁上，非常仔细地什么都不想，以一种高度机械的方式回答他们的问题或回应他们的议论。我感到自己终于成了一个娃娃——偶尔被拿出来玩一玩，再放回它的小屋，被吩咐去睡觉。……
一天下午我正躺在铺上想着惯常的那个「无」，一声尖叫穿过巨大的房间：
「下雪了（Il tombe de la neige）——圣诞（Noël!）圣诞（Noël!）」
我坐了起来。

===Original===

The Guard Champêtre was at the nearest window, dancing a little horribly and crying:
“*Noël! Noël!*”
I went to another window and looked out. Sure enough. Snow was falling, gradually and wonderfully falling, silently falling through the thick soundless Autumn.﻿ ﻿… It seemed to me supremely beautiful, the snow. There was about it something unspeakably crisp and exquisite, something perfect and minute and gentle and fatal.﻿ ﻿… The Guard Champêtre’s cry began a poem in the back of my head, a poem about the snow, a poem in French, beginning *Il tombe de la neige, Noël, Noël.* I watched the snow. After a long time I returned to my bunk and I lay down, closing my eyes; feeling the snow’s minute and crisp touch falling gently and exquisitely, falling perfectly and suddenly, through the thick soundless autumn of my imagination.﻿ ﻿…
“*L’américain! L’américain!*”
Someone is speaking to me.
“*Le petit belge avec le bras cassé est là-bas, à la porte, il veut parler.﻿ ﻿…*”
I marched the length of the room. The Enormous Room is filled with a new and beautiful darkness, the darkness of the snow outside, falling and falling and falling with the silent and actual gesture which has touched the soundless country of my mind as a child touches a toy it loves.﻿ ﻿…
Through the locked door I heard a nervous whisper: “*Dis à l’américain que je veux parler avec lui.*”﻿—“*Me voici*” I said.
“Put your ear to the keyhole, *M’sieu’ Jean*,” said the Machine-Fixer’s voice. The voice of the little Machine-Fixer, tremendously excited. I obey﻿—“*Alors. Qu’est-ce que c’est, mon ami?*”
“*M’sieu’ Jean! Le Directeur va vous appeler tout de suite!* You must get ready instantly! Wash and shave, eh? He’s going to call you right away. And don’t forget! Oloron! You will ask to go to Oloron Sainte Marie, where you can paint! Oloron Sainte Marie, Basse Pyrenées! *N’oubliez pas, M’sieu’ Jean! Et dépêchez-vous!*”
“*Merci bien, mon ami!*”﻿—I remember now. The little Machine-Fixer and I had talked. It seemed that *la commission* had decided that I was not a criminal, but only a suspect. As a suspect I would be sent to some place in France, any place I wanted to go, provided it was not on or near the sea coast. That was in order that I should not perhaps try to escape from France. The Machine-Fixer had advised me to ask to go to Oloron Sainte Marie.

===Chinese===

田野卫兵（The Guard Champêtre）在最近的窗前，微微可怕地跳着舞，喊着：
「圣诞！圣诞（Noël! Noël!）」
我走到另一扇窗前向外看。千真万确。雪在落，徐缓而奇妙地落，无声地落过浓稠而无声的秋天。……在我看来，雪美到极致。它身上有种说不出的脆爽与精致，某种完美的、微小的、温柔的、致命的东西。……田野卫兵的喊声在我后脑开始了一首诗，一首关于雪的诗，一首法语诗，开头是：下雪了，圣诞，圣诞（Il tombe de la neige, Noël, Noël.）。我望着雪。过了很久我回到铺上躺下，闭上眼；感受雪的微小而脆爽的触碰，轻柔而精致地落下，完美而突然地落下，穿过我想象中浓稠无声的秋天。……
「美国人！美国人！（L'américain! L'américain!）」
有人在对我说话。
「断臂的小比利时人在那边，门口，他想说话。……」
我大步穿过整个房间。巨大的房间充满一种全新而美丽的黑暗，外面落雪的黑暗，落着，落着，落着，以那无声而实在的姿态——它已触碰过我头脑中无声的国度，像孩子触碰它心爱的玩具。……
透过锁着的门我听见一个紧张的耳语：「跟美国人说我要跟他说话（Dis à l'américain que je veux parler avec lui.）。」——「我在这儿呢（Me voici）」我说。
「把耳朵贴到钥匙孔上，让先生（M'sieu' Jean），」机器修理工的声音说。小小的机器修理工的声音，激动万状。我照办——「那么。什么事，我的朋友（Alors. Qu'est-ce que c'est, mon ami?）」
「让先生！所长马上就要叫您了（Le Directeur va vous appeler tout de suite!）！您必须立刻准备！洗脸刮胡子，嗯？他马上就来叫您。别忘了！奥洛龙！您要请求去奥洛龙圣玛丽（Oloron Sainte Marie），在那儿您可以画画！奥洛龙圣玛丽，下比利牛斯省（Basse Pyrenées）！别忘了，让先生！快（Et dépêchez-vous!）」
「多谢，我的朋友（Merci bien, mon ami!）」——现在我记起来了。小小的机器修理工和我谈过。似乎 la commission 已裁定我不是罪犯，只是嫌疑犯。作为嫌疑犯，我将被送往法国的某地，任何我想去的地方，只要不在海岸上或海岸附近。那是为了让我或许不至于企图逃离法国。机器修理工曾建议我请求去奥洛龙圣玛丽。

===Original===

I should say that, as a painter, the Pyrenees particularly appealed to me. “*Et qu’il fait beau, là-bas!* The snow on the mountains! And it’s not cold. And what mountains! You can live there very cheaply. As a suspect you will merely have to report once a month to the chief of police of Oloron Sainte Marie; he’s an old friend of mine! He’s a fine, fat, red-cheeked man, very kindly. He will make it easy for you, *M’sieu’ Jean*, and will help you out in every way, when you tell him you are a friend of the little Belgian with the broken arm. Tell him I sent you. You will have a very fine time, and you can paint: such scenery to paint! My God﻿—not like what you see from these windows. I advise you by all means to ask to go to Oloron.”
So thinking I lathered my face, standing before Judas’ mirror.
“You don’t rub enough,” the Alsatian advised, “*il faut frotter bien!*” A number of fellow-captives were regarding my toilet with surprise and satisfaction. I discovered in the mirror an astounding beard and a good layer of dirt. I worked busily, counselled by several voices, censured by the Alsatian, encouraged by Judas himself. The shave and the wash completed I felt considerably refreshed.
**Whang!**
“*L’américain en bas!*” It was the Black Holster. I carefully adjusted my tunic and obeyed him.
The Directeur and the Surveillant were in consultation when I entered the latter’s office. Apollyon, seated at a desk, surveyed me very fiercely. His subordinate swayed to and fro, clasping and unclasping his hands behind his back, and regarded me with an expression of almost benevolence. The Black Holster guarded the doorway.
Turning on me ferociously: “Your friend is wicked, very wicked, ***savez-vous***?” Le Directeur shouted.
I answered quietly: “*Oui? Je ne le savait pas.*”
“He is a bad fellow, a criminal, a traitor, an insult to civilization,” Apollyon roared into my face.
“Yes?” I said again.
“You’d better be careful!” the Directeur shouted. “Do you know what’s happened to your friend?”
“*Sais pas*,” I said.
“He’s gone to prison where he belongs!” Apollyon roared. “Do you understand what that means?”
“Perhaps,” I answered, somewhat insolently I fear.
“You’re lucky not to be there with him! Do you understand?”

===Chinese===

我得说，作为画家，比利牛斯山对我特别有吸引力。「那儿天气多好啊（Et qu'il fait beau, là-bas!）！山上的雪！还不冷。多好的山啊！在那儿生活非常便宜。作为嫌疑犯，您只需每月到奥洛龙圣玛丽的警察局长那里报到一次；他是我的老朋友！是个体面的、胖胖的、红脸膛的人，非常和气。他会给您行方便的，让先生，还会想尽办法帮您，只要您告诉他您是断臂小比利时人的朋友。告诉他是我派您去的。您会过得非常舒心，还可以画画：多好的风景可画！我的上帝——跟这些窗户里看见的可不一样。我劝您无论如何要求去奥洛龙。」
我这样想着，往脸上涂皂沫，站在犹大（Judas）的镜子前。
「您搓得不够，」阿尔萨斯人指点道，「得使劲搓（il faut frotter bien!）」一些难友正又惊又喜地围观我的梳妆。我在镜子里发现一副惊世骇俗的胡子和厚厚一层污垢。我忙碌地干着，好几条嗓子出主意，阿尔萨斯人挑剔，犹大本人亲自鼓励。剃须和洗漱完毕，我感到神清气爽了不少。
当！（Whang!）
「美国人下来（L'américain en bas!）」是黑枪套（The Black Holster）。我仔细整了整上衣，服从了他。
我进屋时，所长和监督正在磋商。亚玻伦坐在一张书桌后，非常凶恶地打量我。他的下属来回摇晃，双手在背后时而交握时而松开，以一种近乎仁慈的表情看着我。黑枪套守着门口。
朝我凶猛地转过身来：「您的朋友很坏，非常坏，您知道吗（savez-vous）？」所长吼道。
我平静地回答：「是吗？我还不知道（Oui? Je ne le savait pas.）。」
「他是个坏蛋，一个罪犯，一个叛徒，文明的耻辱，」亚玻伦冲着我的脸咆哮。
「是吗？」我又说。
「您最好小心点！」所长喊道。「您知道您朋友出什么事了吗？」
「不知道（Sais pas），」我说。
「他进监狱了，他活该！」亚玻伦咆哮。「您明白这意味着什么吗？」
「也许吧，」我答道，恐怕有点无礼。
「您运气好，没跟他关在一起！您明白吗？」

===Original===

Monsieur Le Directeur thundered, “and next time pick your friends better, take more care, I tell you, or you’ll go where he is﻿—**to prison for the rest of the war**!”
“With my friend I should be well content in prison!” I said evenly, trying to keep looking through him and into the wall behind his black, big, spidery body.
“In God’s Name, what a fool!” the Directeur bellowed furiously﻿—and the Surveillant remarked pacifyingly: “He loves his comrade too much, that’s all.”﻿—“But his comrade is a traitor and a villain!” objected the Fiend, at the top of his harsh voice﻿—“*Comprenez-vous; votre ami est **un salop**!*” he snarled at me.
He seems afraid that I don’t get his idea, I said to myself. “I understand what you say,” I assured him.
“And you don’t believe it?” he screamed, showing his fangs and otherwise looking like an exceedingly dangerous maniac.
“*Je ne le crois pas, Monsieur.*”
“O God’s name!” he shouted. “What a fool, *quel idiot*, what a beastly fool!” And he did something through his froth-covered lips, something remotely suggesting laughter.
Hereupon the Surveillant again intervened. I was mistaken. It was lamentable. I could not be made to understand. Very true. But I had been sent for﻿—“Do you know, you have been decided to be a suspect?” Monsieur le Surveillant turned to me, “and now you may choose where you wish to be sent.” Apollyon was blowing and wheezing and muttering﻿ ﻿… clenching his huge pinkish hands.
I addressed the Surveillant, ignoring Apollyon. “I should like, if I may, to go to Oloron Sainte Marie.”
“What do you want to go there for?” the Directeur exploded threateningly.
I explained that I was by profession an artist, and had always wanted to view the Pyrenees. “The environment of Oloron would be most stimulating to an artist﻿—”
“Do you know it’s near Spain?” he snapped, looking straight at me.
I knew it was, and therefore replied with a carefully childish ignorance: “Spain? Indeed! Very interesting.”
“You want to escape from France, that’s it?” the Directeur snarled.
“Oh, I hardly should say that!” the Surveillant interposed soothingly; “he is an artist, and Oloron is a very pleasant place for an artist. A very nice place, I hardly think his choice of Oloron a cause for suspicion. I should think it a very natural desire on his part.”﻿—His superior subsided snarling.

===Chinese===

所长先生雷霆大吼，「还有，下回交朋友挑着点儿，多加小心，我告诉您，不然您就去他待的地方——战争的剩余时光都蹲监狱！」
「跟我朋友一起蹲监狱我才称心呢！」我不紧不慢地说，努力保持目光穿过他、射进他那黑色庞大蜘蛛身子后面的墙壁。
「以上帝之名，多蠢的傻瓜！」所长狂怒地大吼——监督打圆场说：「他太爱他的同志了，仅此而已。」——「可他的同志是叛徒、是恶棍！」魔王扯着他那副粗嗓子反对——「您明白吗；您的朋友是个下流胚（Comprenez-vous; votre ami est un salop!）!」他冲我咸牙。
他好像怕我不懂他的意思，我对自己说。「我听懂您说的话了，」我向他保证。
「而您不信？」他尖叫，露出獠牙，其余部分活像一个极其危险的精神病人。
「我不信，先生（Je ne le crois pas, Monsieur.）。」
「哦上帝之名！」他喊道。「多蠢的傻瓜，多白痴（quel idiot），多可恶的傻瓜！」然后他隔着覆满白沫的嘴唇做了个什么动作，勉强有点像笑。
于是监督再次介入。我错了。这很可悲。我没法被说服。非常对。但人家叫我来是有事的——「您知道吗，您已被定为嫌疑犯了？」监督先生转向我，「现在您可以挑选想去哪儿了。」亚玻伦喘着、噗着、咕哝着……攥紧他巨大的粉红大手。
我对监督说话，不理亚玻伦。「我想，如果可以的话，去奥洛龙圣玛丽。」
「您去那儿干什么？」所长威胁性地发作。
我解释说我是职业画家，一直想看看比利牛斯山。「奥洛龙的环境对艺术家会是最有启发性的——」
「您知道那儿靠近西班牙吗？」他直瞪着我抢白道。
我知道，于是以一种精心装出的孩子气无知回答：「西班牙？真的吗！很有意思。」
「您想逃出法国，是不是？」所长狺狺。
「哦，我可不这么认为！」监督息事宁人地插话；「他是艺术家，奥洛龙对艺术家是个非常宜人的地方。很好的地方，我不认为他选奥洛龙有什么可疑。我倒觉得这是一个很自然的愿望。」——他的上司狺狺着平息了。

===Original===

After a few more questions I signed some papers which lay on the desk, and was told by Apollyon to get out.
“When can I expect to leave?” I asked the Surveillant.
“Oh, it’s only a matter of days, of weeks perhaps,” he assured me benignantly.
“You’ll leave when it’s proper for you to leave!” Apollyon burst out. “Do you understand?”
“Yes, indeed. Thank you very much,” I replied with a bow, and exited. On the way to The Enormous Room the Black Holster said to me sharply:
“*Vous allez partir?*”
“*Oui.*”
He gave me such a look as would have turned a mahogany piano leg into a mound of smoking ashes, and slammed the key into the lock.
—Everyone gathered about me. “What news?”
“I have asked to go to Oloron as a suspect,” I answered.
“You should have taken my advice and asked to go to Cannes,” the fat Alsatian reproached me. He had indeed spent a great while advising me; but I trusted the little Machine-Fixer.
“*Parti?*” Jean le Nègre said with huge eyes, touching me gently.
“No, no. Later, perhaps; not now,” I assured him. And he patted my shoulder and smiled, “*Bon!*” And we smoked a cigarette in honour of the snow, of which Jean﻿—in contrast to the majority of *les hommes*﻿—highly and unutterably approved. “*C’est jolie!*” he would say, laughing wonderfully. And next morning he and I went on an exclusive promenade, I in my sabots, Jean in a new pair of slippers which he had received (after many requests) from the *bureau*. And we strode to and fro in the muddy *cour* admiring *la neige*, not speaking.
One day, after the snowfall, I received from Paris a complete set of Shakespeare in the Everyman edition. I had forgotten completely that B. and I﻿—after trying and failing to get William Blake﻿—had ordered and paid for the better-known William; the ordering and communicating in general being done with the collaboration of Monsieur Pet-airs. It was a curious and interesting feeling which I experienced upon first opening to *As You Like It*﻿ ﻿… the volumes had been carefully inspected, I learned, by the *sécrétaire*, in order to eliminate the possibility of their concealing something valuable or dangerous. And in this connection let me add that the *sécrétaire* or (if not he) his superiors, were a good judge of what is valuable﻿—if not what is dangerous.

===Chinese===

又问了几个问题之后，我在桌上的几份文件上签了字，亚玻伦叫我出去。
「我什么时候能走？」我问监督。
「哦，只是几天的事，也许几个星期，」他慈祥地向我保证。
「该您走的时候您就走！」亚玻伦爆发。「明白吗？」
「明白，明白。非常感谢，」我鞠躬答道，退了出去。回巨大的房间的路上，黑枪套尖刻地对我说：
「您要走了（Vous allez partir?）」
「是（Oui.）。」
他给了我那样一眼——足以把红木钢琴腿变成一堆冒烟的灰烬——随后把钥匙砰地砸进锁孔。
——人人围拢过来。「什么消息？」
「我已作为嫌疑犯请求去奥洛龙，」我回答。
「您真该听我的劝去戛纳，」胖阿尔萨斯人数落我。他确实花了老大工夫劝我；可我信小小的机器修理工。
「走了（Parti?）」让·勒内格尔眼睛瞪得老大，轻轻碰了碰我。
「不，不。以后吧，也许；现在不，」我向他保证。他拍拍我的肩，笑了，「好（Bon!）」我们抽了一支烟，向雪致敬——让与大多数男人们（les hommes）不同，对雪高度地、说不出口地赞成。「真好看（C'est jolie!）」他会说，笑得妙不可言。第二天早晨他和我在 cour 里进行了一场专场散步，我穿木鞋，让穿一双新拖鞋——他（多次申请之后）从办公处（bureau）领来的。我们在泥泞的 cour 里大步来回，欣赏着雪（la neige），不说话。
落雪后的一天，我收到巴黎寄来的一整套「人人丛书」版莎士比亚。我全忘了：B. 和我曾试着弄威廉·布莱克未果之后，订购并付了钱买那位更有名的威廉；订购和联络全靠佩特埃尔斯先生协作。头一次翻开《皆大欢喜》（As You Like It）时我体验到一种古怪而有趣的感觉……我后来得知，这些卷册曾被秘书仔细检查过，以排除它们藏匿贵重或危险物品的可能。就此让我补充一句：那位秘书，或者（若非他）他的上司们，对什么算贵重——若非什么算危险——是很有眼光的判断者。

===Original===

I know this because, whereas my family several times sent me socks in every case enclosing cigarettes, I received invariably the former sans the latter. Perhaps it is not fair to suspect the officials of La Ferté of this peculiarly mean theft; I should, possibly, doubt the honesty of that very same French censor whose intercepting of B.’s correspondence had motivated our removal from the *Section Sanitaire*. Heaven knows I wish (like the Three Wise Men) to give justice where justice is due.
Somehow or other, reading Shakespeare did not appeal to my disordered mind. I tried *Hamlet* and *Julius Caesar* once or twice, and gave it up, after telling a man who asked “Shah-kay-spare, who is Shah-kay-spare?” that Mr. S. was the Homer of the English-speaking peoples﻿—which remark, to my surprise, appeared to convey a very definite idea to the questioner and sent him away perfectly satisfied. Most of the timeless time I spent promenading in the rain and sleet with Jean le Nègre, or talking with Mexique, or exchanging big gifts of silence with The Zulu. For Oloron﻿—I did not believe in it, and I did not particularly care. If I went away, good; if I stayed, so long as Jean and The Zulu and Mexique were with me, good. “*M’en fous pas mal*,” pretty nearly summed up my philosophy.
At least the Surveillant let me alone on the *Soi-Même* topic. After my brief visit to Satan I wallowed in a perfect luxury of dirt. And no one objected. On the contrary everyone (realizing that the enjoyment of dirt may be made the basis of a fine art) beheld with something like admiration my more and more uncouth appearance. Moreover, by being dirtier than usual I was protesting in a (to me) very satisfactory way against all that was neat and tidy and bigoted and solemn and founded upon the anguish of my fine friends. And my fine friends, being my fine friends, understood. Simultaneously with my arrival at the summit of dirtiness﻿—by the calendar, as I guess, December the twenty-first﻿—came the Black Holster into The Enormous Room and with an excited and angry mien proclaimed loudly:
“*L’américain! Allez chez le Directeur. De suite.*”
I protested mildly that I was dirty.
“*N’importe. Allez avec moi*,” and down I went to the amazement of everyone and the great amazement of myself. “By Jove! wait till he sees me this time,” I remarked half-audibly.﻿ ﻿…
The Directeur said nothing when I entered.

===Chinese===

我知道这一点，因为我家人几次给我寄袜子、每回都附香烟，而我回回只收到前者没有后者。怀疑拉费尔泰的官员们干下这桩特别下贱的偷窃，也许不公平；我或许应该怀疑的，恰恰是那个截获 B. 的信件、致使我们被从卫生队（Section Sanitaire）除名的法国检查官。天知道我愿意（像三博士一样）把公道归给应得公道者。
不知怎么的，读莎士比亚提不起我这颗紊乱头脑的兴致。我试了一两回《哈姆雷特》和《裘力斯·凯撒》，就放弃了——此前有个问我「沙—凯—士—比—亚，谁是沙—凯—士—比—亚？」的人，我告诉他沙先生是说英语民族的荷马——这句话令我很吃惊地似乎给了提问者一个非常明确的概念，让他心满意足地走开了。在那无始无终的时光里，我大部分时间用来同让·勒内格尔在雨夹雪中放风，或者同墨西哥人聊天，或者同祖鲁人大宗交换沉默。至于奥洛龙——我不信它，也毫不关心。走，好；留，只要让和祖鲁人、墨西哥人跟我在一起，也好。「老子不在乎（M'en fous pas mal）」差不多概括了我的哲学。
至少监督不再拿「自我（Soi-Même）」话题烦我。撒旦那里短暂一游之后，我在一种完美的肮脏奢侈里打滚。无人反对。相反，人人（意识到肮脏的享受可以成为一门精美艺术的基础）带着近乎钦佩的神情目睹我越来越粗野的尊容。而且，通过比平时更脏，我在以一种（对我而言）非常满意的方式抗议着一切整洁的、体面的、偏执的、庄严的、建立在我好朋友们苦难之上的东西。而我的好朋友们，既是我得好朋友们，是懂得的。同我抵达肮脏之巅同时——按日历算，我猜是12月21日——黑枪套走进巨大的房间，一脸兴奋和怒气，大声宣布：
「美国人！去所长那儿。马上（L'américain! Allez chez le Directeur. De suite.）。」
我温和地抗议说我身上脏。
「不要紧。跟我走（N'importe. Allez avec moi）」于是我下去了，令所有人惊讶，也令我自己大讶。「天哪！等着瞧这回他看见我这模样，」我半出声地说。……
所长在我进门时一言不发。

===Original===

The Directeur extended a piece of paper, which I read.
The Directeur said, with an attempt at amiability: “*Alors, vous allez sortir.*”
I looked at him in eleven-tenths of amazement. I was standing in the *bureau* de Monsieur le Directeur du Camp de Triage de la Ferté Macé, Orne, France, and holding in my hand a slip of paper which said that if there was a man named Edward E. Cummings he should report immediately to the American Embassy, Paris, and I had just heard the words:
“Well, you are going to leave.”
Which words were pronounced in a voice so subdued, so constrained, so mild, so altogether ingratiating, that I could not imagine to whom it belonged. Surely not to the Fiend, to Apollyon, to the Prince of Hell, to Satan, to Monsieur le Directeur du Camp de Triage de la Ferté Macé﻿—
“Get ready. You will leave immediately.”
Then I noticed the Surveillant. Upon his face I saw an almost smile. He returned my gaze and remarked:
“*Uh-ah, uh-ah, Oui.*”
“That’s all,” the Directeur said. “You will call for your money at the *bureau* of the Gestionnaire before leaving.”
“Go and get ready,” the Fencer said, and I certainly saw a smile.﻿ ﻿…
“I? Am? Going? To? Paris?” somebody who certainly wasn’t myself remarked in a kind of whisper.
“*Parfaitement.*”﻿—Pettish. Apollyon. But how changed. Who the devil is myself? Where in Hell am I? What is Paris﻿—a place, a somewhere, a city, life; (to live: infinitive. Present first singular: I live. Thou livest). The Directeur. The Surveillant. La Ferté Macé, Orne, France. “Edward E. Cummings will report immediately.” Edward E. Cummings. The Surveillant. A piece of yellow paper. The Directeur. A necktie. Paris. Life. *Liberté. La liberté.* “*La Liberté!*” I almost shouted in agony.
“*Dépêchez-vous. Savez-vous, vous allez partir de suite. Cet après-midi. Pour Paris.*”
I turned, I turned so suddenly as almost to bowl over the Black Holster, Black Holster and all; I turned toward the door, I turned upon the Black Holster, I turned into Edward E. Cummings, I turned into what was dead and is now alive, I turned into a city, I turned into a dream﻿—
I am standing in The Enormous Room for the last time. I am saying goodbye. No, it is not I who am saying goodbye. It is in fact somebody else, possibly myself.

===Chinese===

所长递过来一张纸，我读了。
所长带着一次和蔼的尝试说：「那么，您要出去了（Alors, vous allez sortir.）。」
我带着十分之十一的惊愕看着他。我正站在法国奥恩省拉费尔泰马塞筛查营所长先生的办公室里，手里捏着一张纸条，上面写着：倘有一名爱德华·E·卡明斯（Edward E. Cummings），应立即向美国驻巴黎大使馆报到，而我刚听见这句话：
「那么，您要走了。」
这句话的嗓音如此低柔、如此拘谨、如此温和、如此彻底地讨好，以致我想不出它属于谁。断不属于魔王，不属于亚玻伦，不属于地狱亲王，不属于撒旦，不属于拉费尔泰马塞筛查营所长先生——
「去准备吧。您马上就走。」
这时我注意到了监督。他脸上我看到一个几乎可算微笑的东西。他回看我的目光，说：
「嗯啊，嗯啊，是（Uh-ah, uh-ah, Oui.）。」
「就这样，」所长说。「走之前到管理员办公处领您的钱。」
「去准备吧，」击剑手说，而我确实看见了一个微笑。……
「我？要？去？巴？黎？」某个肯定不是我的家伙用一种耳语说。
「当然（Parfaitement.）」——乖戾。亚玻伦。可多么判若两人。我究竟是谁？我究竟在什么鬼地方？巴黎是什么——一个地点、一个某处、一座城市、生活；（生活：不定式。现在时第一人称单数：我生活。你生活）。所长。监督。法国奥恩省拉费尔泰马塞。「爱德华·E·卡明斯应立即报到。」爱德华·E·卡明斯。监督。一张黄纸。所长。一条领带。巴黎。生活。自由。自由（Liberté. La liberté.）。「自由（La Liberté!）」我痛苦得几乎喊出声。
「快点（Dépêchez-vous.）。您知道吗，您马上就要走了。今天下午。去巴黎（Pour Paris.）。」
我转身，我转得那么猛，几乎把黑枪套撞个趔趄，连同黑枪套的全部；我转向门，我转向黑枪套，我转变成爱德华·E·卡明斯，我转变成那死而复活的东西，我转变成一座城市，我转变成一个梦——
我最后一次站在巨大的房间里。我在道别。不，道别的不是我。其实是别的什么人，可能是我自己。

===Original===

Perhaps myself has shaken hands with a little creature with a wizened arm, a little creature in whose eyes tears for some reason are; with a placid youth (Mexique?) who smiles and says shakily:
“Goodbye, Johnny; I no for‑get you,”
with a crazy old fellow who somehow or other has got inside B.’s tunic and is gesticulating and crying out and laughing; with a frank-eyed boy who claps me on the back and says:
“Goodbye and good luck t’you”
(is he The Young Skipper, by any chance?); with a lot of hungry wretched beautiful people﻿—I have given my bed to The Zulu, by Jove! and The Zulu is even now standing guard over it, and his friend The Young Pole has given me the address of “*mon ami*,” and there are tears in The Young Pole’s eyes, and I seem to be amazingly tall and altogether tearless﻿—and this is the nice Norwegian, who got drunk at Bordeaux and stole three (or four was it?) cans of sardines﻿ ﻿… and now I feel before me someone who also has tears in his eyes, someone who is in fact crying, someone whom I feel to be very strong and young as he hugs me quietly in his firm, alert arms, kissing me on both cheeks and on the lips.﻿ ﻿…
“Goo-bye, boy!”
—O goodbye, goodbye, I am going away, Jean; have a good time, laugh wonderfully when *la neige* comes.﻿ ﻿…
And I am standing somewhere with arms lifted up. “*Si vous avez une lettre, sais-tu, il faut dire.* For if I find a letter on you it will go hard with the man that gave it to you to take out.” Black. The Black Holster even. Does not examine my baggage. Wonder why? “*Allez!*” Jean’s letter to his *gonzesse* in Paris still safe in my little pocket under my belt. Ha, ha, by God, that’s a good one on you, you Black Holster, you Very Black Holster. That’s a good one. Glad I said goodbye to the cook. Why didn’t I give Monsieur Auguste’s little friend, the *cordonnier*, more than six francs for mending my shoes? He looked so injured. I am a fool, and I am going into the street, and I am going by myself with no *planton* into the little street of the little city of La Ferté Macé which is a little, a very little city in France, where once upon a time I used to catch water for an old man.﻿ ﻿…
I have already shaken hands with the cook, and with the *cordonnier* who has beautifully mended my shoes. I am saying goodbye to *les deux balayeurs*. I am shaking hands with the little (the very little) Machine-Fixer again.

===Chinese===

也许我自己已同一个手臂枯瘦的小家伙握过手，那小家伙的眼睛里不知为何有泪；同一个平和的青年（墨西哥人？）握过手，他微笑着、颤声说：
「再见，约翰尼；我不忘你（Goodbye, Johnny; I no for-get you）」
同一个疯老头握过手，他不知怎么钻进了 B. 的救护车短外套，正又比划又喊又笑；同一个目光坦率的男孩握过手，他拍着我的背说：
「再见，祝你好运（Goodbye and good luck t'you）」
（他会不会就是少年船长？）；同一大群饥饿、凄惶而美丽的人们——我把床送给祖鲁人了，天哪！祖鲁人此刻正守在床边，他的朋友小波兰人（The Young Pole）给了我「我的朋友（mon ami）」的地址，小波兰人的眼里有泪，而我似乎高得惊人、全然无泪——这是那位好心的挪威人，在波尔多喝醉偷了三罐（还是四罐来着？）沙丁鱼……现在我面前又是一个眼里含泪的人，一个确实正在哭的人，一个当他在那结实而警觉的臂弯里静静抱住我、亲吻我双颊和嘴唇时，让我感到非常强壮而年轻的人。……
「再——见，小子（Goo-bye, boy!）」
——哦再见，再见，我要走了，让；痛快玩吧，雪（la neige）来的时候笑个痛快。……
我站在某个地方，双臂举起。「要是您身上带信，知道吗，必须申报（Si vous avez une lettre, sais-tu, il faut dire.）。因为要是在您身上搜出一封信，托您把信捎出去的那人可要吃不了兜着走。」黑。黑枪套本人。不检查我的行李。不知为什么？「走（Allez!）」让写给他巴黎妞（gonzesse）的信还安然藏在我腰带下的小口袋里。哈，哈，老天作证，这一手整得好，你黑枪套，你这非常黑的枪套。这一手整得好。幸好我跟厨子道了别。我怎么没多给奥古斯特先生（Monsieur Auguste）的小朋友、修鞋匠（cordonnier），六法郎以外的什么？他看上去那么委屈。我是个傻瓜，而我要走上大街了，我要独自一人、不带 planton 地走进拉费尔泰马塞小城的小街，那是法国一座小小的、很小的城，从前我常在那里替一位老人打水。……
我已同厨子握过手，同把我的鞋修得漂漂亮亮的修鞋匠握过手。我正同两个清洁工（les deux balayeurs）道别。我再次同小小的（很小的）机器修理工握手。

===Original===

I have again given him a franc and I have given Garibaldi a franc. We had a drink a moment ago on me. The tavern is just opposite the gare, where there will soon be a train. I will get upon the soonness of the train and ride into the now of Paris. No, I must change at a station called Briouse did you say, Goodbye, *mes amis, et bonne chance*! They disappear, pulling and pushing a cart *les deux balayeurs﻿ ﻿… de mes couilles﻿ ﻿…* by Jove what a tin noise is coming, see the wooden engineer, he makes a funny gesture utterly composed (composed silently and entirely) of *merde*. *Merde! Merde.* A wee tiny absurd whistle coming from nowhere, from outside of me. Two men opposite. Jolt. A few houses, a fence, a wall, a bit of *neige* float foolishly by and through a window. These gentlemen in my compartment do not seem to know that La Misère exists. They are talking politics. Thinking that I don’t understand. By Jesus, that’s a good one. “Pardon me, gentlemen, but does one change at the next station for Paris?” Surprised. I thought so. “Yes, Monsieur, the next station.” By Hell I surprised somebody.﻿ ﻿…
Who are a million, a trillion, a nonillion young men? All are standing. I am standing. We are wedged in and on and over and under each other. Sardines. Knew a man once who was arrested for stealing sardines. I, sardine, look at three sardines, at three million sardines, at a carful of sardines. How did I get here? Oh yes of course. Briouse. Horrible name “Briouse.” Made a bluff at riding *deuxième classe* on a *troisième classe* ticket bought for me by *les deux balayeurs*. Gentleman in the compartment talked French with me till conductor appeared. “Tickets, gentlemen?” I extended mine dumbly. He gave me a look. “How? This is third class!” I looked intelligently ignorant. “*Il ne comprend pas français*,” says the gentleman. “Ah!” says the conductor, “tease ease eye-ee thoorde claz tea-keat. You air een tea say-coend claz. You weel go ean-too tea thoorde claz weal you yes pleace at once?” So I got stung after all. Third is more amusing certainly, though goddamn hot with these sardines, including myself of course. O yes of course. *Poilus en permission.* Very old some. Others mere kids. Once saw a *planton* who never saw a razor. Yet he was *réformé. C’est la guerre.* Several of us get off and stretch at a little tank-town-station.

===Chinese===

我又给了他一法郎，也给了加里波第一法郎。方才我们喝了一杯，我请的。小酒馆就在车站正对面，很快就有火车。我将登上那辆很快的火车，驶进现在的巴黎。不，我得在一个叫布里乌兹的站换车，你说的是吗，再见，我的朋友们，祝好运（mes amis, et bonne chance）！他们推着一辆小车消失了，两个清洁工…………
天哪，一阵多么铁皮的噪音来了，瞧那木头工程师，他做了个滑稽的手势，全然由 merde 构成（静静地、整个地构成）。Merde！Merde.（屎！屎。）一声细小荒谬的汽笛，不知从何处来，从我之外来。对面两个男人。一颠。几座房子、一道栅栏、一堵墙、一片雪傻乎乎地飘过、穿过车窗。我车厢里的这些先生们似乎不知道拉米泽尔的存在。他们在谈政治。以为我听不懂。老天作证，这招妙。「对不起，先生们，去巴黎是在下一站换车吗？」吃了一惊。我就知道。「是的，先生，下一站。」见鬼，我让人吃了一惊。……
那是一百万、一万亿、一千秭的年轻人是谁？全都站着。我也站着。我们彼此楔进、楔上、楔过、楔下。沙丁鱼。从前认识一个人，因偷沙丁鱼被捕。我，一条沙丁鱼，看着三条沙丁鱼，看着三百万条沙丁鱼，看着一车厢的沙丁鱼。我怎么到这儿来的？哦对，当然。布里乌兹。好可怕的名字「布里乌兹」。我用两个清洁工买的三等票冒充二等，虚晃了一枪。包厢里的先生用法语陪我聊到查票员出现。「车票，先生们？」我默默地递上我的。他看了我一眼。「怎么？这是三等票！」我露出聪明的茫然。「他不懂法语（Il ne comprend pas français）」先生说。「啊！」查票员说，「迪斯伊斯特阿三等替克特。幼阿因则二等。幼威尔够因图则三等，威尔幼耶斯普利斯阿特万斯？（这是三等车票。您在二等车厢。请您马上到三等去，好吗？）」于是我终究被将了一军。三等当然更好玩，虽然跟这些沙丁鱼挤在一起热得要命，当然包括我自己。哦对当然。休假的兵（Poilus en permission.）。有的很老。有的纯粹是孩子。从前见过一个从没见过剃刀的 planton。可他还是免役了（réformé）。这就是战争（C'est la guerre.）。我们几个人下车，在一个小小的水箱镇车站伸伸腿。

===Original===

Engine thumping up front somewhere in the darkness. Wait. They get their *bidons* filled. Wish I had a *bidon*, a *dis-donc bidon n’est-ce pas. Faut pas t’en faire*, who sang or said that?
**Pee**-p.﻿ ﻿…
We’re off.
I am almost asleep. Or myself. What’s the matter here? Sardines writhing about, cut it out, no room for that sort of thing. Jolt.
“Paris.”
Morning. Morning in Paris. I found my bed full of fleas this morning, and I couldn’t catch the fleas, though I tried hard because I was ashamed that anyone should find fleas in my bed which is at the Hotel des Saints Pères whither I went in a fiacre and the driver didn’t know where it was. Wonderful. This is the American embassy. I must look funny in my pelisse. Thank God for the breakfast I ate somewhere﻿ ﻿… good-looking girl, Parisienne, at the switchboard upstairs. “Go right in, sir.” A1 English by God. So this is the person to whom Edward E. Cummings is immediately to report.
“Is this Mr. Cummings?”
“Yes.” Rather a young man, very young in fact. Jove I must look queer.
“Sit down! We’ve been looking all over creation for you.”
“Yes?”
“Have some cigarettes?”
“Yes.”
By God he gives me a sac of Bull. Extravagant they are at the American Embassy. Can I roll one? I can. I do.
Conversation. Pleased to see me. Thought I was lost for good. Tried every means to locate me. Just discovered where I was. What was it like? No, really? You don’t mean it! Well I’ll be damned! Look here; this man B., what sort of a fellow is he? Well I’m interested to hear you say that. Look at this correspondence. It seemed to me that a fellow who could write like that wasn’t dangerous. Must be a little queer. Tell me, isn’t he a trifle foolish? That’s what I thought. Now I’d advise you to leave France as soon as you can. They’re picking up ambulance men left and right, men who’ve got no business to be in Paris. Do you want to leave by the next boat? I’d advise it. Good. Got money? If you haven’t we’ll pay your fare. Or half of it. Plenty, eh? Norton-Harjes, I see. Mind going second class? Good. Not much difference on this line. Now you can take these papers and go to.﻿ ﻿… No time to lose, as she sails tomorrow. That’s it. Grab a taxi, and hustle. When you’ve got those signatures bring them to me and I’ll fix you all up. Get your ticket first, here’s a letter to the manager of the Compagnie Générale. Then go through the police department.

===Chinese===

火车头在前面黑暗中的什么地方扑扑地喘。等。他们灌满了水壶（bidon）。但愿我也有个水壶，一个「喂，水壶不是吗（dis-donc bidon n'est-ce pas）」。「别发愁（Faut pas t'en faire）」，是谁唱过或说过这句？
噗——噗。……
我们开动了。
我差不多睡着了。或者我自己。这儿怎么回事？沙丁鱼们扭来扭去，省省吧，没地方搞那种名堂。一颠。
「巴黎。」
早晨。巴黎的早晨。今天早晨我发现我床上满是跳蚤，而我抓不到它们，虽然我很努力了，因为让人在我床上发现跳蚤我感到羞愧——这床在圣父旅馆（Hotel des Saints Pères），我是坐马车去的，车夫还不知道它在哪儿。妙极了。这就是美国大使馆。我穿着皮大衣一定很好笑。感谢上帝，我在什么地方吃的那顿早饭……漂亮姑娘，巴黎人，在楼上的电话交换台旁。「请进，先生。」天哪，一等一的英语。这就是爱德华·E·卡明斯要立即报到的那位人物了。
「是卡明斯先生吗？」
「是。」一个相当年轻的人，事实上非常年轻。天哪我看上去一定很怪。
「坐吧！我们找你找遍了全世界。」
「是吗？」
「来支烟？」
「好。」
天哪，他给了我一袋斗牛士（Bull）烟。美国大使馆就是阔气。我会卷烟吗？我会。我卷了。
交谈。见到我很高兴。以为我彻底丢了。千方百计找我的下落。刚刚才发现我在哪儿。那儿怎么样？不会吧，真的？你不是开玩笑吧！好家伙！听着；这位 B. 是个什么样的人？听你这么说我很感兴趣。瞧瞧这些信件。在我看来，能写出这种信的人不危险。准是有点怪。告诉我，他是不是有点傻？我也这么想。现在我劝你尽快离开法国。他们正在到处抓救护车司机，抓那些没理由待在巴黎的人。你想搭下一班船走吗？我劝你走。好。有钱吗？没有的话我们付你旅费。或者付一半。很宽裕，嗯？诺顿-哈耶斯的，我明白。介意坐二等舱吗？好。这条线差别不大。现在你拿着这些文件去……一刻也不能耽误，船明天就开。就这样。叫辆出租车，麻利点。签好字拿来给我，我把你一切办妥。先买票，这是给总公司经理的信。然后去警察部门。

===Original===

You can do it if you hurry. See you later. Make it quick, eh? Goodbye!
The streets. *Les rues de Paris.* I walked past Notre Dame. I bought tobacco. Jews are peddling things with American trademarks on them, because in a day or two it’s Christmas I suppose. Jesus it is cold. Dirty snow. Huddling people. *La guerre.* Always *la guerre*. And chill. Goes through these big mittens. Tomorrow I shall be on the ocean. Pretty neat the way that passport was put through. Rode all day in a taxi, two cylinders, running on one. Everywhere waiting lines. I stepped to the head and was attended to by the officials of the great and good French Government. Gad that’s a good one. A good one on *le gouvernement français*. Pretty good. *Les rues sont tristes.* Perhaps there’s no Christmas, perhaps the French Government has forbidden Christmas. Clerk at Norton-Harjes seemed astonished to see me. O God it is cold in Paris. Everyone looks hard under lamplight, because it’s winter I suppose. Everyone hurried. Everyone hard. Everyone cold. Everyone huddling. Everyone alive; alive: alive.
Shall I give this man five francs for dressing my hand? He said “anything you like, monsieur.” Ship’s doctor’s probably well-paid. Probably not. Better hurry before I put my lunch. Awe-inspiring stink, because it’s in the bow. Little member of the crew immersing his guess-what in a can of some liquid or other, groaning from time to time, staggers when the boat tilts. “*Merci bien, Monsieur!*” That was the proper thing. Now for the﻿—never can reach it﻿—here’s the *première classe* one﻿—any port in a storm.﻿ ﻿… Feel better now. Narrowly missed American officer but just managed to make it. Was it yesterday or day before saw the Vaterland, I mean the what deuce is it﻿—the biggest afloat in the world boat. Damned rough. Snow falling. Almost slid through the railing that time. Snow. The snow is falling into the sea; which quietly receives it: into which it utterly and peacefully disappears. Man with a college degree returning from Spain, not disagreeable sort, talks Spanish with that fat man who’s an Argentinian.﻿—Tinian?﻿—Tinish, perhaps. All the same. In other words Tin. Nobody at the table knows I speak English or am American. Hell, that’s a good one on nobody. That’s a pretty fat kind of a joke on nobody. Think I’m French.

===Chinese===

抓紧的话你办得完。回头见。快点，嗯？再见！
大街。巴黎的街道（Les rues de Paris.）。我走过圣母院。我买了烟。犹太人沿街叫卖带美国商标的东西，我猜因为过一两天就是圣诞了。老天，真冷。脏雪。缩着的人们。战争（La guerre.）。永远是战争。还有寒气。穿透这双大手套。明天我就在海上了。护照办得真利索。出租车里坐了一整天，两个汽缸，一个在转。到处都在排队。我径直排到队首，由伟大而善良的法国政府的官员们亲自接待。天哪这真妙。对法国政府妙得很的一招。相当妙。街道是忧伤的（Les rues sont tristes.）。也许没有圣诞了，也许法国政府取缔了圣诞。诺顿-哈耶斯的职员见到我似乎很吃惊。哦上帝，巴黎真冷。灯下人人面色严峻，我猜因为是冬天。人人匆匆。人人严峻。人人冷。人人缩着。人人活着；活着：活着。
要不要给这个给我包扎手的人五个法郎？他说「随便您给，先生」。船医薪水大概不低。也许不。趁我还没把午饭吃下去，快点。骇人的恶臭，因为在船头。一个小个子船员正把他那猜是什么的东西浸进一罐什么液体，不时呻吟，船一倾就踉跄。「多谢了，先生（Merci bien, Monsieur!）」这样说才得体。现在去那个——永远够不着——这是一等舱（première classe）的那个——风暴里不择港。……现在感觉好多了。险些撞见美国军官，总算躲开了。是昨天还是前天看见祖国号（Vaterland）来着，我是说那鬼东西叫什么——世界上最大的船。浪大得要命。下着雪。方才差点从栏杆滑出去。雪。雪正落进海里；海静静接住它：让它彻底而安详地消失。一个从西班牙回来的大学毕业生，不算讨厌的家伙，正同那个阿根廷胖子说西班牙语。——泰尼安？——泰尼什，也许。都一样。换句话说：锡。桌上没人知道我会说英语、是美国人。见鬼，这个玩笑开在了无人身上。开在无人身上的一个相当肥的玩笑。他们当我是法国人。

===Original===

Talk mostly with those three or four Frenchmen going on permission to somewhere via New York. One has an accordion. Like second class. Wait till you see the *gratte-ciel*, I tell ’em. They say “*Oui?*” and don’t believe. I’ll show them. America. The land of the flea and the home of the dag’﻿—short for dago of course. My spirits are constantly improving. Funny Christmas, second day out. Wonder if we’ll dock New Year’s Day. My God what a list to starboard. They say a waiter broke his arm when it happened, ballast shifted. Don’t believe it. Something wrong. I know I nearly fell downstairs.﻿ ﻿…
My God what an ugly island. Hope we don’t stay here long. All the red-bloods first-class much excited about land. Damned ugly, I think.
Hullo.
The tall, impossibly tall, incomparably tall, city shoulderingly upward into hard sunlight leaned a little through the octaves of its parallel edges, leaningly strode upward into firm hard snowy sunlight; the noises of America nearingly throbbed with smokes and hurrying dots which are men and which are women and which are things new and curious and hard and strange and vibrant and immense, lifting with a great ondulous stride firmly into immortal sunlight.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

主要同那三四个经纽约去某地休假的法国人聊天。有一个带手风琴。喜欢二等舱。等着瞧摩天楼（gratte-ciel）吧，我告诉他们。他们说「是吗（Oui?）」不信。我会给他们看。美国。跳蚤的国土、南欧佬（dag'）的家园——dag' 当然是 dago 的简称。我的情绪持续高涨。好玩的圣诞，出海第二天。不知我们会不会元旦那天进港。我的天，船向右舷倾斜得厉害。他们说出事那会儿一个服务员摔断了胳膊，压舱物移了位。不信。不对劲。我知道我差点摔下楼梯。……
我的天，多丑的岛。希望我们别在这儿久留。头等舱的全体热血之士为陆地兴奋不已。我觉得丑得要命。
喂。
那座高得、高得不可思议、高得无与伦比的城市，肩扛着向上，插进坚硬的阳光里，微微倾侧着穿过它平行棱边的各个八度，倾侧着大步向上走进坚实、坚硬、积雪的阳光；美国的噪音携着烟尘和匆匆的小点——它们是男人、是女人、是崭新而奇特、坚硬而陌生、震颤而巨大的万物——渐渐逼近着搏动，以一阵伟大的波动步伐，坚定地举进不朽的阳光。……
